# Write, attach, and send

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/compose/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/compose/)

> How to write and send messages in Bearly, attach files and photos, unlock password-protected PDFs, share your screen, call tools and files with / and @, queue follow-ups while Bearly works, stop or interrupt an answer, and what the PII Blocker redacts.

The composer at the bottom of every chat is where you type, attach files, name the exact tool or file you want, and line up your next messages while Bearly is still answering. It works the same in the web app, a phone browser, the desktop app, and the iOS app. The browser extension's sidebar is a separate chat without these tools and isn't covered here.

## The composer at a glance

- **+** opens the **Add** menu: **Attach files** and **Share your screen**.
- **Take photo**, a camera button, appears on phones and tablets.
- The sliders button opens this chat's controls: **Web**, **Style**, **Scholar**, **Reasoning**, **Memories**, and **History**. See [Tune a chat](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/settings.md).
- The model button on the right shows the current model's short name and chooses who answers. See [Choose and compare models](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/models.md).
- Voice and dictation controls sit next to it. See [Voice](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/voice.md).
- The send button. While Bearly is working it becomes **Queue message** when you have a draft, or **Stop** when the composer is empty.
- Under the editor (except in the iOS app), a strip shows the chat's project, its encryption lock, **Work locally** in the desktop app, connectors, and **Permissions**. See [Permissions](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/permissions.md).

## Write and send a message

Type in the composer and send. In an existing chat the empty composer reads "How can I help? Type / or @ for tools". On a new chat it shows a rotating prompt line instead.

**Use it when:** asking a question, giving Bearly a task, or continuing a conversation.
**You need:** Nothing extra. Every plan.



1. Press <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>L</kbd> (<kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>L</kbd> on Windows and Linux) or click anywhere on the composer — this puts the cursor in the composer from anywhere in the chat.
2. Type your message. Press <kbd>Shift</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd> for a new line — plain <kbd>Enter</kbd> sends.
3. Press <kbd>Enter</kbd> or select the round arrow send button — the message goes out and Bearly starts answering. On phones and tablets, <kbd>Enter</kbd> doesn't send; tap the send button.



To reuse something you sent earlier, press <kbd>↑</kbd> in an empty composer. Each press goes one message further back; <kbd>↓</kbd> moves forward, and going past either end clears the composer.

**What you get:** Your message in the thread and Bearly's answer below it. Every message gets its own controls; see [Work with answers](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/answers.md).
**Limits:** The composer holds up to 200,000 characters. Paste more than that and Bearly attaches the text as a file named `pasted.txt` instead of filling the editor. You can't send while a file is still uploading or being analyzed, or when there's no text and no file.
**Combines with:** [Keyboard shortcuts](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/shortcuts.md) to do all of this without the mouse.

## Attach files and photos

Attach documents, spreadsheets, images, audio, and video to a message, and Bearly works with them in that chat. What it can do with each kind of file is covered in [Documents](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/documents.md), [Data and code](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code.md), and [Images, video, and audio](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/images-video-audio.md).

**Use it when:** asking about a contract, analyzing a spreadsheet, photographing a receipt or a whiteboard, or comparing several files at once.
**You need:** File uploads allowed. If your organization turns off **File uploads**, attaching, drag and drop, pasting files, the camera, and screen sharing all disappear.



1. Select **+** → **Attach files** — your system file picker opens. You can pick several files at once, of any type.
2. Or drag files onto the chat — a drop zone appears while you drag.
3. Or paste files or screenshots into the composer — when the clipboard holds files and no text, each file is attached.
4. On a phone or tablet, tap **Take photo** — the camera opens directly. It's its own button in the toolbar, not an item in the **+** menu.



Each file shows its status while it gets ready: checking a PDF, password required, unlocking, uploading with progress, then analyzing. You can send once every file is ready.

To point at a file that's already in the chat or its project, type `@` and pick it under **Assets**. See [Reference tools, files, and skills](#reference-tools-files-and-skills).

### Unlock a password-protected PDF

Bearly detects a password-protected PDF when you attach it and asks for the password.



1. Attach the PDF — the file shows "Enter the PDF password. Bearly uses it once to unlock this file."
2. Type the password in the **PDF password** field. The eye button shows or hides what you typed.
3. Select **Unlock and add** — Bearly unlocks the file and adds it to your message.
4. If you see "That password didn't work.", type it again. Remove the file to cancel.



If unlocking fails for another reason, the file shows "Unable to unlock this PDF."

**What you get:** Files attached to your message and saved in the chat, so you can point at them again later with `@`.
**Limits:** 300 MB per file; larger files can't be attached. If a file shows "Unable to upload and process.", remove it and attach it again.
**Combines with:** [/ and @ references](#reference-tools-files-and-skills) to name a file and a tool in the same message; [Search chats and files](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/organize.md#search-chats-and-files) to find a file later.

## Share your screen

Share a screen or window with Bearly, and while sharing stays on, each message you send automatically includes a fresh still capture of it. Bearly sees the screen as it was when you pressed send, not a video.

**Use it when:** you're stuck in another app and want step-by-step help, asking about a chart or error on screen, or checking a form before you submit it.
**You need:** The desktop app, or a desktop web browser that supports screen sharing. Your organization must allow **Screen Share** and **File uploads**.



1. Select **+** → **Share your screen** — the desktop app opens its own picker ("Share your screen with your agent"); a web browser opens its standard screen picker.
2. In the desktop app, choose a window under **Windows** or a display under **Whole screen** — that's what Bearly will capture.
3. Ask your questions as usual — every message carries a new capture of what's on screen at that moment.
4. Stop with the **×** on the shared-screen chip in the composer, below your text (its tooltip reads "Stop sharing with your agent.") — later messages go out without captures.



**What you get:** Answers about what's actually on your screen, kept current with every message.
**Limits:** Captures are still images, one per message. Not offered when file uploads are off.
**Combines with:** [Browser and computer use](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/browser-and-computer.md) when you want Bearly to act on the screen, not only look at it.

## Reference tools, files, and skills

Type `/` at the start of a message, or `@` anywhere in it, to choose a tool, research feature, skill, prompt template, or file by name. Your choice becomes a colored chip in the message. When you send, Bearly turns each chip into a plain instruction for the AI: use this tool for this request, or this is exactly the file I mean. The rest of your words stay as you typed them. A chip removes the guesswork: the AI uses what you named instead of deciding on its own. The chip stays in the conversation, so later turns read your earlier request the same way.

**Use it when:** you know which tool you want (`/deep-research`, `/chart`, `/image`), you want the AI to use one specific file out of many, or you want several tools in one request.
**You need:** Each item appears only when its feature is available to you; some depend on your plan, your organization's settings, or the desktop app.



1. Type `/` as the very first character, or `@` after a space or punctuation — the picker opens. An `@` right after a letter, as in an email address, doesn't open it.
2. Keep typing to filter the list. Use <kbd>↑</kbd> <kbd>↓</kbd> to move, <kbd>Enter</kbd> or <kbd>Tab</kbd> to insert, <kbd>→</kbd> to open a browsable item such as a folder, <kbd>←</kbd> to go back, and <kbd>Esc</kbd> to close.
3. Or type the exact label followed by a space or punctuation, such as `@web-search` — it turns into a chip as soon as you type the space.
4. Write the rest of your request and send. <kbd>Backspace</kbd> removes a whole chip at once.



The picker groups items under these headings:

| Heading | What's there |
|---|---|
| **Assets** | Files in this chat and its project, listed by file name |
| **Skills** | Built-in **Visualize** (`/visualize`) and **Bearly AI Models**, skills you installed in the desktop app, and your Prompt Templates, listed by title |
| **Research** | `/recall`, `/deep-research`, `/scholar`, `/bestpick`, `/council`, `/web-search`, `/read-site`, `/image-search`, `/people-search`, `/maps`, `/weather`, `/seo`, `/search-x`, and more |
| **Tools** | `/youtube`, `/share` (prepare a shareable Page of the chat's key takeaways), `/python`, `/document`, `/chart`, `/image`, `/edit-image`, `/video`, `/transcribe`, `/say`, `/grammar`, `/email`, `/sms`, `/summarize`, `/computer`, tools from your connectors, and more |
| **Local files** | Files and folders in the working folder you chose in the desktop app |

Until you type a filter, the picker shows up to eight items per heading.

**Combine several references.** A message can start with one `/` command, and it can hold any number of `@` references anywhere in the text. Mix files and tools freely:

```text
@web-search today's average 30-year mortgage rate, then @chart it next to the monthly rates in the spreadsheet I attached
```

**Prompt templates.** Your saved Prompt Templates appear under **Skills**, by title. Choosing one opens its fill-in form instead of inserting text. Fill it in and select **Submit** (or press <kbd>Shift</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd>); **Cancel** closes it. Blank fields are sent as "N/A". The filled form stays in the chat, so you can change a value and **Submit** again to get a new version.

**What you get:** A request the AI reads exactly as you meant it, with the named tool, file, or skill used for that turn.
**Limits:** Only one `/` command per message, and it must come first. Installed skills and local files appear in the desktop app only.
**Combines with:** [Research](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/research.md), [Data and code](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code.md), and [Connectors and skills](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/connectors-and-skills.md), where each tool you can reference is explained; [Building blocks](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/building-blocks.md) for chains of tools.

## Send while Bearly is working

You don't have to wait for an answer to finish. Anything you send while Bearly is working waits in a short queue above the composer, labeled **Up next**, and goes out automatically, in order, as each answer finishes.

**Use it when:** you think of the next step while a long answer streams, or you want to stack a chain such as "now make a table", then "now email it to me".
**You need:** Nothing extra. Every plan, on every app platform.



1. While Bearly is answering, type your next message — the send button changes to **Queue message**.
2. Press <kbd>Enter</kbd> or select **Queue message** — the message waits under **Up next**. Hover the header to see "Queued messages send automatically, in order, when the current response finishes."
3. Manage a waiting message with its buttons: **Edit queued message** brings it back into the composer (clear the composer first), **Send now** stops the current answer and sends this one right away, and **Remove from queue** deletes it.
4. To pull back the newest waiting message, press <kbd>↑</kbd> in an empty composer.



The queue stops on its own instead of sending the next message when:

- the last answer ended in an error: "Queue paused — the last message failed."
- you reached a usage limit: "Queue paused — usage limit reached."
- you pressed **Stop** twice within 2 seconds: "Queue paused."

After an error or a usage limit, the queue starts again once a later answer finishes cleanly. A single **Stop** ends only the current answer, and the queue carries on. A queue you paused with a double Stop stays paused until you use **Send now** or clear the waiting messages. **Send now** also restarts a queue paused for any reason.

**What you get:** A chain of follow-ups that runs without you watching, and stops instead of piling up failures.
**Limits:** Up to 4 waiting messages. When the queue is full, the send button is disabled ("Queue full — send, edit, or remove a waiting message first"); press <kbd>Enter</kbd> anyway and the header briefly reads **Queue is full**. Waiting messages aren't saved with the chat. Editing an earlier message never queues; it stops the current answer and reruns.
**Combines with:** [Email and text](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/email-and-text.md) as the last step of a chain; [History modes](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/settings.md#history-modes) for long chains of tool work.

## Stop or interrupt an answer

Stop an answer that's going the wrong way, or replace it with new directions in one step.

**Use it when:** an answer misread the question, is running long, or needs a correction now rather than after it finishes.
**You need:** Nothing extra.



1. To stop, leave the composer empty and select the square **Stop** button — Bearly stops; whatever it already wrote stays in the chat.
2. To stop and redirect at once, type your new message and press <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd> (<kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd> on Windows and Linux) — the current answer stops and your message goes out right away, ahead of anything waiting in **Up next**.
3. On a phone or tablet, queue the message, then select **Send now** on it — same result.



**What you get:** Partial work kept, and the conversation moving in the direction you want.
**Limits:** The send button never shows an "Interrupt" label; interrupting is <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd> or **Send now**.

## PII Blocker

**PII Blocker** is an organization setting. When an admin turns it on, Bearly finds email addresses, phone numbers in 10-digit North American format, credit card numbers written as one run of digits, and US Social Security numbers in your message text and replaces each with a placeholder such as `[REDACTED: Email Address]` before the message is sent. A notice, **Personal Information Redacted**, names what it found: "Detected Email Address. Information has been automatically redacted."

**Use it when:** your organization wants a guard against people pasting personal or payment details into chats.
**You need:** A managed organization. An admin turns it on in **Organizations** → organization name → **Policies** → policy name → **Privacy & Security** → **PII Blocker**. It's off by default.

**What you get:** The message still sends, with the details replaced. Messages waiting in **Up next** show the redacted text.
**Limits:** It replaces at most five matches of each kind in a message, and card numbers broken up by spaces or dashes aren't recognized as cards, so check long pastes yourself.
**Combines with:** [Organizations](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/organizations.md) and [Privacy and security](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/privacy-and-security.md) for the rest of the admin and privacy controls.

## Combinations to try

**Research, then chart, then email, without waiting.** Send the first request, then queue the other two while it runs. Each queued message builds on the answer before it, and the queue pauses if a step fails. The email step needs email sending, which is on unless your organization turned it off.

```text
Find the last five years of US new-home sales by year and summarize the trend.
```

```text
@chart those numbers as a bar chart.
```

```text
@email me a short summary of this chat.
```

**Name a file and a tool in one message.** Type `@` and pick the spreadsheet under **Assets**; it appears as a chip with the file's name. Typing the file name yourself doesn't make a chip, so pick it from the list. Then add a tool chip and your request, so the AI uses that exact file with that exact tool:

```text
@chart monthly revenue, one line per region
```

**Walk through another app.** Turn on **Share your screen**, choose the app's window, then ask one step at a time. Each question gets a fresh capture, so "what do I click next" stays accurate as the screen changes.

```text
I'm on this settings page. What do I change to turn on two-step sign-in?
```

## Related

- [Choose and compare models](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/models.md)
- [Work with answers](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/answers.md)
- [Keyboard shortcuts](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/shortcuts.md)
- [Everyday recipes](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/everyday-recipes.md)

# Choose and compare models

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/models/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/models/)

> How to choose, compare, and switch AI models in Bearly, including the model picker, Bearly Fast, Pro, and Ultra, defaults by plan and usage, per-chat and new-chat defaults, plan-gated and hidden models, Zero Data Retention only, running out of credits, and comparing answers with Rerun and Council of Experts.

Bearly puts Claude, GPT, Gemini, Grok, and more in one app. You pick the model for each chat, set a default for new chats, and can rerun any message on a different model to compare answers side by side. The models on offer change often, so this page explains how choosing works rather than listing them. For the current lineup, open the model picker in the app.

## Open the model picker

The model button sits at the right of the composer and shows the current model's short name. Selecting it opens the picker.

**Use it when:** switching this chat to a faster, cheaper, or stronger model, or finding a model you used before.
**You need:** Nothing extra. Some models need a higher plan; see [Models that need a higher plan](#models-that-need-a-higher-plan).

You can also choose a model in three other places:

- **Settings** → **This Chat** → **Model**, for the open chat.
- **Settings** → **New Chats** → **Model**, the default for chats you start later. It adds a **Recommended** option.
- The **Rerun** menu under any message, to answer that one message with another model. See [Compare models on the same prompt](#compare-models-on-the-same-prompt).

## How the picker is laid out

The picker opens on a short list and expands on request:

- **Favorites** — models you marked with the heart. Hover a model to see its heart.
- **Recently Used** — your two most recent models that aren't favorites.
- **Featured** — a short list Bearly recommends.
- **Show all models** — expands to every available model, grouped by the company that makes it. **Show featured models** goes back. The picker always reopens on the featured view.

Each row shows the model's name, a one-line description, and a retention badge: **ZDR** (zero data retention) or **Standard**. Hover the badge for what it means for that model. Some rows carry a tag: **Featured**, **Recent**, or **Unavailable**. A double check mark shows the model the chat is using now.

Favorites and recent models are saved to your account, so they follow you to every device.

**What you get:** The chat switches to the model you pick, starting with your next message.
**Limits:** A model marked **Unavailable** can't be chosen; selecting it shows a notice with **Got it** and **Read the announcement**. Some models show a warning you confirm before switching.

## Bearly Fast, Bearly Pro, and Bearly Ultra

**Bearly Fast**, **Bearly Pro**, and **Bearly Ultra** are Bearly's own picks, each described in the picker as "Always kept up to date." Choose one when you'd rather not track which underlying model is best this month. Bearly Pro and Bearly Ultra are featured for everyone; Bearly Fast is featured on the Free plan.

**Use it when:** you want a strong choice without comparing models yourself. The picker describes Bearly Fast as a "Fast, well-priced frontier model", Bearly Pro as a "Smart daily-driver frontier model", and Bearly Ultra as a "Frontier model for the most demanding work."

Your default model depends on your plan and usage:

| Your account | Default model |
|---|---|
| Free plan | **Bearly Fast** |
| Any paid plan | **Bearly Pro**, with Medium reasoning |
| Paid plan at 95% or more of its usage | **Bearly Fast**, while usage stays at or above 95% |

The default applies when you haven't chosen a model yourself. A model you picked for a chat stays picked. An open chat on the default follows the switch to Bearly Fast as soon as you cross 95%. Your usage meter shows on the account menu button at the bottom of the sidebar; see [Plans and credits](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/plans-and-credits.md).

## This chat or every new chat

A model you pick in the composer applies to the open chat only, and it's saved with that chat. In a shared project chat, each person's model choice is their own, so you and a collaborator can each use a different model in the same chat.

**Use it when:** one chat needs a different model than your usual one, or you want every new chat to start on a model you prefer.
**You need:** Nothing extra.



1. For one chat, pick a model in the composer, or open **Settings** → **This Chat** → **Model** — only this chat changes.
2. For new chats, open **Settings** → **New Chats** → **Model** and choose a model, or **Recommended** to follow Bearly's default for your plan — chats you start from now on use it.



**What you get:** New Chats defaults are saved to your account and apply on every device.
**Limits:** New Chats defaults don't apply inside projects; a project's own settings do. An organization or project can set or lock the model; see [When models are hidden or locked](#when-models-are-hidden-or-locked) and [Tune a chat](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/settings.md#which-setting-wins).
**Combines with:** [Projects](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects.md) to give every chat in a project the same model.

## Models that need a higher plan

Some models need **Pro** or **Analyst**. They still appear in the picker. Choosing one closes the picker and opens **Settings** → **Billing** with a message that names the model and the plan it needs, ending "Upgrade to use this model and unlock the rest of its tier." The **Rerun** menu does the same.

**Use it when:** deciding whether a plan change is worth it for a specific model.
**You need:** To upgrade, the plan the message names. Personal plans run Free, Pro, Analyst, and Research; see [Plans and credits](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/plans-and-credits.md).

**What you get:** Your chat stays on its current model until you upgrade and choose again.

## When models are hidden or locked

Your settings or your organization can narrow the picker. A line at the top of the picker says why:

| Banner | Why models are hidden |
|---|---|
| "ZDR on · Some models hidden" | You turned on **Zero Data Retention only**. **Change** opens **Settings** → **Privacy & Security**. |
| "ZDR on until settings sync · Some models hidden" | Bearly keeps Zero Data Retention only in force while your settings load or reconnect. |
| "Managed account · Some models hidden" | Your organization allows only some models. |
| "ZDR on, managed account · Some models hidden" | Both at once. |

If a chat's saved model isn't allowed anymore, Bearly answers with the first available model in the picker instead. Your saved choice stays as it was, so the chat goes back to it if it becomes allowed again.

When an organization's allow-list names fewer than five models, every allowed model shows as featured. When an organization or project locks the model, the model button and the **Rerun** menu disappear, and **This Chat** no longer shows **Model**.

**Limits:** Only an organization admin can change which models members may use; see [Organizations](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/organizations.md).

## Zero Data Retention only

Turn on **Zero Data Retention only** and Bearly shows only zero-retention models, and automatic fallbacks stay on them too. This applies to chat models.

**Use it when:** you want your chats to run only on zero-retention models.
**You need:** Nothing extra. Any plan.



1. Open **Settings** → **Privacy & Security** — or select **Change** on the picker's "ZDR on" banner.
2. Turn on **Zero Data Retention only** — the picker narrows to zero-retention models and shows the "ZDR on · Some models hidden" banner.



**What you get:** The setting is saved to your account and applies on every device. While your settings load, or if they can't sync, Bearly treats Zero Data Retention only as on and the switch can't be changed until it reconnects.
**Limits:** Fewer models to choose from. A chat set to a model without zero retention answers with the first allowed model instead.
**Combines with:** [Privacy and security](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/privacy-and-security.md) for the other privacy controls.

## When you run out of credits

On a paid plan, using up your plan's credits doesn't end the conversation. A notice above the composer reads "You're out of credits. Chat continues with Bearly Fast or Bearly Pro. Features like transcription need credits." with **View billing**. If your organization's allowance for you is used up, it reads "You've used your team allowance. Chat continues with Bearly Fast until it resets." with **View usage**. While either notice shows, the picker offers only the models you can still use.

**Limits:** Paid tools still need credits. The Free plan has no fallback: when its allowance runs out, Bearly asks you to upgrade or wait for the reset. See [Plans and credits](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/plans-and-credits.md).

## Compare models on the same prompt

Rerun any message on a different model and keep every answer. Each rerun becomes a new version of that turn, and you flip between versions with the arrows under the message. The chat's own model doesn't change.

**Use it when:** checking whether a stronger model changes the answer, picking a model for a recurring task, or getting a second opinion on a hard question.
**You need:** A chat where the model isn't locked.



1. Under your message or Bearly's answer, open the **Rerun** menu (it shows the model's short name) — models are listed with featured ones first.
2. Pick a model — Bearly answers the same message again with that model, as a new version. Plan requirements and model warnings apply as in the picker.
3. Repeat with a third model if you like.
4. Use the version arrows ("‹ 2 / 3 ›") to compare — **Previous version of query** and **Next version of query** step through them. The version you leave selected is saved with the chat.



To rerun on the same model, select the circular arrow under your message (**Rerun with same model**).

**What you get:** Several answers to one question, one per model, kept in the chat.
**Combines with:** [Work with answers](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/answers.md#rerun-and-switch-versions) for the other version controls.

## Ask several models at once with Council of Experts

`/council` sends one question to several models in parallel and then combines their answers into one reply, with each model's answer available in its own tab. Use it when you want a synthesized answer instead of reading versions one by one.

**You need:** Pro or higher. Your organization must allow Council.

```text
/council Should a 10-person startup use a monthly or annual pricing model for its first product? Give the strongest case for each.
```

Details, model choice, and limits are on [Research](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/research.md).

## Combinations to try

**Three models, then a council.** Rerun a hard question on three models from the **Rerun** menu and read the versions. If they disagree, send the question to `/council` for one combined answer. Council's experts don't see the chat's earlier messages, so restate the question in full:

```text
/council Our software company gets 40% of its revenue from one customer. What are the three biggest risks, and what should we do about each this quarter? Note where you disagree.
```

**A zero-retention comparison.** Turn on **Zero Data Retention only** first. The **Rerun** menu then offers only zero-retention models, so every version you compare stays on them.

**One model per project.** Set a model in a project's settings so every chat in that project starts on it, while your **New Chats** default stays your choice everywhere else. See [Projects](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects.md).

## Related

- [Tune a chat: settings, style, reasoning, and memory](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/settings.md)
- [Plans and credits](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/plans-and-credits.md)
- [Research recipes](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/research-recipes.md)

canonical: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/answers/"

# Work with answers

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/answers/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/answers/)

> How to copy, edit, delete, rerun, and compare versions of messages in Bearly, read answers aloud or open them in Canvas, read Thinking steps and citations, what answers can display, what to do when an answer fails, and how to move around long chats with the timeline and chapters.

Every message in a chat has its own controls: copy it, edit what you asked, rerun it on another model, step through versions, or hear it read aloud. This page covers those controls, how answers are displayed, and how to find your way around a long conversation.

## Message controls

Controls sit under each message. On a computer they appear when you hover over the message. On phones, tablets, and windows 768 pixels wide or narrower, they're always visible. Chats you can only read, such as project chats shared with you as a reader, show no message controls.

| Control | Under your message | Under Bearly's answer | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Copy** | Yes | Yes | Copies the message with its formatting, so bold text, lists, and links survive a paste into a document or email. |
| **Edit Query** (pencil) | Yes | No | Loads your message and its attachments back into the composer to change and resend. |
| **Delete Query** | Yes | Yes | Removes that turn after you confirm. |
| **Rerun with same model** (circular arrow) | Yes | No | Asks the same model again, as a new version. |
| **Rerun** menu | Yes | Yes | Answers the same message with a model you pick, as a new version. |
| Version arrows | When versions exist | When versions exist | Step between versions of a turn: "‹ 2 / 3 ›". |
| **Query info** | Yes | Yes | Shows when the turn **Started**, when it **Completed**, and its **Duration**. |
| **Read aloud** | No | On answers made only of text | Plays the answer as speech. |
| **Edit in Canvas** | No | When Canvas is available | Opens the answer in the Canvas editor. |

There's no thumbs-up or thumbs-down on messages. To send feedback about Bearly, use **Give Feedback** in the account menu at the bottom of the sidebar.

## Edit a message

Change what you asked and get a new answer, without losing the old one.

**Use it when:** you left out a detail, want to try a different angle, or attached the wrong file.
**You need:** A chat you can edit.



1. Select **Edit Query** (the pencil) under your message — the message and its attachments load into the composer, and the strip under it shows **Editing message**.
2. Change the text or attachments.
3. Send — Bearly answers the edited message as a new version of that turn. If an answer is still running, it stops first. Edits never wait in the queue.
4. To back out, select **Cancel** in the **Editing message** strip, or press <kbd>Esc</kbd>.



**What you get:** A new version of the turn. The earlier version is still there behind the version arrows.

## Delete a message

**Delete Query** removes one turn. Bearly asks "Delete this query?" with "This action cannot be undone." Select **Delete Message** to confirm. Later messages stay in the chat and attach to the message before the deleted one.

**Limits:** Deleting can't be undone. To remove a whole chat, see [Organize, find, share, and export chats](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/organize.md#delete-chats).

## Rerun and switch versions

Each rerun and each edit adds a version to the same turn instead of a new message, so the conversation stays readable while you try alternatives.

**Use it when:** an answer missed the mark, you want to compare models on the same question, or you want a fresh attempt.
**You need:** A chat where the model isn't locked by your organization or project. A locked model hides the **Rerun** menu.



1. Select **Rerun with same model** under your message for another attempt from the same model, or open the **Rerun** menu, which shows the model's short name, and pick a different model — featured models are listed first.
2. Read the new version — it replaces the old one on screen, and the version arrows appear.
3. Select **Previous version of query** or **Next version of query** (the arrows around "2 / 3") to compare — the version you leave selected is saved with the chat.



**What you get:** Several answers to one turn, kept in place. Rerunning with another model doesn't change the chat's model.
**Limits:** Models that need a higher plan open the upgrade message instead of rerunning; see [Choose and compare models](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/models.md#models-that-need-a-higher-plan).
**Combines with:** [Council of Experts](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/models.md#ask-several-models-at-once-with-council-of-experts) when you want one combined answer from several models.

## Read aloud and Edit in Canvas

**Read aloud** plays an answer as speech. It appears only on answers made entirely of text; an answer that includes tool steps, a **Thinking** step, images, or files doesn't get it. Your organization must allow text-to-speech. Voice options and spoken conversations are on [Voice](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/voice.md).

**Edit in Canvas** opens an answer in the Canvas editor, where you can reshape it into a document. See [Pages and Canvas](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md).

## Thinking steps

When a model reasons before answering, Bearly shows that reasoning as a collapsible **Thinking** step above the answer. Open it to see how the model worked through the problem; leave it closed to read only the answer. If a model writes its reasoning into the answer between thinking tags, Bearly folds that into the same **Thinking** step. How much a model thinks depends on the model and on **Reasoning Effort**; see [Tune a chat](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/settings.md#reasoning-effort).

## How answers are displayed

Answers render as formatted text:

- Headings, bulleted and numbered lists, tables, block quotes, and links. Line breaks are kept as written.
- Code blocks with syntax highlighting and the language name. Hover a block and select **Copy** to copy just the code.
- Math as typeset formulas. Dollar amounts such as $30 stay plain text.
- Web and email links, which are clickable. Web links open in your browser.

For a chart or diagram, ask for one: `/chart` makes charts, and `/visualize` turns an idea, process, or dataset into a focused visual, such as an interactive Page, a chart, or a diagram. See [Data and code](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code.md) and [Pages and Canvas](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md).

## Citations

When an answer draws on sources, it cites them so you can check the claim:

- Web search answers carry numbered links to the pages they used.
- Scholar answers mark claims with small superscript citations. Hover one for the paper; select it to open the paper.
- Answers from your documents cite passages you can select to open the document near that page.
- Deep Research reports include numbered citations and a **Sources** list.

Details for each are on [Research](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/research.md) and [Documents](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/documents.md).

## When an answer fails

A failed answer shows its error, or "An error occurred. Please wait and try again." when there's no specific message. **Get help** next to it opens a support dialog. A message that can't be displayed shows "Unable to load message."



1. Read the message — plan and usage-limit messages come with a button to upgrade or to open billing.
2. Select **Rerun with same model** under your message, or pick another model from the **Rerun** menu — either one tries the turn again as a new version.
3. If it keeps failing, select **Get help**.



**Limits:** If messages are waiting in **Up next**, a failed answer pauses the queue until a later answer succeeds or you select **Send now**. See [Send while Bearly is working](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/compose.md#send-while-bearly-is-working).

## Move around a long chat

Long conversations get a map and shortcuts so you can get back to any point quickly.

**Use it when:** returning to an earlier prompt in a long working session, or reviewing what a chat covered.
**You need:** For the timeline, a window at least 1,280 pixels wide and a chat with five or more prompts. Not shown on phones.

- **Timeline:** A slim rail of tick marks along the right edge marks every prompt. Hover a tick for "Prompt 3 of 12" (for example), a preview of the prompt, and the shortcut hint. Select a tick to jump there.
- **Chapters:** About every five turns, Bearly labels the recent stretch of the conversation in the background. Labeled ticks are highlighted; hover one to see **Chapter** and its title. Chats you can only read don't get chapters.
- **Jump between prompts:** <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>⇧</kbd> <kbd>↑</kbd> and <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>⇧</kbd> <kbd>↓</kbd> move to the previous and next prompt (<kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>Shift</kbd> <kbd>↑</kbd> and <kbd>↓</kbd> on Windows and Linux).
- **Scroll to bottom:** When you've scrolled up, a button appears to take you back to the latest message.

**Combines with:** [Download as Markdown](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/organize.md#download-a-chat-as-markdown) to keep a full record of a long chat.

## Combinations to try

**Best of three, then publish.** Rerun a draft announcement on two other models, pick the version you like with the version arrows, then select **Edit in Canvas** to polish it, and type `/share` to turn the chat into a Page you review before publishing. See [Pages and Canvas](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md).

**Check a citation at the source.** After a web search answer, have Bearly read one of the cited pages in full and test the claim against it. `/read-site` reads a single page you name; it needs the chat's **Web** setting on **Auto**.

```text
@read-site https://www.example.com/annual-report and tell me whether it supports the revenue figure in your second paragraph. Quote the sentence.
```

**Fix the question, keep the history.** Instead of adding "I meant…" in a new message, select **Edit Query** on the original, correct it, and send. The chat stays short, and the earlier answer is still one arrow away.

## Related

- [Write, attach, and send](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/compose.md)
- [Choose and compare models](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/models.md)
- [Keyboard shortcuts](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/shortcuts.md)

---
title: "Organize, find, share, and export chats"
description: "How to start, rename, pin, archive, delete, select, and move chats into projects in Bearly, read the sidebar, use Needs attention, search chats and files, use the command menu, download a chat as Markdown, share a chat as a Page, ask Bearly to manage chats for you, and open What's New."
canonical: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/organize/"
