# Organize, find, share, and export chats

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/organize/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/organize/)

> How to start, rename, pin, archive, delete, select, and move chats into projects in Bearly, read the sidebar, use Needs attention, search chats and files, use the command menu, download a chat as Markdown, share a chat as a Page, ask Bearly to manage chats for you, and open What's New.

Chats live in the left sidebar, grouped by project, pins, and date. This page covers starting and naming chats, keeping the list tidy, finding anything you wrote or uploaded, exporting and sharing a chat, and having Bearly do the filing for you. Everything here works in the web app, phone browser, desktop app, and iOS app, except dragging chats into projects, which needs a mouse or trackpad.

## Start a new chat

**Use it when:** starting a new topic, or keeping unrelated work in separate chats so each one stays focused.
**You need:** Nothing extra.



1. Select **New Chat** in the sidebar — opens the new-chat screen, outside any project.
2. Or press <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>N</kbd> or <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>⇧</kbd> <kbd>N</kbd> (<kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>N</kbd> or <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>Shift</kbd> <kbd>N</kbd> on Windows and Linux; a web browser may keep these keys for its own windows), use the pen icon in the chat header (shown when the sidebar is closed), or choose **New** in the command menu — from a project chat, these start the new chat in the same project.
3. To pick a project on the new-chat screen, use the project chip under the composer ("Start this chat in a project") — search for a project, choose from **Recent** or **All projects**, create one with **New project**, or choose **No project**. The project is fixed once the chat starts.
4. Type your first message and send.



**What you get:** A new chat, listed in the sidebar once you send the first message. Until then the empty draft shows only on the device where you opened it.
**Limits:** Pressing the shortcut while you're already on the new-chat screen just puts the cursor in the composer and shows "You're already in a new chat".
**Combines with:** [Projects](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects.md) to give a group of chats shared files, instructions, and settings.

## Name and rename chats

Bearly titles each chat automatically from your first message, in the background. If titling fails, it tries again later, and it repairs a chat still named "New Chat" with a number when you open it.



1. Click the title in the chat header and type a new one — <kbd>Enter</kbd> saves, <kbd>Esc</kbd> puts back the old title.
2. Or open **More actions** (the **…** on the chat's sidebar row) → **Rename**, or choose **Rename** in the command menu.
3. To have Bearly write a fresh title from the conversation so far, open the command menu and choose **Generate New Name**.



**Limits:** An empty title isn't saved.

## Read the sidebar

The list is grouped, top to bottom:

- **Projects**, with their chats.
- **Pinned** chats.
- Everything else by last activity: **Today**, **This Week**, **This Month**, then each earlier month of this year, then each earlier year.

A chat's row can show:

| Indicator | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Accent bar on the left | Unread replies |
| Spinner ("Bearly is working") | An answer is running |
| Shield ("Waiting for approval" or "Shell approval needed") | Bearly is waiting for you to approve an action |
| Lock ("Approve this device to open this chat") | An end-to-end encrypted chat this device can't open yet |
| Archive icon | The chat is archived |

Pins and archives are saved to your account, so the sidebar looks the same on every device.

## Use the More actions menu

Each chat row has a **More actions** menu (**…**). It appears on hover, and is always visible on touch devices and for the chat you have open.

| Item | What it does |
|---|---|
| **Rename** | Edit the title in place. <kbd>Enter</kbd> or clicking away saves. |
| **Pin** / **Unpin** | Keep the chat at the top, under **Pinned**. |
| **Move to project...** | Move the chat into a project, or out of one. Shown when you have projects. |
| **Archive** / **Unarchive** | Take the chat out of the main list without deleting it. |
| **Mark unread** / **Mark read** | Flag a chat to come back to. Shown once the chat has activity. |
| **Delete** | Delete the chat after you confirm. Archived chats show "Delete · unarchive first" instead. |
| **Select** | Start choosing several chats at once. |

## Archive chats

Archiving keeps a chat but takes it out of your everyday list.



1. Choose **Archive** in the chat's **More actions** menu, or select several chats and choose **Archive** — they leave the main list.
2. To see them, select **Archives** (with its count) above the account menu at the bottom of the sidebar — archived chats are grouped by the date you archived them, each with its project.
3. With eight or more archived chats, use **Filter archives** to narrow the list.
4. Select the back arrow (**Back to all chats**) to return.



**Limits:** An archived chat can't be deleted until you unarchive it ("Unarchive this chat before deleting it."). Projects can be archived too, and a chat can't be moved into an archived project.

## Delete chats

Deleting a chat removes the conversation, its files, and its memory for good.

**Use it when:** you don't need the chat or anything in it again.



1. Choose **Delete** in the chat's **More actions** menu, or press <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>D</kbd> (<kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>D</kbd>) in the open chat, or choose **Delete** in the command menu.
2. Read the **Delete Chat** confirmation — it names the chat and warns that its files and memory will be lost and that this can't be undone.
3. Select **Delete**.



To delete the open chat and start a fresh one in one step, use **Delete & New** in the command menu, or press <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>⇧</kbd> <kbd>D</kbd> (<kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>Shift</kbd> <kbd>D</kbd>).


A deleted chat's messages, files, and memory are gone. Archive instead if you might need it later.


**Limits:** Unarchive a chat before deleting it.

## Select several chats



1. Open any chat's **More actions** menu and choose **Select** — checkboxes appear, and the header reads "Select chats", then the number selected.
2. Check chats, or use **Select all** — it selects only the chats visible in the current view.
3. Choose **Move** (shown when you have projects), **Archive** (**Unarchive** in Archives), or **Delete**.
4. Select **Cancel**, press <kbd>Esc</kbd>, or switch views to stop selecting.



**Limits:** Bulk **Delete** skips archived chats and says how many it skipped. If every selected chat is archived, it asks you to unarchive them first.

## Move chats into projects



1. Choose **Move to project...** in a chat's **More actions** menu, or select several chats and choose **Move** — the **Move to project** dialog opens.
2. Search with **Search projects...**, or pick from **Pinned**, **Recent**, or **All projects**. The chat's current project is marked **Current**. Arrow keys and <kbd>Enter</kbd> work too.
3. To take a chat out of its project, choose **Remove from project** — it goes back to the main chat list.



With a mouse or trackpad, you can also drag a chat onto a project in the sidebar, or drag a project chat onto the main list to remove it from the project. Dragging isn't available on touch devices.

**Combines with:** [Projects](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects.md) for project settings, files, and sharing.

## Needs attention

When other chats are waiting for an approval or have unread replies, a **Needs attention** chip with a count appears in the sidebar. Select it to replace the list with **Awaiting approval** and then **Unread** chats, newest first, including project chats with their project name. Select it again to show all chats.

**Use it when:** several chats are running tools at once and you want to see which ones need you.
**Limits:** The chat you have open and archived chats aren't listed. Approvals show only for chats that have been active since you opened Bearly.

## Search chats and files

Search finds messages, files you uploaded, and files Bearly created, across all your chats.

**Use it when:** you remember what was said or what a file contained, but not which chat it was in.
**You need:** Nothing extra. Search works whether or not **Memories** is on.



1. Select the magnifier at the top of the sidebar, or press <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>F</kbd> (<kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>F</kbd>) — the search box opens with "Search chats and files...".
2. Type what you're looking for — results update as you type. Search matches meaning, not only exact words, and ranks exact text and title matches higher.
3. Switch between the **Chats** and **Files** tabs. A chat result shows its title, the date of the matching message, the number of matches, and a preview. A file result is labeled **Uploaded**, **Generated**, or **Saved by Bearly**.
4. Select a result to open its chat. Press <kbd>Esc</kbd> to close.



**What you get:** Chats ranked by their best match, with up to 20 matches per chat. If nothing matches, Bearly says "No results found for" and your search.
**Limits:** Search covers project chats but not end-to-end encrypted chats.
**Combines with:** [Memories](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/settings.md#memories), which lets the AI itself search past chats while it answers.

## The command menu

Press <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>K</kbd> (<kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>K</kbd>) or select **…** in the chat header to open the command menu. It shows the chat's title, a **Search...** box, an **Actions** group, and **Switch Chats**, which lists every chat, including project chats. Use <kbd>↑</kbd> <kbd>↓</kbd> to move, <kbd>Enter</kbd> to run, and <kbd>Esc</kbd> to close.

| Action | What it does |
|---|---|
| **New** | Start a new chat (in the same project when you're in a project chat) |
| **Delete & New** | Delete this chat and start a new one, after you confirm |
| **Delete** | Delete this chat, after you confirm |
| **Rename** | Rename this chat |
| **Refresh Chat** | Reload this chat's data |
| **Generate New Name** | Retitle the chat from the conversation |
| **Download as Markdown** | Export the chat as a Markdown file |
| **New Routine** | Open Routines to schedule a prompt. Pro and higher, when your organization allows Routines. |
| **Show all...** → **Show Release Notes** | Open What's New |

The chat also refreshes itself when you come back to the window.

## Download a chat as Markdown

Export a chat as a readable Markdown file, with or without a record of the tools Bearly used.

**Use it when:** keeping a record of a decision, pasting a conversation into a document or ticket, or archiving a project's history outside Bearly.
**You need:** Nothing extra.



1. Open the command menu and choose **Download as Markdown** — the **Download Markdown** dialog opens: "Export this chat as a readable Markdown file."
2. Turn on **Include tool activity** if you want "compact records of tool calls, inputs, assets, and summarized results" — leave it off for just the conversation.
3. Select **Download** to save the file, or **Copy** to put the Markdown on your clipboard.



**What you get:** A file named after the chat's title, ending in `.md`. Each turn is headed "User" or "Bearly".
**Limits:** Markdown only; there's no PDF or HTML export of a whole chat.

## Share a chat as a Page

Sharing doesn't hand out your conversation. Bearly turns the substance of the chat into a Page, with artwork and a link preview, and you review it before anything is published.

**Use it when:** sending the outcome of a chat to someone outside Bearly, such as a summary, a plan, or a short guide.
**You need:** A chat you can edit. Your organization must allow sharing.



1. Select **Share** in the chat header — the **Share conversation** panel opens.
2. Select **Prepare share** — Bearly writes the Page. The panel notes "Your full conversation isn't shared. You review the Page before publishing."
3. When it's ready, the Page opens on its own, marked "Ready to share" — check it, then select **Publish** in its header to choose who can open it. If you closed it, select **Share** again and choose **Review and publish**, or **Create another** for a different take.



You can also type `/share` in the chat. To give a single file its own Page, use **Share file** on that file.

**What you get:** A Page you control, built from the conversation's substance rather than its transcript.
**Limits:** Preparing a Page uses your plan's usage. Not available in chats you can only read.
**Combines with:** [Pages and Canvas](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md) for editing and publishing Pages.

## Ask Bearly to manage your chats

You can ask in any chat for Bearly to find, read, create, rename, move, or delete chats for you. It asks before it looks through your chats or reads another one, and before it deletes anything.

**Use it when:** filing a batch of chats into a project, pulling context from an earlier chat into this one, or cleaning up old chats by topic.
**You need:** Your organization must allow **Chat management**. It's on unless an admin turned it off.

What Bearly can do from a chat:

- List your chats and your projects.
- Create a new chat, optionally in a project, without switching you to it.
- Read another chat, at three levels of detail: your messages only, the conversation, or the conversation with tool activity.
- Rename chats, and move them into or out of projects.
- Delete chats.

Before it lists your chats, Bearly asks for permission and explains: "Lists your chats and their titles so Bearly can find the right one." Before it reads another chat, it names that chat and says: "Reads another chat's full history into this chat. Allow only chats you want this chat to see." You can allow it for this response, for this session, or for this chat on this device. Before deleting, Bearly shows "Delete this chat?" or how many chats it will delete, with their titles. If you allow deleting for longer than one request, later deletions asked for in that chat go ahead within that scope without asking again. **Full access** never skips any of these questions.

```text
Find my chats about the Q3 budget, rename each one to start with "Q3 budget:", and move them all into the Finance project.
```

**Limits:** Up to 20 chats per delete, and a chat can't delete itself. Reading returns up to 100 turns at a time, and a very large chat comes back as a Markdown file. Regular chats can't see your end-to-end encrypted chats. Bearly can't send messages from one chat into another.
**Combines with:** [Memories](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/settings.md#memories) to find the right earlier chat by meaning; [Permissions](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/permissions.md) for how approvals work.

## What's New

When a new Bearly release is out, **What's New** opens on its own the next time you use a chat on that device. It lists **Highlights**, **Also new**, and **Fixes & improvements**, with links to related posts and docs. New accounts don't see it on their first visit.

To open it any time, press <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>K</kbd> and choose **Show all...** → **Show Release Notes**. The same notes are on the public [changelog](https://bearly.ai/docs/changelog.md).

## Combinations to try

**Find, summarize, and file.** In a project chat with **Memories** set to **Project Only**, ask Bearly to find an earlier decision and file the current chat. Memories finds the earlier chat; chat management reads it (after you allow it), then renames and moves this one.

```text
Find what we decided about pricing in this project, summarize it in five bullets, then rename this chat to "Pricing decision" and move it to the Finance project.
```

**Export the record, then clear the list.** When a long piece of work is done, choose **Download as Markdown** with **Include tool activity** on for a full record, then **Archive** the chat. It leaves your everyday list but stays in **Archives**.

**From template to published Page.** Run one of your Prompt Templates from the `/` menu, then type `/share` and review the Page before publishing it. See [Write, attach, and send](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/compose.md#reference-tools-files-and-skills).

## Related

- [Projects](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects.md)
- [Keyboard shortcuts](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/shortcuts.md)
- [Privacy and security](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/privacy-and-security.md)

---

---
title: "Tune a chat: settings, style, reasoning, and memory"
description: "Every Bearly chat setting explained, including This Chat versus New Chats, which setting wins when projects and organizations set their own, Response Style, Reasoning Effort, Temperature, Instructions, Language, Web Search, History modes, Memories, Personalization, and how Bearly tells the AI today's date."
canonical: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/settings/"
markdown: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/settings.md"
---

# Tune a chat: settings, style, reasoning, and memory

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/settings/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/settings/)

> Every Bearly chat setting explained, including This Chat versus New Chats, which setting wins when projects and organizations set their own, Response Style, Reasoning Effort, Temperature, Instructions, Language, Web Search, History modes, Memories, Personalization, and how Bearly tells the AI today's date.

Each chat has its own model, reply language, style, reasoning level, instructions, and memory settings. You can change them for the chat in front of you, set defaults for every chat you start later, and tell Bearly about yourself once so every chat can use it. This page covers every chat setting and how they combine with project and organization settings.

## Open chat settings

You can reach chat settings three ways:

- Press <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>,</kbd> (<kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>,</kbd> on Windows and Linux).
- Open the account menu at the bottom of the sidebar and choose **App Settings**.
- Open the sliders button in the composer and choose **Chat Settings**.

The **Settings** window lists chat settings under **Chats**: **This Chat**, **New Chats**, **Personalization**, **Voice**, and **Dictation**. Voice and dictation are on [Voice](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/voice.md). The window's header shows **Saving...**, **Saved**, or **Not saved** as changes save. On a narrow screen, the list of panes becomes a menu at the top.

The sliders button in the composer holds quick per-chat switches: **Web**, **Style**, **Scholar**, **Reasoning**, **Memories**, and **History**. **History** and **Scholar** live only there, not in the **Settings** window.

## This Chat and New Chats

**This Chat** changes only the open chat: "Settings for this conversation only. Changes here don't affect other chats." **New Chats** sets the starting point for chats you start later: "These settings apply to all new chats by default." Both have the same controls:

| Control | Options | New Chats starts at |
|---|---|---|
| **Model** | The models in the model picker. **New Chats** adds **Recommended**, which follows Bearly's default for your plan. | Recommended |
| **Language** | **Default**, Arabic, Bengali, English, English (UK & AU), French, German, Hindi, Indonesian, Italian, Japanese, Korean, Mandarin Chinese, Persian, Portuguese, Russian, Spanish, Swahili, Thai, Turkish, Urdu, Vietnamese | Default |
| **Response Style** | **Normal**, **Classic**, **No Style**, **Learning**, **Concise**, **Explanatory**, **Formal** | Normal |
| **Memories** | **Off**, **Auto** | Auto |
| **Web Search** | **Off**, **Auto** | Auto |
| **Reasoning Effort** | **Default**, **None**, **Low**, **Medium**, **High**, **Extra High** | Default |
| **Instructions** | Free text | Empty |
| **Temperature** | Presets **Factual**, **Default**, **Creative**, and a slider from **Focused** to **Creative** | 1.00 (Default) |

**This Chat** also has **Reset all to defaults**. It appears once this chat has its own value for any control, and puts the chat's model, language, instructions, temperature, and reasoning back to those defaults. Organization and project settings still apply afterward.

**New Chats** defaults are saved to your account, so they apply on every device.

**Use it when:** one chat needs something different (This Chat), or you keep making the same change in every new chat (New Chats).
**Limits:** **New Chats** doesn't apply inside projects. In a project chat the pane says "Default settings are not available in project chats. Use Project Settings to configure." A control your organization or project locks is hidden or can't be changed.
**Combines with:** [Projects](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects.md) for settings shared by every chat in a project; [Choose and compare models](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/models.md) for picking models.

## Which setting wins

A chat's settings are built up in layers. Each later layer overrides the ones before it:

1. Bearly's own defaults.
2. Your **New Chats** defaults. Skipped in project chats.
3. Your organization's chat defaults. Skipped in project chats.
4. Chat settings your organization applies to every chat, including project chats.
5. The project's shared settings.
6. Your personal settings for that project.
7. **This Chat**.

An organization or project can also lock a setting. A locked setting can't be changed in **This Chat**, and its control disappears from the chat or stays fixed. Settings that can be locked: model, instructions, language, temperature, reasoning effort, response style, memories, web search, always searching project documents, and personalization.

One exception: if your organization or project sets a reasoning level, it applies even when it isn't locked.

Projects add two settings of their own: **Always Search Documents**, which makes every message in the project search the project's files first, and **Disable Personalization**. See [Projects](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects.md) and [Organizations](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/organizations.md).

## Response Style

**Response Style** shapes how answers read: length, structure, and tone. Change it per chat from the sliders button (**Style**) or in **This Chat**, and set your usual style in **New Chats**.

| Style | What answers are like |
|---|---|
| **Normal** | Conversational and focused, like a knowledgeable colleague. Prose over bullet lists unless the content is a list, such as steps or options, and few headings. |
| **Classic** | Thorough on complex or open-ended questions and when you ask for length; concise on simple ones. |
| **No Style** | Bearly adds no style guidance at all; you get the model's own habits. |
| **Learning** | A patient teacher. A short direct answer first, then a step-by-step build-up from the basics, with analogies and examples, jargon defined, common misconceptions flagged, and a takeaway at the end of complex topics. |
| **Concise** | The answer first, in short bullets, without introductions or sign-offs. Code questions get the code with at most a one-line explanation. Yes-or-no questions start with yes or no. |
| **Explanatory** | In depth, from several angles: what, why, how, and when. Context, trade-offs, structured sections, examples, edge cases, and why one approach beats the alternatives. |
| **Formal** | Professional, precise, and neutral, in prose paragraphs, with no slang. Conclusions first, then the reasoning, with trade-offs and limitations stated rather than opinions. |

Every style except **Classic** and **No Style** also follows a few house rules: no filler openers, no repeating your question back, no needless sign-off or recap, matching how formally you write, using bold sparingly, and not hedging when the answer is clear.

On a phone-sized screen, 480 pixels wide or less, Bearly also asks the AI to keep answers brief and to prefer lists over tables. This applies with every style except **No Style**.

**Limits:** A chat's style is shared by everyone in it and follows the chat to your other devices. An organization or project can set or lock it.

## Reasoning Effort

**Reasoning Effort** sets how much the model thinks before it answers. Higher levels ask for more reasoning; how much that changes the answer depends on the model.

| Option | What it does |
|---|---|
| **Default** | Medium when the chat is on Bearly Pro as your plan's default model; otherwise the model's own default |
| **None**, **Low**, **Medium**, **High**, **Extra High** | Asks the model for that level of reasoning |

The model's reasoning shows above the answer as a collapsible **Thinking** step; see [Work with answers](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/answers.md#thinking-steps).

**Use it when:** a multi-step problem, math, or code needs more care (raise it), or you want faster replies to simple questions (lower it).
**Limits:** Your reasoning choice is your own, even in a shared chat. A reasoning level set by your organization or project overrides yours.

## Temperature

**Temperature** sets how varied the wording and ideas in an answer are. The presets are **Factual** (0.5), **Default** (1), and **Creative** (1.5). The slider runs from 0 to 2 in steps of 0.05, labeled **Focused** at the low end and **Creative** at the high end. Above 1.5, Bearly warns: "High temperatures may produce incoherent output."

**Use it when:** you want steadier, more literal answers for facts and data (lower), or more varied ideas for brainstorming and creative writing (higher).

## Instructions

**Instructions** is free text Bearly includes with every message in the chat: who the answers are for, what format you want, terms to use or avoid. In **This Chat** the box reads "Custom instructions for this chat..."; in **New Chats**, "Default instructions for new chats...". It saves when you click away.

```text
I'm preparing a board update. Use plain language, round numbers to one decimal place, and end every answer with the one decision I need to make.
```

**Limits:** Project instructions are added to every chat in that project too. An organization or project can lock instructions.

## Language

**Language** asks the AI to answer only in the language you pick, whatever language you write in. **Default** adds no language instruction. The setting covers the AI's replies; Bearly has no setting for the language of its own menus and buttons.

## Web Search

**Web Search** (**Web** in the sliders menu) decides whether the AI can search the web in this chat. **Auto** lets it search when a question needs current information, and answers cite their sources. **Off** keeps web search out of the chat; reading a page you link needs **Auto** too. `/deep-research` works either way, on plans that include it.

**Scholar**, in the sliders menu only, works the same way for research papers: **Auto** or **Off**. Typing `/scholar` uses it for one message even when it's off. See [Research](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/research.md).

## History modes

**History** in the sliders menu decides how the AI reads a long conversation. It's set per chat and follows the chat to your other devices.

| Mode | What the AI reads | Best for |
|---|---|---|
| **Chat (Dynamic)** (default) | The newest messages in full and shorter and shorter excerpts of older ones. Once a chat is very long, the oldest messages drop out of what the AI reads. | Ordinary conversations |
| **Agentic (Compaction)** | When the conversation gets long, Bearly writes a summary of the older part in the background and keeps the most recent part word for word. The chat shows **Conversation compacted so you can keep going**. If a summary fails, the full history is kept. | Long working sessions with many tool steps, such as research, data work, or coding |

In both modes, a single very long message or tool result is shortened when the AI rereads it.

**Use it when:** a long, tool-heavy chat starts losing track of what happened early on: switch it to **Agentic (Compaction)** so the early work survives as a summary instead of being trimmed away.

## Memories

**Memories** lets the AI search your past chats while it answers. After each turn, Bearly indexes that part of the chat. When Memories is on, the AI can look things up when you refer to earlier work or when it's missing context, and it gets short excerpts from each matching chat.

**Use it when:** "like we discussed last week", picking up a project after a break, or asking what you decided before.
**You need:** Your organization must allow **Memory Recall**. It's on unless an admin turned it off.

| Where | Options |
|---|---|
| Sliders button → **Memories**, outside a project | **Off**, **All Chats** (default) |
| Sliders button → **Memories**, in a project chat | **Off**, **Project Only** (default), **All Chats** |
| **This Chat** and **New Chats** → **Memories** | **Off**, **Auto** |

**All Chats** searches every chat you have. **Project Only** searches only that project's chats. To make the AI search past chats for one message, type `/recall`:

```text
/recall What did I decide about the venue for the offsite, and why?
```

**Limits:** Memories isn't available in end-to-end encrypted chats, and those chats never show up in results. Deleting a chat deletes its memory. An organization or project can set or lock the Memories setting.
**Combines with:** [Ask Bearly to manage your chats](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/organize.md#ask-bearly-to-manage-your-chats) to read the full chat a memory came from; [Search chats and files](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/organize.md#search-chats-and-files) when you'd rather search yourself.

## Personalization

**Personalization** tells the AI about you once, for every chat: "Tell the AI about yourself so it can tailor responses to your needs."



1. Open **Settings** → **Personalization**.
2. Turn on **Personalization** ("Include your profile context in your chats.") — the fields below unlock. It's off until you turn it on.
3. Fill in **Name** ("How should the AI address you?"), **Occupation** ("What do you do?"), and **About you** ("What should the AI know about you?") — only what you enter here is added to your chats.



**What you get:** With the switch on, Bearly adds what you wrote in these fields to every chat and asks the AI to address you the way you asked. The pane notes: "This is shared with AI models in your conversations." Your Personalization settings are saved to your account and apply on every device.

With the switch off, nothing from your profile is added to your chats.

**Limits:** A project with **Disable Personalization** on, or an organization setting, sends nothing from your profile, including your name.

## Date awareness

Bearly tells the AI today's date, so "tomorrow", "next Friday", and "last week" work out correctly. There's no setting for it. On the first message of a chat, and again whenever the date has changed or at least 12 hours have passed since the last message, Bearly gives the AI your local date, your time zone, and how long it's been since the previous message (for example, "about 3 days"). None of this is saved in the chat.

## Combinations to try

**Your profile everywhere, with one-off exceptions.** Turn on **Personalization**, then set **New Chats** → **Response Style** to **Concise** and **Language** to the language you work in. Every new chat starts that way. For a single chat that needs a different voice, add **Instructions** in **This Chat**; they apply to that chat only.

**Long agentic session.** Before starting a long chain of tool work, switch **History** to **Agentic (Compaction)** and raise **Reasoning** to **High**. Then queue the follow-up steps in **Up next** so they run in order. See [Send while Bearly is working](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/compose.md#send-while-bearly-is-working).

**Project memory only.** In a project, leave **Memories** on **Project Only** so recall stays inside the project's chats, and turn on **Always Search Documents** in the project's settings so every answer checks the project's files first. See [Projects](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects.md).

## Related

- [Settings reference](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/settings-reference.md)
- [Choose and compare models](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/models.md)
- [Privacy and security](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/privacy-and-security.md)

---

---
title: "Talk to Bearly: dictation and voice calls"
description: "How to speak to Bearly instead of typing, with push-to-talk dictation into the message box and live voice calls that can run actions, plus Read aloud, every Dictation and Voice setting, plan and platform requirements, and limits."
canonical: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/voice/"
markdown: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/voice.md"
---

# Talk to Bearly: dictation and voice calls

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/voice/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/voice/)

> How to speak to Bearly instead of typing, with push-to-talk dictation into the message box and live voice calls that can run actions, plus Read aloud, every Dictation and Voice setting, plan and platform requirements, and limits.

You can talk to Bearly in two ways. **Dictation** turns what you say into text in the message box, so you can check it before sending. A **voice call** is a live spoken conversation in which Bearly answers out loud and can run the chat's tools while you talk. **Read aloud** works the other way and plays an answer to you.

| You want | Use | Control |
| --- | --- | --- |
| To speak a message and review it before it's sent | [Dictation](#dictation) | Microphone icon in the top-right corner of the message box, or hold <kbd>Right Shift</kbd> |
| A back-and-forth conversation out loud, hands-free | [Voice call](#voice-calls) | **Start voice call** (sound-wave icon) next to Send |
| To hear an answer | [Read aloud](#read-aloud) | **Read aloud** under the answer |

## Dictation

Dictation is push-to-talk speech to text. Speak, and Bearly writes the words into the message box, optionally cleaning up punctuation and phrasing first.

**Use it when:** writing a long prompt faster than you can type, describing a problem while looking at something else, or capturing an idea mid-thought.

**You need:** any plan. Admin controls **Dictation** and **Cloud dictation** (under **Media & Creativity**). Works on the web app, phone browsers, the desktop app, and the iOS app.



1. Select the microphone icon in the top-right corner of the message box — the first time, it opens **Settings** → **Dictation**, because dictation starts off.
2. Turn on the switch at the top of the **Dictation** pane — the pane reads "Click the dictation button to start or stop. You can also hold Right Shift while you speak."
3. Back in the chat, select the microphone and speak — a rising tone plays, the icon becomes a filled square, and a strip at the top shows "Listening…" with your words as they come in.
4. Select it again to stop — a falling tone plays, and the text fills the message box.



To dictate without clicking, hold <kbd>Right Shift</kbd> while you speak and let go when you're done. A brief tap doesn't start it, and pressing any other key cancels. The shortcut works while the Bearly window is focused; there's no system-wide shortcut.

While dictation works, the strip shows its progress: "Preparing transcription…", "Opening microphone…", "Connecting to transcription…", "Listening…", "Finishing transcript…", and, with cleanup on, "Cleaning up with" the model's name.

**What you get:** your words in the message box, ready to edit and send, or sent straight away if you chose **Send immediately**.

**Limits:** a recording stops on its own after 10 minutes. If something goes wrong you'll see "Microphone access failed. Check your browser or system permission.", "Transcription is unavailable. Try again.", or "Dictation could not finish. Try again."

### Dictation settings

Open **Settings** → **Dictation** (in the **Chats** section). Settings sync across your devices.

| Setting | What it does | Default |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Switch at the top of the pane | Turns dictation on | Off |
| **Keyboard shortcut** ("Hold while Bearly is focused.") | The push-to-talk keys. Select the shortcut, then at "Press your shortcut" hold the keys you want (1 to 6 keys) and let go. The switch beside it turns push-to-talk off | <kbd>Right Shift</kbd> |
| **After dictation** | **Fill the composer** puts the text in the message box. **Send immediately** sends it as a normal message, with all the chat's tools | **Fill the composer** |
| **Transcript cleanup** ("Fix punctuation and phrasing with a fast, low-reasoning model.") | Tidies the transcript before it reaches the message box. **Model** picks the cleanup model from those your plan and organization allow. **Instructions** ("Applied after transcription. The original audio is not sent.") tells it how | Off |
| **Vocabulary** ("Names, acronyms, and technical terms.") | Terms the cleanup model should spell correctly. Used only when cleanup is on | Empty |
| **Replacements** ("One exact spoken → written pair per line.") | Swaps exact words you say for what you want written, matched as whole words and ignoring case, before and after cleanup | Empty |

Write each replacement on its own line with an arrow; `=>` works too:

```text
bearly → Bearly
q four → Q4
acme corp => ACME Corporation
```

If cleanup fails, Bearly uses the raw transcript without a message, so you never lose what you said.

The pane also says: "Live audio is sent through Bearly for transcription." If your organization has turned off **Cloud dictation**, the pane says "Your team has disabled audio transcription." and the microphone shows "Dictation is disabled by your team". With **Dictation** off, there's no microphone icon and no **Dictation** pane.

## Voice calls

A voice call is a live conversation: you talk, Bearly answers out loud, and you can interrupt it at any point. During the call Bearly can use the same tools as the chat, such as web search, images, Run Python, Pages, and connectors, and it asks for approval the same way.

**Use it when:** thinking a problem through out loud, practicing a pitch or interview, getting step-by-step help while your hands are busy, or asking Bearly to look something up and build something without typing.

**You need:** any plan; calls use your plan allowance. Admin control: **Voice** (under **Media & Creativity**). A microphone and a secure connection. Works in the web app, phone browsers, the desktop app, and the iOS app; not in Firefox ("Voice is temporarily unavailable in Firefox. Use Chrome or the desktop app.").



1. Select **Start voice call** — the sound-wave icon just left of Send. Allow microphone access if asked.
2. Talk — the message box becomes a call bar that shows "Connecting", "Listening", "Thinking", or "Speaking", a timer, and the model's name.
3. Interrupt whenever you like — start talking and Bearly stops to listen.
4. Use **Mute microphone** and **Unmute microphone** as needed, and pick a different microphone from the call bar; Bearly remembers your choice on that device.
5. Select **End call** when you're done.



**What you get:** a call card in the chat with the status, length, and model, and **Show transcript** with "You:" and "Bearly:" lines. Later typed messages in the chat can see that transcript. The card has no audio playback.

### What Bearly can do during a call

- **Run actions.** It works through the chat's tools one at a time. Each spoken turn can take up to 12 action rounds; past that you'll see "Voice hit its action limit for this turn."
- **Ask for approval.** The call bar shows "Approval needed". Some voice models accept spoken answers: "yes", "no", "don't ask me again", or "stop". Others need you to select the on-screen approval card. Connector sign-in always happens on screen.
- **Use the chat so far.** A call starts with the chat's recent turns as background, so you can pick up a typed conversation by voice.

### Choose the voice model

Open **Settings** → **Voice** (in the **Chats** section). It reads "Voice calls use this model to listen, answer, and run actions. Calls use your plan allowance."

- On paid plans, pick a model under **Model** ("Takes effect on your next call.").
- On the Free plan, calls use a fixed model and the list shows "Choosing a voice model needs a paid plan." See [Plans and credits](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/plans-and-credits.md).
- Each model speaks in its own voice; there's no separate voice picker.
- If a call switches to a backup model, the call bar adds "· fallback" after the model name.

**Zero Data Retention only** applies to chat models; it doesn't filter the voice models.

**Limits:**

- A call ends after 30 seconds of silence, including while you're muted.
- If the connection stops making progress, the call ends with "Voice connection stalled."
- Each call has a usage limit. When it's reached, the call bar shows "Voice limit reached" and the card says "This voice session reached its limit. Start a fresh one anytime."
- On iOS the screen stays awake during a call, and the call doesn't continue in the background.
- If the button is unavailable, its tooltip says why: "Voice isn't available in this app.", "Voice needs a secure connection.", or "No microphone is available." Older desktop app versions without microphone support show no button; update the app.
- Voice calls don't run in [Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md).

## Read aloud

Under any answer that's all text, select **Read aloud** to hear it. It uses a standard voice unless you turn on **Use for Read Aloud** in Voice Studio, which switches Read aloud to the voice, engine, and delivery you picked there.

**You need:** any plan. Admin control: **Text to speech**.

For downloadable recordings, voice presets, and comparing voices, see [Text to speech](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/images-video-audio.md#text-to-speech).

## Try this

### Dictate a complex task and send it hands-free

You speak a full request and Bearly starts working without you touching the keyboard again.

**Why it works:** with **After dictation** set to **Send immediately**, a finished dictation is sent as a normal message, so it can use every tool the chat offers, including Pages and Run Python.

**You need:** any plan. Admin controls **Dictation** and **Cloud dictation**.



1. In **Settings** → **Dictation**, set **After dictation** to **Send immediately**, and turn on **Transcript cleanup** for cleaner text.
2. In a chat, hold <kbd>Right Shift</kbd> and speak the request, then let go.

   ```text
   Build a Page that tracks my weekly running miles, with a line chart, a weekly goal I can change, and a streak counter.
   ```

3. Watch the answer arrive — the Page opens in the chat like any other.



**Swap in:** **Fill the composer** when you want to review before sending; Vocabulary entries for product names you use often.

**If it stalls:** if a name comes out wrong every time, add a line under **Replacements**, such as `bearly → Bearly`.

### Ask a voice call to look something up and build a tool

You get a working Page from a spoken conversation.

**Why it works:** a call has the chat's tools, so web search and Page building can both run during the conversation, one action at a time.

**You need:** any plan. Admin controls **Voice** and **Web**.



1. Select **Start voice call**.
2. Say what you need.

   ```text
   Look up today's euro to dollar exchange rate and make me a currency converter Page that uses it.
   ```

3. Approve any action you're asked about — by voice where the model accepts it, or on screen.
4. End the call and open the Page in the chat.



**Swap in:** "Make a packing list for a three-day trip and email it to me"; "Find three recent articles on this and summarize them."

**If it stalls:** if you hear "Voice hit its action limit for this turn.", ask for the next step in a new sentence, or finish by typing in the chat, which can see the call's transcript.

### Proofread your writing by ear

You hear your own draft read back, which makes clumsy sentences easier to catch.

**Why it works:** Voice Studio's **Proofread by ear** preset picks a neutral, even delivery that reads exactly as written and pauses at punctuation, and **Use my message** fills the script with what you wrote.

**You need:** any plan. Admin control **Text to speech**.



1. Paste your draft and send it with `/say`.

   ```text
   /say Paste your draft here.
   ```

2. In Voice Studio, pick **Proofread by ear** under "What are you making?", then **Generate**.
3. Listen with the text highlighted, then fix what sounded off.



**Swap in:** [Fix Grammar](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/documents.md#fix-grammar) for line-by-line suggestions; **Read aloud** on Bearly's revised version.

**If it stalls:** for a long document, record one section at a time.

## Related

- [Write, attach, and send](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/compose.md)
- [Make images, video, and audio](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/images-video-audio.md)
- [Record and use meetings](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/meetings.md)
- [Settings reference](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/settings-reference.md)

title: "Keyboard shortcuts"
description: "Every Bearly keyboard shortcut for macOS and for Windows and Linux, covering sending, new lines, interrupting, the / and @ picker, the command menu, search, new and deleted chats, jumping between prompts, Settings, dialogs, dictation, and the desktop app's global shortcut."
canonical: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/shortcuts/"
markdown: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/shortcuts.md"
---

# Keyboard shortcuts

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/shortcuts/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/shortcuts/)

> Every Bearly keyboard shortcut for macOS and for Windows and Linux, covering sending, new lines, interrupting, the / and @ picker, the command menu, search, new and deleted chats, jumping between prompts, Settings, dialogs, dictation, and the desktop app's global shortcut.

These shortcuts work in the web app on a computer and in the desktop app. On macOS, ⌘ is the Command key; on Windows and Linux, the same shortcuts use Ctrl. On phones and tablets, <kbd>Enter</kbd> doesn't send a message: tap the send button.

## In the composer

| Action | macOS | Windows and Linux |
|---|---|---|
| Send | <kbd>Enter</kbd> | <kbd>Enter</kbd> |
| New line | <kbd>Shift</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd> | <kbd>Shift</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd> |
| Send now, stopping the current answer and skipping the queue | <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd> | <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd> |
| Bring back earlier messages, or the newest queued one (empty composer) | <kbd>↑</kbd> and <kbd>↓</kbd> | <kbd>↑</kbd> and <kbd>↓</kbd> |
| Open the tool, file, and skill picker | <kbd>/</kbd> at the start of a message, <kbd>@</kbd> anywhere | <kbd>/</kbd> at the start of a message, <kbd>@</kbd> anywhere |
| Remove a whole chip | <kbd>Backspace</kbd> | <kbd>Backspace</kbd> |
| Cancel editing a message | <kbd>Esc</kbd> | <kbd>Esc</kbd> |

While Bearly is still answering, <kbd>Enter</kbd> queues your draft instead of sending it right away. See [Send while Bearly is working](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/compose.md#send-while-bearly-is-working).

## In the / and @ picker

| Action | Keys |
|---|---|
| Move through the list | <kbd>↑</kbd> <kbd>↓</kbd> |
| Insert the highlighted item | <kbd>Enter</kbd> or <kbd>Tab</kbd> |
| Open a browsable item, such as a folder | <kbd>→</kbd> |
| Go back | <kbd>←</kbd> |
| Close the picker | <kbd>Esc</kbd> |

Typing an item's exact label followed by a space or punctuation, such as `@web-search`, turns it into a chip as soon as you type the space, without choosing from the list. See [Reference tools, files, and skills](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/compose.md#reference-tools-files-and-skills).

## Anywhere in a chat

| Action | macOS | Windows and Linux |
|---|---|---|
| Put the cursor in the composer | <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>L</kbd> | <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>L</kbd> |
| Open or close the command menu | <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>K</kbd> | <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>K</kbd> |
| Search chats and files | <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>F</kbd> | <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>F</kbd> |
| New chat, in the current chat's project if it has one | <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>N</kbd> or <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>⇧</kbd> <kbd>N</kbd> | <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>N</kbd> or <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>Shift</kbd> <kbd>N</kbd> |
| Delete this chat, after you confirm | <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>D</kbd> | <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>D</kbd> |
| Delete this chat and start a new one, after you confirm | <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>⇧</kbd> <kbd>D</kbd> | <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>Shift</kbd> <kbd>D</kbd> |
| Jump to the previous prompt | <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>⇧</kbd> <kbd>↑</kbd> | <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>Shift</kbd> <kbd>↑</kbd> |
| Jump to the next prompt | <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>⇧</kbd> <kbd>↓</kbd> | <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>Shift</kbd> <kbd>↓</kbd> |
| Open Settings | <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>,</kbd> | <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>,</kbd> |
| Dictate while you hold the key, with Bearly focused (push to talk) | Right <kbd>Shift</kbd> | Right <kbd>Shift</kbd> |

Pressing the new-chat shortcut while you're already on a new chat puts the cursor in the composer and shows "You're already in a new chat". Right Shift is the default dictation key; change it in **Settings** → **Dictation** → **Keyboard shortcut**. See [Voice](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/voice.md).

Web browsers keep some key combinations for themselves. If a shortcut does something in your browser instead of in Bearly, use the matching button or the command menu, or use the desktop app.

## In menus and dialogs

| Action | Keys |
|---|---|
| Move, run, and close in the command menu | <kbd>↑</kbd> <kbd>↓</kbd>, <kbd>Enter</kbd>, <kbd>Esc</kbd> |
| Save or undo a title you're editing in the chat header | <kbd>Enter</kbd> to save, <kbd>Esc</kbd> to put the old title back |
| Submit a Prompt Template form | <kbd>Shift</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd> |
| Confirm in a confirmation dialog | <kbd>Shift</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd> |
| Close search or a side panel, or stop selecting chats | <kbd>Esc</kbd> |
| Move and choose in the **Move to project** dialog | <kbd>↑</kbd> <kbd>↓</kbd>, <kbd>Enter</kbd> |

## In the desktop app

| Action | macOS | Windows and Linux |
|---|---|---|
| Bring Bearly to the front from any app (default, changeable) | <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>⇧</kbd> <kbd>P</kbd> | <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>Shift</kbd> <kbd>P</kbd> |
| Reload the window | <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>R</kbd> | <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>R</kbd> |
| Force reload the window | <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>⇧</kbd> <kbd>R</kbd> | <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>Shift</kbd> <kbd>R</kbd> |

The global shortcut works system-wide, even when Bearly isn't the active app. To change it:



1. Open **Settings** → **Desktop** → **Keyboard shortcut** ("Global hotkey to summon Bearly.").
2. Select **Set** or **Change** — Bearly shows "Press keys..." and records the next combination you press.
3. Press the new combination, then select **Save**, or **Cancel** to keep the current one.
4. To turn the global shortcut off, select **Disable**.



With **Auto-hide** on in the same pane, Bearly hides when it loses focus, and the global shortcut brings it back.

## Related

- [Write, attach, and send](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/compose.md)
- [Work with answers](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/answers.md)
- [Organize, find, share, and export chats](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/organize.md)
- [Get Bearly](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/get-bearly.md)
