# Work with files and documents

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/documents/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/documents/)

> How Bearly opens, previews, searches, cites, summarizes, edits, converts, and proofreads files in a chat or project, including supported file types, size limits, password-protected PDFs, Always Search Documents, the Document editor, and what changes in end-to-end encrypted chats.

Attach a file and Bearly can show it, read it, search it, quote it with page citations, summarize it, or hand back a new edited copy. This page covers every file and document tool in a chat: adding files, what each file type looks like, finding files again, the document research tools, the Document editor, and Fix Grammar.

## Add files to a chat

You can attach files in four ways. All of them need uploads to be allowed.

- Select **+** → **Attach files** under **Add**, and pick one or more files of any type.
- Drag files onto the chat. A drop zone appears while you drag.
- Paste files or screenshots into the message box. Pasting more than 200,000 characters of text attaches it as `pasted.txt` instead.
- On a phone or tablet, tap **Take photo** in the composer toolbar to open the camera.

**You need:** any plan. Admin control: **File uploads** (under **Privacy & Security**). When it's off, attaching, dropping, pasting files, and the camera are all hidden.

**Limits:** 300 MB per file; a larger file isn't attached. While a file is being prepared, its chip shows a spinner (hover it for "Checking PDF.", "Uploading" with progress, or "Analyzing."), and you can't send until it finishes. If a chip shows a warning icon with "Unable to upload and process.", remove it with its **×** and attach the file again; you can't send while it's there.

For everything else about the message box, see [Write, attach, and send](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/compose.md).

## Password-protected PDFs

Bearly detects a locked PDF when you attach it and asks for the password before adding it.

**Use it when:** you have a bank statement, contract, or report that opens only with a password.

**You need:** the same as attaching any file.



1. Attach the PDF — the upload chip shows a lock, and the message "Enter the PDF password. Bearly uses it once to unlock this file." appears.
2. Type the password in **PDF password** — a show/hide toggle lets you check what you typed.
3. Select **Unlock and add** — Bearly unlocks the file and adds the unlocked copy to the chat.



**What you get:** an ordinary PDF in the chat that Bearly can read, search, and cite.

**Limits:** a wrong password shows "That password didn't work." and you can try again. If the file can't be unlocked at all you'll see "Unable to unlock this PDF." Cancelling removes the file from the message.

## What Bearly can open and read

Every file appears as a card in the chat. Select **Open** on a card to see it full size in the side panel.

| File type | In the chat | In the side panel | What Bearly does with it |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PDF | PDF preview | Full PDF viewer with page controls | Reads, searches, and cites it by page |
| Word, Excel, PowerPoint, OpenDocument, `.pages`, `.numbers`, `.key`, `.epub` | Converted to PDF for the preview | PDF view; **Download** offers **PDF** or **Original** | Reads and searches the text; the Document editor edits Word, Excel, and PowerPoint files |
| CSV, TSV, and pipe-separated (`.csv`, `.tsv`, `.tab`, `.psv`) | Spreadsheet grid showing rows × columns, with **Copy table** and **Open** | Editable grid | Searches it as text; Run Python can analyze it; Charts can draw from it |
| Markdown | Rendered, with **Copy** and **Open** | Rendered | Reads and searches it |
| Images | Image | Full-size image | Looks at it with View Media; Touch Up can edit it |
| Audio | Compact player | Full player | Transcribe turns speech into text |
| Video | Video player | Video player | View Media watches it; Transcribe turns speech into text |
| HTML | Live [Page](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md) preview | The live Page | Reads it |
| JSON, plain text (`.txt`), logs | Text preview | Text | Reads it |
| Code (about 60 file types, such as `.py`, `.ts`, `.go`, `.sql`, `.yaml`, `.xml`, `.jsonl`, Dockerfile, and Makefile) | Syntax-highlighted preview | Syntax-highlighted | Reads it |
| Calendar (`.ics`) and anything else | Name card | Panel with **Download** | Can pass it to Run Python |

Word covers `.doc`, `.docx`, `.docm`, `.dot`, `.dotx`, and `.dotm`. Excel covers `.xls`, `.xlsx`, `.xlsm`, `.xlt`, `.xltx`, and `.xltm`. PowerPoint covers `.ppt`, `.pptx`, `.pptm`, `.pot`, `.potx`, `.potm`, and `.pps`. OpenDocument covers `.odt`, `.ods`, `.odp`, and `.odg`.

Bearly pulls the text out of documents up to 200 MB so the document tools can search and read them. Images, audio, and video skip this step: use [Transcribe](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/images-video-audio.md#transcribe) for what was said and [View Media](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/images-video-audio.md#view-media) for what's on screen.

## Find files again with Browse files

When a chat has files, its header shows **Browse files** (a Files icon with a count). It opens a list of the chat's files, newest first, with filters:

- **All** — every file.
- **Project** — files in the chat's project (shown only in a project chat).
- **Uploaded** — files you attached.
- **Generated** — files Bearly made, such as Pages, Python outputs, and images.

On iOS, a PDF's main action in the list is **Open PDF**. You can also ask in chat with `/files` (**Browse Files**) and Bearly lists them.

To find a file in any chat, press <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>F</kbd> (<kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>F</kbd> on Windows and Linux), type in **Search chats and files...**, and switch to the **Files** tab. Results are labelled **Uploaded**, **Generated**, or **Saved by Bearly**. End-to-end encrypted chats aren't searched. See [Organize, find, and share](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/organize.md).

## Point Bearly at one file with @

When a chat has several files, name the one you mean. Type `@` and part of the file name, then pick it under **Assets**. Every chat and project file is listed; a generated file appears once, at its latest version.

You can combine a file with a tool in one message. Only one `/` command counts and it must come first, but you can add several `@` references anywhere:

```text
Compare @q3-board-deck.pptx with @q2-board-deck.pptx and list every number that changed.
```

These file tools are in the `/` menu under **Tools**:

| Type | Menu shows | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `/files` | Browse Files | Lists the chat's files |
| `/read-file` | Read Asset | Reads a file |
| `/create-file` | Create Asset | Writes a new file: a Page (HTML), Markdown, plain text, CSV, or JSON |
| `/edit-file` | Edit Asset | Edits a file Bearly wrote; each edit saves a new version |
| `/rename-file` | Rename File | Renames a file without making a new version |
| `/analyze-media` | View Media | Looks at an image or video |

## Preview, edit a table, and download

Open any file in the side panel to get its controls: a version dropdown for files Bearly has edited, **Copy** where the content can be copied, **Share file** (when sharing is allowed and you can edit the chat), **Download**, and **Close**. For documents previewed as PDF, **Download** offers **PDF** or **Original**; choose **Original** for the editable file.

CSV files are editable in the side panel:



1. Open the CSV — the bar above the grid shows rows × columns, the separator when it isn't a comma, and how many parse issues Bearly found.
2. Edit cells, or add **+ Row** and **+ Column** — **Revert** throws away your edits.
3. Select **Save** — for a file Bearly generated this saves a new version ("Saved as v2"). For a file you uploaded, the button is **Save copy** and saves a new generated copy instead ("Saved as a new copy"). In a read-only chat the grid is marked **Read only** and can't be edited.



If you try to leave with unsaved edits, Bearly asks "Discard your unsaved CSV changes?"

## Research your documents: Search Docs, Read Doc, Scan Doc

Bearly has four document tools. They appear once the chat or its project has at least one file, and Bearly picks one on its own when you ask about your files. Type the label to force one.

| Type | Menu shows | What it does | Good for |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `/search-docs` | Search Docs | Searches every attached and project document by meaning and returns cited passages | "Where do our contracts mention auto-renewal?" |
| `/read-document` | Read Doc | Reads a document page by page | Reading a section in full before quoting it |
| `/find-in-document` | Scan Doc | Finds exact words or a pattern in one document, with page, line, and surrounding text | Every mention of a name, clause number, or figure |
| `/summarize` | Summarize Doc | Writes a structured summary of a long document | An overview before you dig in |

**Use it when:** comparing terms across several agreements, pulling every figure from a report with its page, checking a claim against the source, or answering questions from a folder of policies.

**You need:** any plan. The document tools have no admin control of their own; attaching files needs **File uploads**, and adding files to a project needs **Project documents and data**.



1. Attach the documents, or open a chat in a project that has them — wait until each file finishes "Analyzing."
2. Ask a focused question and say what form the answer should take — Bearly searches the documents and can read further around what it finds before answering.

   ```text
   Compare the renewal, termination, and price-increase terms in these three agreements. Make a table with one row per agreement and cite the page for every cell.
   ```

3. Select a citation in the answer — the document opens near the cited page so you can check the surrounding text.



**What you get:** an answer with clickable citations, and sometimes a strip of matching document sources above it.

**Limits:** one search returns up to 10 sources. Spreadsheets and CSV files are searched as text, not as tables; for sums, filters, or pivots, use [Run Python](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code.md#run-python). Search Docs doesn't cover audio, video, or images. Scan Doc returns 10 matches at a time and pages through the rest.

**Combines with:** [Run Python](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code.md) for numbers in a spreadsheet, the [Document editor](#document-editor) to turn findings into a Word or PDF file, and [Research](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/research.md) to check your documents against public sources.

### Summarize Doc and Deep Summary

Summarize Doc condenses one long document into a structured overview. When Bearly shows the summary itself, it's headed **Deep Summary**. Bearly reaches for it when you ask for a summary or when a document is very long.



1. Attach the document and type `/summarize` — the menu shows **Summarize Doc**.
2. Name the document and the reader — say what matters most.

   ```text
   /summarize Summarize @annual-report.pdf for a board member. Keep the central argument, the major risks, and every explicit recommendation.
   ```

3. Read the summary, then ask follow-ups in the same chat — expand a section, or check a claim against the source.



**Limits:** for a very long document, Deep Summary reads up to 30 evenly spaced sections rather than every word. When completeness matters, ask Bearly to read the specific section with Read Doc or find every mention with Scan Doc.

## Always Search Documents (projects)

In a project, you can make Bearly search the project's documents before every answer, so replies stay grounded in your material without asking each time.

**Use it when:** a project is a knowledge base, such as a policy library, a client file, or course readings, and every answer should cite it.

**You need:** a project with documents, and permission to edit its settings. An organization policy can also set and lock this switch.



1. In the sidebar, expand the project and select **Settings**.
2. In **Project Chat Settings** (**Shared Project Settings** in a shared project), turn the card on with its switch if it shows **Disabled**.
3. Turn on **Always Search Documents** ("Always search project documents before responding") — the lock toggle beside it decides whether people can change it in their own chats.



**What you get:** Bearly runs Search Docs before answering each message in the project's chats.

**Limits:** it has no effect in end-to-end encrypted chats, which don't support Search Docs. See [Projects](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects.md) for the other project settings.

## Document editor

The Document editor creates new office documents and edits or converts the ones you attach. It works by running Python in Bearly's cloud sandbox with helpers for each format, so a Run Python step appears while it works.

**Use it when:** you need a real file back, such as a Word draft with tracked changes, a filled PDF form, a slide deck from your notes, or a spreadsheet with working formulas.

**You need:** any plan; each run counts as usage. Admin control: **Run Python** (under **Code & Automation**). Turning it off removes both `/python` and `/document`.

What it can do, by format:

- **PDF:** extract text and tables, merge, split, rotate, create, read scanned pages with text recognition, add a watermark or password, pull out images, and fill forms, including forms with no fillable fields.
- **Word:** create documents, edit with tracked changes and comments, accept all changes, and convert `.doc` to `.docx`.
- **PowerPoint:** create decks, or build from a template you attach.
- **Excel:** build workbooks that use formulas rather than pasted values, recalculate them, and format financial models.
- **Checking:** render pages or slides as images to check the layout before handing the file back.



1. Type `/document` — the menu shows **Document editor** under **Tools**.
2. Attach every source file — skip this when starting from scratch.
3. Describe the finished file: format, reader, what changes, and what must stay the same.

   ```text
   /document Revise the attached contract for plain language and return a new DOCX with tracked changes. Keep the clause numbering, defined terms, and existing comments.
   ```

4. Watch the **Run Python** step — select it to see **Input** and **Result**, or **Error** if the run failed.
5. Open the new file and select **Download** — for Word, Excel, and PowerPoint files the preview is a PDF, so choose **Original** to get the editable file.



**What you get:** one or more new files in the chat. Your original stays unchanged, so you can compare them or ask for another pass.

**Limits:** up to five new files per run. Runs are short and resource-limited, so split a big batch into a few named requests. Check the downloaded file in the app where it will be used before you send or sign it; conversion can shift layout, fonts, or page breaks.

**Combines with:** the [document research tools](#research-your-documents-search-docs-read-doc-scan-doc) to gather facts first, [Share file](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md#share-and-publish-a-page) to give the result a Page and link, and [Run Python](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code.md) for analysis that doesn't need a polished file.

## Fix Grammar

Fix Grammar marks problems in a passage and lets you accept each fix one at a time. It flags grammar, spelling, punctuation, style, vocabulary, and clarity, and colors each mark by severity.

**Use it when:** proofreading an email, report, cover letter, or announcement where you want to keep your own wording and approve each change.

**You need:** any plan. No admin control.



1. Type `/grammar` — the menu shows **Fix Grammar** under **Tools**. You can also just ask Bearly to proofread.
2. Paste the passage and send — Bearly returns it in an editor card with each issue highlighted.
3. Select a highlighted passage — a card shows the kind of issue, an explanation, and the before and after text. Select **Accept** to apply that one change.
4. Use **Accept All** for every remaining suggestion, **Check Again** for a fresh pass on the current text, and **Copy corrected text** to copy the result.



**What you get:** a summary line that counts what's left, then "All issues resolved" or "No issues found".

**Limits:** not available in Routines. Suggestions you don't accept are never applied, so names, quotes, and house style stay as written unless you choose otherwise.

## End-to-end encrypted chats

Files work in end-to-end encrypted chats, with these differences:

- **Search Docs** isn't available, and **Always Search Documents** has no effect. Read Doc, Scan Doc, and Summarize Doc still work.
- Files from encrypted chats don't appear in **Search chats and files...**.
- Run Python and the Document editor still work. The files you give a run are sent to the cloud sandbox to process, up to 50 MB in total per run.

> **Note — Encryption**
> Chats and files are encrypted at rest, each with its own key. Turn on end-to-end encryption and your new chats are encrypted on your devices. Bearly stores and syncs only the locked copies. Each message and any file a tool needs still goes to the model or service doing the work. See [Privacy and security](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/privacy-and-security.md).


## Try this

Each recipe runs in one chat.

### Compare a stack of contracts, then return a redlined copy

You get a cited comparison table and a Word file with your proposed changes as tracked edits.

**Why it works:** Search Docs finds the matching clauses across every attached agreement and the answer cites pages, so the comparison stays checkable. The table stays in the chat, so the Document editor can apply its conclusions to one agreement as tracked changes.

**You need:** any plan. Admin controls **File uploads** and **Run Python**. A chat that isn't end-to-end encrypted, for Search Docs.



1. Attach the agreements and ask for the comparison — check a few citations against the pages they open.

   ```text
   Compare the limitation-of-liability, indemnity, and termination clauses across these agreements. One row per agreement, cite the page for every cell, and flag anything unusual.
   ```

2. Turn the findings into a redline — the Document editor writes a new DOCX and leaves the original untouched.

   ```text
   /document Using the table above, edit @vendor-msa.docx so its liability cap and termination notice match our other agreements. Return a new DOCX with tracked changes and a comment explaining each change.
   ```

3. Download **Original** and open it in your word processor — review each tracked change before you send it.



**Swap in:** Scan Doc for the first step when you need every exact mention of a term; a project with **Always Search Documents** instead of attaching the files each time; a filled PDF instead of a DOCX for a form.

**If it stalls:** if citations point to the wrong file, name the files with `@`. If the run fails, select the **Run Python** step, read **Error**, and ask Bearly to retry with one file.

### Fill a PDF form from your own documents

You get a completed PDF built from facts in files you already have.

**Why it works:** the document tools read the facts from your attached files, and the Document editor can fill a form even when it has no fillable fields. Both run in the same chat, so the facts carry straight into the form.

**You need:** any plan. Admin controls **File uploads** and **Run Python**.



1. Attach the blank form and the documents with the details.

   ```text
   /document Fill in the attached supplier onboarding form using the company details in @company-profile.pdf and the bank letter. Leave any field you can't source blank and list those fields for me.
   ```

2. Open the filled PDF and compare it with the sources — ask for corrections by field name.
3. Optional: select **Share file** on the result to give it a Page of its own that you can publish.



**Swap in:** a scanned form (Bearly reads it with text recognition first); a Word template instead of a PDF; several forms in one request, up to five output files.

**If it stalls:** if text lands in the wrong place on a form without fields, tell Bearly which field moved and ask it to render the page and check before returning it.

### Proofread a report, then publish it as Word and PDF

You get a corrected report as both an editable Word file and a PDF.

**Why it works:** Fix Grammar keeps your wording and applies only the changes you accept. The corrected text stays in the chat, so the Document editor can lay it out as files in one run.

**You need:** any plan. Admin control **Run Python** for the files.



1. Proofread first.

   ```text
   /grammar Paste your report text here.
   ```

2. Accept the changes you want, then select **Copy corrected text** and paste it into a new message with the next request.

   ```text
   /document Lay out this text as a two-page report with a title, headings, and page numbers. Return a DOCX and a PDF.
   ```

3. Download both files — choose **Original** for the DOCX.



**Swap in:** a slide deck instead of a report; [Edit in Canvas](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md#canvas) for a longer rewrite before proofreading; [email the files](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/email-and-text.md) to yourself.

**If it stalls:** for a long report, proofread one section per message so each passage keeps its context.

## Related

- [Analyze data and run code](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code.md)
- [Make Pages and edit in Canvas](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md)
- [Projects](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects.md)
- [Privacy and security](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/privacy-and-security.md)

---
title: "Analyze data and run code"
description: "How to analyze spreadsheets and files with Run Python, draw KPI, bar, and line charts with Charts, and pick the right visual with Visualize in Bearly, including what the Python sandbox includes, file inputs and outputs, chart limits, and how these combine with documents and Pages."
canonical: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code/"
markdown: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code.md"
---

# Analyze data and run code

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code/)

> How to analyze spreadsheets and files with Run Python, draw KPI, bar, and line charts with Charts, and pick the right visual with Visualize in Bearly, including what the Python sandbox includes, file inputs and outputs, chart limits, and how these combine with documents and Pages.

Bearly can run Python on your files in a cloud sandbox, turn the result into a chart, and build it into an interactive Page. Ask in plain words; Bearly writes and runs the code, and you see the code, the result, and any files it made.

## Which tool for which job

| You want | Use | Type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Numbers worked out from a spreadsheet, a cleaned file, a plot, a converted file | [Run Python](#run-python) | `/python` |
| A finished Word, PowerPoint, Excel, or PDF file | [Document editor](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/documents.md#document-editor) | `/document` |
| A KPI, bar, or line chart you can hover and download | [Charts](#charts) | `/chart` |
| An interactive calculator, planner, or dashboard you keep | [Page](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md) | Ask for it |
| Bearly to choose the smallest useful visual | [Visualize](#visualize) | `/visualize` |
| Code that runs on files on your own computer | [Work locally](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/browser-and-computer.md) (desktop app) | Turn it on in the composer |

As a rule of thumb, Bearly uses Python for calculation, cleaning, and one-off charts, and a Page for interactive tools you'll come back to.

## Run Python

Run Python executes a complete Python script in a fresh cloud sandbox and returns what it printed and any files it saved. It's the engine behind data analysis, file conversion, and the Document editor.

**Use it when:** summarizing a spreadsheet by region and month, cleaning a messy export, running a statistical test, converting between file formats, resizing or processing images, or trimming and merging audio and video.

**You need:** any plan; each run counts as usage. Admin control: **Run Python** (under **Code & Automation**). It works on every platform because it runs in the cloud.



1. Attach the data files, or point to files already in the chat or project with `@` — the sandbox only gets the files Bearly hands to that run.
2. Ask for the result you want, or start with `/python` to make Bearly use it — the menu shows **Run Python** under **Tools**.

   ```text
   /python From the attached sales.xlsx, total revenue by region and quarter, show the top three regions by growth, and save the summary as a CSV.
   ```

3. Watch the **Run Python** step — select it to see **Input** (the code), **Result** (what it printed), and **Error** if something failed. Bearly often runs a quick look at the columns first, then the full analysis.
4. Open the output files — they appear as file cards in the chat, and saved plots show inline.



**What you get:** the answer in the chat, plus up to five new files per run, such as CSVs, spreadsheets, images, PDFs, or videos. Each file has **Download**, and later turns can use it.

**Limits:**

- Each run starts from scratch. Nothing carries over between runs unless Bearly passes a file from an earlier run back in.
- Runs are short and resource-limited. Long jobs, endless loops, and heavy processing of large media may not finish; split the work.
- Up to five output files per run; extras are dropped.
- The sandbox has internet access for small public downloads, but it can't reach your computer, your local files, or your apps. For that, use [Work locally](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/browser-and-computer.md) in the desktop app.
- Not available in [Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md).

**Combines with:** [Charts](#charts), which draw from a CSV that Python prepares; [Pages](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md), which can carry Python's results; [Text to speech](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/images-video-audio.md#text-to-speech) and [Make Video](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/images-video-audio.md#make-video), whose files Python can merge.

### What the sandbox includes

The sandbox comes with common Python libraries, and Bearly can install a missing package for a run.

- **Data and statistics:** pandas, NumPy, SciPy, statsmodels, scikit-learn, SymPy.
- **Charts:** matplotlib, seaborn, Plotly, Altair.
- **Office files:** openpyxl, xlrd, pyxlsb, python-docx, python-pptx.
- **PDFs and text recognition:** pypdf, pdfplumber, pdfminer.six, pdf2image, ReportLab, pytesseract.
- **Images:** Pillow, OpenCV.
- **Web:** requests, httpx, Beautiful Soup, lxml.

Command-line tools are available to scripts too: ffmpeg for audio and video, ImageMagick and image optimizers, Poppler, Ghostscript, and qpdf for PDFs, Tesseract for text recognition, and pandoc and LibreOffice for document conversion.

For office documents, the sandbox also has helpers that the [Document editor](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/documents.md#document-editor) uses: filling PDF forms, editing Word files with tracked changes, building decks from PowerPoint templates, and writing Excel workbooks with real formulas.

### In end-to-end encrypted chats

Run Python works in end-to-end encrypted chats. The files you give a run are sent to the cloud sandbox so it can process them, up to 50 MB in total per run, and each output file can be up to 100 MB. See [Privacy and security](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/privacy-and-security.md).

## Charts

Charts draws a **KPI**, **bar** (single, grouped, or stacked), or **line** (single or multi-series) chart from a small, prepared CSV file. The chart card is interactive and you can download both the picture and the data.

**Use it when:** showing monthly revenue as a line, comparing regions as grouped bars, or putting one headline number with its goal on screen.

**You need:** any plan. Admin control: **Charts** (under **Media & Creativity**). To prepare the data, Bearly needs **Run Python**, or **Work locally** in the desktop app; without either, it can chart only a CSV that's already in chart-ready shape.



1. Attach the data, or use a table already in the chat.
2. Ask for the chart, or start with `/chart` — the menu shows **Create chart**.

   ```text
   /chart Show monthly revenue for 2026 as a line chart, one line per product, in USD.
   ```

3. Bearly prepares a chart-ready CSV first, usually with Run Python, then draws the chart from that file — the rows never pass through the chart request itself.
4. Use the card — point or tap to read the nearest value, select **View data** to see the CSV grid and **View chart** to go back, and use **Download CSV** or **Download PNG**.



**What you get:** a **Chart** card in the chat. Bar and line charts download as a PNG at twice screen resolution.

Chart options you can ask for:

- **Bar:** sort by category or by value.
- **Line:** dates or ordered labels along the bottom.
- **Values:** plain numbers, currency (AUD, CAD, CHF, CNY, EUR, GBP, INR, JPY, KRW, or USD), or percentages from either fractions or whole numbers.
- **KPI:** an optional goal with a progress indicator.
- **Text:** a title up to 160 characters and a description up to 500.

**Limits:**

| Chart | Rows | Other limits |
| --- | --- | --- |
| KPI | Exactly 1 | — |
| Bar | Up to 100 | Up to 30 categories |
| Line | Up to 2,000 | Up to 12 series |
| Every CSV | — | At most 10 columns, 2 MB, and 2,000 characters per cell |

Duplicate keys are rejected, and a chart never quietly samples or cuts rows, so data over the limits needs aggregating first. If a chart fails to draw, the card says "The chart could not load. The data is available below." Turning the **Charts** control off stops new charts but leaves existing ones visible. Charts aren't drawn in [Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md).

**Combines with:** [Run Python](#run-python) to aggregate data down to chart size, [Research](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/research.md) to chart figures from a report, and [Pages](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md) for a dashboard with filters.

## Visualize

Visualize is a built-in skill that turns an idea, layout, process, or dataset into the smallest visual that makes it clearer. You don't pick the format; Bearly does.

**Use it when:** planning a room layout, comparing loan options side by side, mapping a process, or exploring a dataset where adjusting inputs helps you decide.

**You need:** any plan, on every platform. Admin control: **Skills**.



1. Type `/visualize` — it's listed under **Skills**.
2. Describe the decision and give real numbers, dimensions, or examples.

   ```text
   /visualize Compare a 15-year and a 30-year mortgage on $450,000 at 6.1% and 6.6%. Let me change the rate and down payment and see total interest.
   ```

3. Review what Bearly made — an interactive Page when controls help, or a static chart, table, or diagram when they wouldn't.



**What you get:** a focused visual with units and assumptions shown. When you change the brief in chat, Bearly edits the existing Page instead of making a new one when it can.

**Limits:** Visualize makes an image only when you explicitly ask for a realistic render or illustration. Bearly can't see what you select or type inside a Page, so if a follow-up depends on a value you picked there, tell Bearly the value.

> **Tip — Diagrams**
> Answers don't render diagram code blocks as pictures. For a flowchart, org chart, or timeline, ask for it as a Page or as an image made with Run Python.


## Python, Charts, and Pages together

The three tools hand work to each other inside one chat:

- **Python → Chart.** Python reads your upload and writes a summary CSV; the chart draws from that file.
- **Python → Page.** For a data-backed Page, Bearly cleans and aggregates with Python, then bakes the results into the Page as data, so the Page doesn't need the original file.
- **Documents → Python.** Spreadsheets attached to a chat are searchable as text, but for sums, filters, and pivots, Bearly passes the file to Python. See [Work with files and documents](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/documents.md).
- **Page → link.** Prepare and publish the Page to share it. See [Share and publish a Page](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md#share-and-publish-a-page).

## Try this

Each recipe runs in one chat.

### Turn a spreadsheet into a published dashboard

You get a summary table, a chart, and an interactive dashboard Page at a link your team can open.

**Why it works:** Python reads the upload and writes a chart-sized CSV, Charts draws from that file, and Bearly bakes the same aggregates into a Page as data. Sharing then prepares the Page with artwork and a link preview for you to review and publish.

**You need:** any plan. Admin controls **File uploads**, **Run Python**, **Charts**, and **Sharing**, plus **Public links** or **Share with people and teams** for the audience.



1. Attach the spreadsheet and ask for the analysis — check the **Run Python** step's **Result** for the totals.

   ```text
   Analyze the attached sales.xlsx by region and quarter. Save a summary CSV with revenue per region per quarter.
   ```

2. Chart it.

   ```text
   Chart quarterly revenue by region as a grouped bar chart in USD.
   ```

3. Build the dashboard — the Page opens in the chat; select **Open** to use it full size.

   ```text
   Build an interactive dashboard Page from that summary with filters for region and quarter, the grouped bar chart, and the three biggest changes called out at the top.
   ```

4. Open the Page and select **Share file**, then **Prepare share** — when it's ready, the prepared Page opens marked **Ready to share**. Select **Publish** in its header, choose the **Audience**, and select **Publish**.



**Swap in:** a CSV export instead of a workbook; a line chart of monthly revenue instead of bars; **People and teams** to restrict the link to named colleagues; [Deep Research](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/research.md) figures instead of your own data.

**If it stalls:** if the chart is refused for size, ask Bearly to aggregate to fewer rows or categories first. If the publish popover says "Your team policy does not allow a publishing audience.", ask an admin to turn on **Public links** or **Share with people and teams**.

### Get a spoken KPI briefing from a CSV

You get last month's key numbers as KPI charts and a 30-second audio briefing.

**Why it works:** Python computes the figures and writes one-row CSVs that KPI charts can draw. The numbers stay in the chat, so text to speech can read a short script built from them.

**You need:** any plan. Admin controls **Run Python**, **Charts**, and **Text to speech**.



1. Attach the export and ask for the figures.

   ```text
   From the attached orders.csv, compute last month's revenue, order count, and average order value, with the change from the month before.
   ```

2. Chart the headline number.

   ```text
   Show last month's revenue as a KPI with a goal of $250,000.
   ```

3. Record the briefing — the take appears in Voice Studio, where you can play and download it.

   ```text
   /say Write and record a 30-second briefing on these numbers, calm and clear, leading with the biggest change.
   ```



**Swap in:** a bar chart of revenue by channel instead of a KPI; [email](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/email-and-text.md) the audio file to yourself; a Page that shows the numbers with the audio.

**If it stalls:** if the KPI is refused, ask for the figure as a single-row CSV first. If audio fails with "The audio could not be created.", ask again; Bearly doesn't retry a failed recording on its own.

### Rebuild a messy export as a working Excel model

You get a clean workbook whose totals are live formulas, not pasted numbers.

**Why it works:** Python cleans the data, and the sandbox's Excel helpers write formulas and recalculate them, so the file keeps working when you change an input.

**You need:** any plan. Admin control **Run Python**.



1. Attach the export.

   ```text
   /document Clean the attached transactions.csv: fix dates, split the category column, and remove duplicates. Build an Excel workbook with a Data sheet and a Summary sheet whose monthly totals and growth rates are formulas. Format it like a financial model.
   ```

2. Open the workbook and select **Download** → **Original** — the preview is a PDF, so download the original to check the formulas.
3. Ask for changes by sheet and cell — Bearly writes a new file each time.



**Swap in:** a PowerPoint summary from your template instead of a workbook; a CSV for a [chart](#charts) instead of formulas.

**If it stalls:** if the run times out on a large file, ask Bearly to process it in two passes or to sample the first rows to fix the cleaning rules first.

# Make Pages and edit in Canvas

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas/)

> How to build interactive Pages in Bearly, refine them through versions, prepare and publish them to a stable bearly.sh link with the right audience, share a conversation's key takeaways, and edit an answer as a document in Canvas, with every limit that applies.

A Page is something Bearly builds for you to use, not just read: a calculator, dashboard, planner, game, prototype, or interactive chart. Canvas is a document editor for revising an answer's text with Bearly's help. This page covers both, plus publishing a Page to a link.

## What a Page is

A Page is a single, self-contained web file that Bearly writes into the chat. It runs live inside the chat in a sandboxed frame, picks up the colors of your Bearly theme, and can load common script libraries from a short list of public sources. Because it's one file, it suits a focused tool better than a multi-page site.

Bearly makes a Page when you ask it to build, make, or create an app, game, calculator, dashboard, prototype, simulator, planner, editor, widget, or interactive visualization.

## Make a Page

**Use it when:** you want a loan calculator with your numbers, a trip planner, a quiz for a class, a pricing simulator, a clickable product prototype, or a dashboard of your data.

**You need:** any plan. A chat you can edit. Works on the web app, phone browsers, the desktop app, and the iOS app.



1. Describe what someone should be able to enter, choose, or see, and the calculations that matter.

   ```text
   Build a mortgage calculator with fields for loan amount, interest rate, and term. Show the monthly payment, total interest, and an amortization chart, with an optional annual overpayment.
   ```

2. Try the preview in the chat — hover it for **Download** and **Open**.
3. Select **Open** to use the Page full size in the side panel.
4. Ask for changes in the chat, naming the Page if the chat has several — each change saves a new version.

   ```text
   In the mortgage calculator, show how many months the overpayment removes and add a toggle for biweekly payments.
   ```



**What you get:** a live Page in the chat, saved as an `.html` file you can download and open in a browser. Reopen it later from **Browse files** → **Generated**.

**Limits:**

- Bearly can't see what you type or pick inside a Page. If a follow-up depends on a value you chose there, tell Bearly the value.
- A Page may remember what you entered when you reopen it in the side panel, but this isn't guaranteed, and the in-chat preview doesn't keep it. To keep a change, ask Bearly to revise the Page.
- When a Page uses files from the chat, such as images, saving embeds copies of them. Each file can be up to 10 MB and all embedded files together up to 25 MB; links to larger or unavailable files are removed and Bearly is told which.
- A Page is saved to the chat. To save it into the chat's project, ask for that explicitly; you need write access to the project.
- In a read-only chat you can view and download Pages but not change them.

**Combines with:** [Run Python](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code.md#run-python), which cleans and aggregates data that Bearly then bakes into the Page; [Charts](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code.md#charts) for a quick chart without a full Page; [Visualize](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code.md#visualize) to let Bearly choose between a Page and a static visual.

> **Tip — Diagrams**
> For a flowchart, org chart, or process map, ask for a Page that draws the diagram, or an image made with Run Python. Diagram code in an answer shows as plain code.


## Versions and editing

Every edit Bearly makes to a Page saves a new version (v1, v2, and so on). Once a Page has two or more versions, a version dropdown appears; select a version to view it. A label beside the dropdown says whether you're looking at the **Latest** version, the one **At this point** (the version from the message you opened it from), or an **Earlier** one.

Edits always build on the latest version, even if you were looking at an older one. There's no restore button; to bring back part of an earlier design, describe what you want back.

The side panel header also has **Copy**, **Share file** (when sharing is allowed and you can edit the chat), **Download**, and **Close**. Once a Page is prepared for sharing, **Publish** appears there too.

You can force the file tools with `/create-file` (**Create Asset**) to write a new Page and `/edit-file` (**Edit Asset**) to change one.

## Share and publish a Page

Publishing gives a Page its own link on bearly.sh. It happens in two stages: **prepare** (Bearly adds artwork, a link preview, and a short brief) and **publish** (you pick the audience and make it live). Nothing goes out until you select **Publish**.

**Use it when:** sending a dashboard to your team, sharing a calculator with a client, or posting a finished write-up that people can open in a browser.

**You need:** any plan; preparing uses text and image generation, which counts as usage. Admin controls **Sharing**, plus **Public links** for a link anyone can open or **Share with people and teams** for a restricted link (all under **Collaboration**). A chat you can edit.



1. Open the Page and select **Share file** — the popover reads "Share this file" ("Give your file a Page of its own, with artwork and a link preview.").
2. Select **Prepare share** — a progress screen shows "Writing the Page", "Reading the source", "Content ready", "Saving the artwork", and "Saving the Page".
3. Wait for it to finish — the prepared Page opens in the side panel, marked **Ready to share**.
4. Select **Publish** in the side panel header — the popover reads **Ready to share** ("Publish this Page to a link people can open.") and previews the link card: artwork, title, description, and bearly.sh.
5. Choose the **Audience** — **Anyone with the link** or **People and teams**. Only the audiences your organization allows are listed.
6. Select **Publish** — the popover changes to **Share** ("This Page is available at a stable link."). Use **Copy link**, or **Share…** for your device's share sheet where available.
7. For a **People and teams** link, select **Manage viewers** to search teammates by name or email (type at least 2 characters) and pick teams.



To share the whole conversation instead of one file, select the **Share** icon in the chat header. The popover reads "Share conversation" ("Turn the substance of this conversation into a Page, with artwork and a link preview.") with **Prepare share**. Your full conversation isn't shared: Bearly writes a Page with the substance itself, such as the answer, plan, findings, or the finished version of a creative piece, and you review it before publishing. If you close the prepared Page, select the **Share** icon again: **Review and publish** reopens it, and **Create another** prepares a fresh version.

**While it prepares:** **Stop building** cancels ("Building stopped. Nothing was published."). Closing the screen with its **×** returns you to the chat without cancelling. If the artwork can't be made, the popover says "The image model could not make artwork. Review the simple preview before publishing."

**After publishing:**

- **Publish update** sends your latest changes to the same link. If the popover says "Use Share to add a preview image and conversation brief before publishing an update.", select **Share file** and prepare the Page again first.
- **Unpublish** takes the link down. **Retry cleanup** appears if an unpublish didn't finish.
- The link stays the same when you republish, rename the file, or change the audience. The address is built from the file name on the first publish, for example `mortgage-calculator-` followed by a short code, on bearly.sh.
- Updates to a public link can take up to a minute to reach people who already opened it.
- People on a **People and teams** link sign in to Bearly to open it.
- Images, video, audio, and PDFs in a published Page show inline; other included files download.
- Published Pages carry **Continue conversation in Bearly**. It opens a new draft chat with the Page's brief and a link to the source, ready for the reader to send. It never imports your original chat and never sends or spends anything on its own.

**Limits:**

- 500 published links per person.
- A Page you prepare must be under 9 MB; a file you share must be under 300 MB ("Choose a Page under 9 MB or a file under 300 MB.").
- Link previews use a title up to 90 characters, a description up to 220, and artwork cropped to 1200 × 630.
- With neither audience allowed by your organization, the popover says "Your team policy does not allow a publishing audience." You can still preview and unpublish.
- Not available in read-only chats. [Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md) can write Pages but can't publish them.

### Publishing from an end-to-end encrypted chat

A published Page is a readable copy, so Bearly asks first: "Publish a readable copy?" — "The published Page isn't end-to-end encrypted. People you share it with can read it, and Bearly stores this copy on its servers. The rest of this chat stays encrypted." Select **Publish copy** to go ahead.

From an encrypted chat, a file included with the Page can be up to 25 MB ("Files over 25 MB can't be shared from an encrypted chat yet.").

## Share the key takeaways

`/share` (**Share the key takeaways**) asks Bearly to prepare the conversation as a Page, the same as the header **Share** button. You can also ask in plain words, such as "make this shareable". Preparing doesn't publish or send anything; you still review and select **Publish**.

```text
/share Focus on the final recommendation and the cost table, for a finance audience.
```

It appears only when sharing is allowed and the chat is writable.

## Canvas

Canvas turns an answer into a document you can edit side by side with Bearly. Select text, say what to change, and Bearly rewrites just that part.

**Use it when:** polishing a long draft, an essay, a cover letter, a policy, or a script, where you want to work on sections rather than regenerate the whole answer.

**You need:** any plan. No admin control. A chat you can edit.



1. Under an answer that has text, select **Edit in Canvas** (the notebook-and-pencil icon) — Bearly opens the text as a Canvas and gives it a short title.
2. Switch between **Preview** ("Show the formatted document") and **Edit** ("Edit the Markdown") — on wide screens, **Edit** shows the editor beside the preview.
3. Select some text, type in "Ask AI about this text...", and select **Submit** — Bearly runs a chat turn titled "Canvas Edit" that rewrites the selection. On a narrow screen, use the "How can I edit this for you?" box.
4. Or ask in the chat — once a Canvas exists, Bearly can edit it directly from an ordinary message.

   ```text
   In the Canvas, tighten the introduction to three sentences and make the closing paragraph more direct.
   ```

5. Use **Copy** in the toolbar, **Undo** and **Redo** while in **Edit**, and **Close Canvas** or <kbd>Esc</kbd> to close it.



**What you get:** a live document inside the chat. Its card reads "Canvas", or "Editing in Canvas" while open.

**Limits:** a Canvas has no version history and no download. **Copy** copies the Markdown; paste it wherever you need it, or ask Bearly to turn it into a file. A Canvas isn't a file, so it doesn't appear in **Browse files**. Not available in [Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md).

**Combines with:** [Fix Grammar](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/documents.md#fix-grammar) for a final proofread, the [Document editor](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/documents.md#document-editor) for a Word or PDF version, and Pages for a styled version you can publish.

## Try this

Each recipe runs in one chat.

### Draft in Canvas, then publish it as a styled Page

You get a polished document and a published, printable Page with the same text.

**Why it works:** Canvas edits stay in the chat, and Bearly writes a Page from the conversation, which includes the Canvas. Sharing then adds artwork and a link preview for review before anything goes live.

**You need:** any plan. Admin controls **Sharing** and an audience control (**Public links** or **Share with people and teams**).



1. Ask for a first draft, then select **Edit in Canvas** under it.

   ```text
   Draft a one-page onboarding guide for new support hires: first week schedule, tools to set up, and who to ask for what.
   ```

2. Revise it with selections — select a section, type the change in "Ask AI about this text...", and select **Submit**.
3. Turn the final text into a Page.

   ```text
   Turn the final Canvas into a styled, printable one-page Page with a clear heading hierarchy and a checklist for the first week.
   ```

4. Open the Page and select **Share file** → **Prepare share**. When the prepared Page opens, select **Publish**, choose the **Audience**, and select **Publish**.



**Swap in:** a DOCX with `/document` instead of a Page; `/share` to publish the substance of the whole chat; **People and teams** to limit the link to your team.

**If it stalls:** if the Page leaves out your last edits, copy the Canvas with **Copy** and paste it into the message that asks for the Page.

### Build an image gallery Page

You get a single Page that shows a set of images from the chat, with the images inside the file.

**Why it works:** Run Python takes image files from the chat as inputs and can write one HTML file with the images embedded. Any HTML file in a chat renders as a live Page, whatever made it.

**You need:** any plan. Admin control **Run Python**; **Images** if you generate the images first.



1. Get the images into the chat — attach them, or make them with `/image`.
2. Ask Python to build the Page.

   ```text
   /python Build a single HTML gallery Page from the images in this chat, with captions from the file names and a lightbox when I click one. Embed the images in the file.
   ```

3. Open the result — it renders as a Page; download it or prepare it for sharing.



**Swap in:** ask Bearly to write the Page directly and link the chat's files, which embeds each one up to 10 MB and 25 MB in total; a slideshow instead of a grid.

**If it stalls:** if the Page is too big to prepare for sharing (over 9 MB), ask Python to shrink the images first.

### Share a research chat's conclusion without the back-and-forth

You get a clean Page with just the findings, and a link that lets readers ask their own follow-ups.

**Why it works:** sharing writes the substance of the conversation, not the transcript, and published Pages carry **Continue conversation in Bearly**, which starts a reader's own draft chat from the Page's brief.

**You need:** any plan. Admin controls **Sharing** and **Public links**.



1. Finish the research in the chat.
2. Prepare the Page.

   ```text
   /share Lead with the recommendation, then the three reasons, then the sources.
   ```

3. When the prepared Page opens, check it, then select **Publish**, set **Audience** to **Anyone with the link**, select **Publish**, and use **Copy link**.



**Swap in:** **People and teams** for internal findings; a single file's **Share file** instead of the whole chat.

**If it stalls:** if the Page isn't what you wanted, select the header **Share** icon again and choose **Create another**, or add direction in a new `/share` message.

# Make images, video, and audio

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/images-video-audio/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/images-video-audio/)

> How to generate and edit images, make and edit short videos, turn text into speech with Read aloud and Voice Studio, transcribe audio and video, and have Bearly look at images and video, with each tool's options, limits, and admin controls.

Bearly can make an image, animate it into a video, record a voiceover, transcribe a recording, and watch a clip to check it, all in one chat. Every result is a file in the chat, so the next step can use it. Bearly makes images and speech only when you ask for them.

To point a tool at a file that's already in the chat, type `@` and pick the file under **Assets**; typing the file name yourself doesn't select it. See [Compose messages](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/compose.md).

| You want | Tool | Type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A new image from a description | [Imagine](#imagine) | `/image` |
| A change to an image, an edited area, wider edges, or a combined image | [The image editor](#the-image-editor) and Touch Up | `/edit-image` |
| A short video from text or an image | [Make Video](#make-video) | `/video` |
| A change to a short clip | [Edit Video](#edit-video) | `/edit-video` |
| Spoken audio from text | [Text to speech](#text-to-speech) | `/say` |
| Text from speech in a recording | [Transcribe](#transcribe) | `/transcribe` |
| Bearly to look at an image or watch a video | [View Media](#view-media) | `/analyze-media` |

## Imagine

Imagine generates an image from your description. The result opens in an image editor inside the answer, where you can keep iterating.

**Use it when:** you need a product shot, an illustration for a post, a mockup, a mood board image, or a starting frame for a video.

**You need:** any plan; every image run counts as usage. Admin control: **Images** (under **Media & Creativity**). Works in the web app, phone browsers, the desktop app, and the iOS app.



1. Describe the image — name the subject, composition, setting, medium, colors, and light.

   ```text
   /image A matte black water bottle on wet slate, soft overcast light, shallow depth of field, studio product photo.
   ```

2. Review the result in the editor — Bearly picks a model unless you name one.
3. Iterate in the editor or in the chat — see [The image editor](#the-image-editor).



**What you get:** an image in the chat with **Copy image**, **Download**, and **View full size**. In full view, zoom with <kbd>+</kbd> and <kbd>-</kbd>, reset the zoom with <kbd>0</kbd>, move between images with the arrow keys, and close with <kbd>Esc</kbd>.

**Limits:** there's no size or aspect-ratio setting; the output shape depends on the model. When Bearly runs an image request from the chat and the model's service fails, it switches to a backup model.

## The image editor

The latest image answer in the chat holds the image editor, and its thumbnail strip shows the chat's images. Earlier image answers show **Go to latest image edit** so you can jump to it.

**Use it when:** you want to compare models on one prompt, change part of an image, widen a shot, or merge a product into a scene.

**You need:** the same as Imagine. In a read-only chat the editor is visible but disabled.

Choose an action at the top of the editor:

| Action | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| **Create image** | Makes a new image from the prompt |
| **Edit image** | Changes the selected image as you describe |
| **Change area** | Changes only a part you select: drag a rectangle over the image, or use **Select center**. The × clears the selection |
| **Extend edges** | Widens the image by 25% of its width or height on every side and fills the new space |
| **Combine images** | Blends two or more source images, such as a room and a product |



1. Pick an action and type in the prompt box — it reads "Describe the image you want to create…" or "What would you like to change?". For edits, say what changes and what stays.
2. Optional: select **Refine** for a better-worded prompt, then **Use this** or **Dismiss** — Refine reads only your text, not the images.
3. Add sources with **Upload** ("Upload images") in the thumbnail strip, or select a result in the strip to work on it.
4. Run it — select a featured model (**Good**, **Fast**, or the third suggestion; **Fast** isn't offered for **Change area** or **Extend edges**), pick any model from **All models** ("Run with a model", "One image per click"), or reuse one under **Recent**.



Each click starts its own run, so you can start several models at once and compare. Unfinished thumbnails show **Running**, **Pending**, **Failed**, or **Canceled**; select one for **Cancel run**, **Try again**, or **Dismiss**. **Re-run** repeats a result's original instructions and inputs. A click runs exactly the model you chose, with no automatic retry.

**Limits:** only some models support **Change area** and **Extend edges**, and the editor marks the others with "No area edits". Models are also marked "Edits only", "Create only", "One input image", or "Up to N input images" when they can't do the current action. For area edits, the model uses your selection as a guide; it isn't guaranteed to leave every other pixel untouched.

To edit from the chat instead, type `/edit-image` (**Touch Up**) and describe the change. If the chat has several images, type `@` and pick the one you mean under **Assets**.

```text
/edit-image Make the background of the bottle photo a warm sunset and keep the bottle exactly as it is.
```

**Combines with:** [Make Video](#make-video), which can use any image in the chat as its first or last frame, and [Pages](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md) for a gallery.

## Make Video

Make Video generates a short video with sound, from a description alone or by animating an image.

**Use it when:** you need a short social clip, a product teaser, an animated version of an image, or a moving background for a presentation.

**You need:** any plan; usage is counted by the second of video, and higher resolution costs more. Admin control: **Video** (under **Media & Creativity**).



1. Describe the shot, motion, and sound — put any dialogue in quotes.

   ```text
   /video An 8-second vertical video of a lighthouse at dusk, slow push-in, waves and gulls in the background.
   ```

2. Optional: pick an image in the chat with `@` as the start frame, and another as the end frame — an end frame needs a start frame.

   ```text
   /video Animate the bottle photo: rain starts falling and the camera slowly circles the bottle. 6 seconds.
   ```

3. Watch the result in the player.



**What you get:** a video card with your prompt in italics, followed by the model name, length, resolution, and **Audio**. The player has **Play video** / **Pause video**, **Mute video** / **Unmute video**, and **Download video**, which saves an MP4.

**Options:**

- **Length:** 4 to 15 seconds. The default is 5.
- **Resolution:** standard 768p by default, or 2K, which costs more.
- **Shape:** 16:9 by default, 9:16, or 1:1. With a start image, the video keeps the image's shape.
- **Sound:** every video includes generated sound.

**Limits:** each attempt counts separately, and a failed attempt isn't retried on another model. On failure you'll see "The video model could not finish this request. Try again later."

## Edit Video

Edit Video changes a short clip you attach or one Bearly made, and saves the result as a new clip.

**Use it when:** you want to change an existing short clip by describing the edit, instead of generating a new video.

**You need:** any plan; usage is counted by the second. Admin control: **Video**.



1. Attach the clip, or pick one already in the chat with `@`.

   ```text
   /edit-video Turn the harbor clip into a snowy winter morning. Keep the boats and camera move the same.
   ```

2. Watch the new clip — it's badged **Edited**, and your source stays unchanged.



**Options:** the original sound is kept by default. Resolution is 1080p by default, or 720p or 4K.

**Limits:** use a source clip 3 to 10 seconds long. Files over 50 MB are refused.

## Text to speech

Bearly can read any text answer aloud and make downloadable voice recordings in Voice Studio.

### Read aloud

Under any answer that's all text, select the speaker icon (**Read aloud**) to hear it in a standard voice. To use your own choice of voice and delivery instead, turn on **Use for Read Aloud** in Voice Studio.

### Voice Studio

Voice Studio is where you write a script, pick a voice, direct the delivery, and record takes. It appears in the chat as a panel headed **Voice** ("Make audio from this chat").

**Use it when:** recording a video voiceover, a podcast intro, an audiobook passage, an announcement, a calm wind-down script, or listening to your own draft to catch problems.

**You need:** any plan; samples, takes, and comparisons all count as usage. Admin control: **Text to speech** (under **Media & Creativity**). The same control turns off **Read aloud**.



1. Type `/say` with the text, or ask Bearly to read something aloud — Bearly records a first take and opens the studio. Ask for the studio without giving any text and it opens without recording. `/tts` and `/texttospeech` work too.

   ```text
   /say Welcome to the quarterly update. Three things changed this quarter, and all three are good news.
   ```

2. Pick what you're making under "What are you making?": **Video voiceover**, **Podcast intro**, **Audiobook**, **Proofread by ear**, **Announcement**, or **Wind-down** — each preset picks an engine, three suggested voices, and a delivery.
3. Edit the script — the box reads "Write a script, or start from the conversation above…". **Use reply** and **Use my message** fill it from the chat. A counter shows the characters and an estimated length.
4. Choose a voice under **Cast** — select ▶ on a card to hear it read your opening line. Suggested voices are marked with a dot.
5. Direct the delivery — type it ("Direct the delivery, e.g. warm and unhurried") or pick "Warm and calm", "Bright and upbeat", "Clear and measured", "Dramatic", or "Whispered".
6. Select **Generate** (or press <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd> in the script box, <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd> on Windows and Linux) for one take, or **Compare 3** to record the script in your chosen voice and two others at once; after you pick a preset, those are its three suggested voices. When the studio is collapsed, **Hear this reply** records the latest answer.



**What you get:** takes in a player that highlights words as they play, with **Download audio**. Older takes are under **Earlier takes**. Each take is also saved as an audio file in the chat (`.wav` or `.mp3`), so later steps can use it.

**Limits:** Voice Studio appears on the latest text-to-speech result in the chat; earlier results show a plain player. The word highlighting uses estimated timing. A failed recording isn't retried automatically; when a recording you asked for in the chat fails, you'll see "The audio could not be created." In read-only chats you get plain players only. The studio's model switch picks the voice engine, such as **Fast** or **Studio**.

## Transcribe

Transcribe turns speech in audio and video files into text with speaker labels and timestamps, and saves a subtitle file you can reuse.

**Use it when:** you have an interview, lecture, podcast, voice memo, or recorded call as a file and want quotes, a summary, action items, or subtitles.

**You need:** any plan. Admin control: **Transcription** (under **Media & Creativity**).



1. Attach one or more audio or video files — Bearly can transcribe several in one request.
2. Type `/transcribe`, or just ask about what was said — Bearly transcribes before it summarizes or answers questions about a recording.

   ```text
   /transcribe Transcribe the attached interview and name the speakers if you can tell who they are.
   ```

3. Read the transcript in the viewer — switch between **Captions** and **Table**, adjust **Density**, and type names for the speakers in the boxes under the transcript. Press play to follow along ("Play to begin").



**What you get:** a transcript viewer, with previous and next buttons when you sent several files, and **Download subtitles**, which saves `AudioSubtitles.srt`. Bearly also saves each transcript in the chat as a subtitle file named after the original, such as `interview transcript.srt` for `interview.mp3`, so later messages and tools can read it. Bearly suggests speaker names from what's said in the recording, and you can change them.

**Limits:** each file can be up to 300 MB, the limit for any chat file. There's no language picker. If some files fail you'll see "Some assets could not be transcribed". Asking about the same file again reuses its transcript.

For live recording with a running transcript, use [Meetings and Recordings](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/meetings.md).

## View Media

View Media lets Bearly look at images and watch video files in the chat, including images from much earlier in a long conversation.

**Use it when:** checking a generated video for mistakes, describing a chart screenshot, reading a whiteboard photo, or comparing two product images.

**You need:** any plan.



1. Type `/analyze-media` (**View Media**) and say which file, picking it with `@` if the chat has several, or just ask Bearly to look.

   ```text
   /analyze-media Watch the demo video and list every moment where the on-screen text doesn't match the voiceover, with timestamps.
   ```



**What you get:** a written answer about what's in the image or video.

**Limits:** Bearly watches up to 10 minutes of video per look and takes longer videos in sections. When it can't watch a video directly, it samples timestamped frames and pairs them with a transcript.

## Controls and model settings

- Organization admins turn these tools on or off with **Images**, **Video**, **Text to speech**, and **Transcription** under **Media & Creativity**. See [Organizations](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/organizations.md).
- **Zero Data Retention only** applies to chat models. It doesn't filter the image, video, transcription, or text-to-speech models, and organization model allow-lists don't apply to them either. With it on, text to speech starts from a different default voice engine. See [Privacy and security](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/privacy-and-security.md).
- [Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md) can't generate images or video, run text to speech, or transcribe.

## Try this

Each recipe runs in one chat.

### Product shot to a narrated video in one MP4

You get a product image, an animated clip made from it, a recorded voiceover, and one MP4 with both.

**Why it works:** every result is a file in the chat. Make Video takes the generated image as its start frame, Voice Studio saves the narration as an audio file, and Run Python can merge the two with ffmpeg, which the sandbox includes.

**You need:** any plan. Admin controls **Images**, **Video**, **Text to speech**, and **Run Python**.



1. Make the image.

   ```text
   /image A matte black water bottle on wet stone, rain in the air, moody product photography.
   ```

2. Animate it — check the video's length badge before you write the script.

   ```text
   /video Animate the bottle image into an 8-second video with a slow push-in and rain sounds.
   ```

3. Record the voiceover — pick **Video voiceover** in Voice Studio and **Generate** until a take fits.

   ```text
   /say Rain or shine, it keeps your water cold for twenty-four hours.
   ```

4. Merge them.

   ```text
   /python Put the latest voiceover onto the video as one MP4. Lower the video's own sound under the voice. Keep the length of the video.
   ```



**Swap in:** skip the image and describe the whole shot in `/video` when you need a set shape, such as 16:9 for a presentation (an animated image keeps the image's shape); **Combine images** to place the product in a room before animating; **Edit Video** to change the scene before merging.

**If it stalls:** Python runs are short and resource-limited, so keep the clip short and the resolution standard. If the voice runs longer than the video, shorten the script and record a new take.

### Burn subtitles into a video

You get your video with captions drawn onto the picture, ready for platforms that play muted.

**Why it works:** Transcribe saves the transcript as an `.srt` subtitle file in the chat, and Run Python can pass both the video and that file to ffmpeg.

**You need:** any plan. Admin controls **Transcription** and **Run Python**.



1. Attach the video and transcribe it — Bearly saves the transcript as an `.srt` file in the chat.

   ```text
   /transcribe Transcribe the attached clip.
   ```

2. Burn in the captions.

   ```text
   /python Burn the transcript .srt for this clip into the video as white captions with a dark outline at the bottom. Return an MP4.
   ```



**Swap in:** a clip from Make Video instead of your own; **Download subtitles** to upload captions to a video platform separately instead of burning them in.

**If it stalls:** long or high-resolution videos may be too heavy for one Python run; trim the clip first or split it into parts.

### Turn a podcast episode into a 60-second spoken recap

You get a short summary of the episode's main points, recorded in a voice you choose.

**Why it works:** Transcribe saves the episode's text in the chat, Bearly summarizes from it, and text to speech records the summary as a take.

**You need:** any plan. Admin controls **Transcription** and **Text to speech**.



1. Attach the episode and ask.

   ```text
   Transcribe the attached episode, write a 60-second summary of the three main arguments, and read it aloud in a warm podcast-host voice.
   ```

2. Adjust in Voice Studio — pick **Podcast intro**, then **Compare 3** to record the summary in its three suggested voices, and **Download audio** on the take you like.



**Swap in:** a meeting from [Meetings and Recordings](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/meetings.md), where **Add to chat** brings its transcript into the chat so you can skip transcribing; [email](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/email-and-text.md) the audio to yourself.

**If it stalls:** attached files can be up to 300 MB; for a longer episode, export a smaller audio-only copy first.

### Watch a generated clip and fix what's wrong

You get a corrected clip after Bearly reviews its own video.

**Why it works:** View Media lets Bearly watch any video in the chat, and Edit Video works on clips of 3 to 10 seconds under 50 MB, which covers generated clips of 10 seconds or less.

**You need:** any plan. Admin control **Video**.



1. Make the clip with `/video`.
2. Ask for a review and a fix.

   ```text
   Watch the clip and list anything inconsistent, such as objects that change or appear from nowhere. Then fix the worst one with Edit Video.
   ```

3. Compare the original and the clip badged **Edited**.



**Swap in:** your own phone video, as long as it's 3 to 10 seconds and under 50 MB; ask for a new clip with `/video` when the problem is the whole shot.

**If it stalls:** if the clip is longer than 10 seconds, trim it to 10 seconds or less with Run Python first.

- [Record and use meetings](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/meetings.md)
