# Record and use meetings

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/meetings/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/meetings/)

> How to record online meetings, in-person conversations, and voice notes in Bearly's Meetings and Recordings page, follow the live transcript, get the final transcript and speaker names, summarize, export, and hand a transcript to a chat or project to draft follow-ups.

**Meetings and Recordings** records a call, a meeting in the room, or a voice note and transcribes it while people speak. When you stop, the audio and transcript are saved to your account, and you can name speakers, summarize, export, or hand the transcript to a chat to draft follow-ups.

**You need:** any plan; live transcription uses your plan allowance. Admin control: **Meetings** (under **Collaboration**).

## Choose what to record

Select **Meetings and Recordings** in the sidebar, then pick a source card:

| Card | What it records | Where it works |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Online meeting** | The call's audio from your computer and your microphone together, for video calls in any app | The desktop app on Windows, or on a Mac with macOS 13 or later; on Linux and older macOS versions the desktop app records your microphone only. On the web app, phone browsers, and the iOS app the card shows **Get desktop app** |
| **In person or voice note** | The room, through this device's microphone ("Use this device's microphone to record the room.") | Every platform |

On a Mac, recording computer audio uses the system's Screen Recording permission. If that access is off, Bearly records your microphone only and shows **Open Screen Recording settings**; if the microphone is blocked in the desktop app, you'll see **Open microphone settings**.

## Record

**Use it when:** taking notes in a client call, capturing a lecture, recording an interview, or dictating a long voice note you want turned into text later.



1. Choose **Online meeting** or **In person or voice note** — allow microphone access if your device asks.
2. Select **Start recording** — the live, speaker-labelled transcript appears as people talk.
3. Pause if you need to — **Pause recording** stops both the audio and the live transcript, and **Resume recording** picks up again. Pausing appears where your device supports it.
4. Select **Stop recording** (it shows the elapsed time) — the recording uploads, and Bearly starts the final transcript.



While you record, you can keep working anywhere in Bearly. With the sidebar open, a compact recording card shows **Pause**, **Resume**, and **Stop**; with it closed, a floating bar shows the same controls on every page. Bearly suggests a title at 30 seconds and again at 3 minutes, unless you've typed your own.

The footer of the recorder sums up how it works: "Saved continuously on this device", "Recording uploads after you stop", "Up to 1 hr 15 min", and "Only record people with their permission."

**Limits:**

- A recording can run up to 1 hour 15 minutes. At the limit Bearly stops and keeps everything ("Recording stopped at the 1 hr 15 min limit.").
- Bearly needs at least 250 MB of free storage on the device before it starts.
- If your allowance runs out mid-meeting, the audio keeps recording and the transcript pauses: "Live transcript paused at the account limit. Audio recording continues."
- In the desktop app, recording continues while you move around Bearly or the window reloads. In a browser, keep the tab open: a refresh stops the capture, but audio already saved survives.

> **Caution — Consent**
> Only record people with their permission. Recording laws vary by place, and getting consent is your responsibility.


## The final transcript and speakers

After the upload is checked, Bearly transcribes the whole recording again with speaker separation and replaces the live transcript. You'll see "Improving speaker labels…" and then "Speaker labels improved". Older recordings that never had this pass show **Improve speaker labels**.

- The transcript header shows how many speakers were identified, or "Voice memo" for a single voice.
- To name speakers, type each name once ("Name each speaker once. Every matching turn updates."). Names carry through to exports and to what you add to a chat.
- If the final transcript fails, the recording shows **Needs attention** and "The final transcript could not be completed." with **Retry**. The audio stays saved.

## Summarize, copy, and download

Select a recording in the list to open it.

- **Create summary** writes a **Quick summary** with Overview, Key points, Decisions, Action items, and Open questions. Owners and due dates appear only if someone said them. **Copy** copies it and **Refresh** rewrites it. Decisions, Action items, or Open questions with nothing to report say "None captured."
- **Copy** next to the transcript opens an export dialog: **Text only**, **Speaker names**, or **Names and timestamps** (a voice memo offers **Text only** or **Timestamps**). Then **Copy**, or **Download .txt**.
- The audio player plays the original at 0.75× to 2× speed, with a download button for the original audio file (`.m4a` or `.webm`).
- **Delete recording** asks "Delete this recording?" and then permanently deletes the audio, transcript, speaker names, and summary.

Completed recordings and transcripts are saved to your account, so they're on your other devices when you sign in.

## Hand the transcript to a chat or project

This is where a recording turns into work. The transcript becomes a file that Bearly can read, quote, and build on.



1. Open the recording and select **Add to chat** — choose **New chat** ("Attach the transcript to a new draft"), or search with "Find a chat..." and pick an existing one.
2. Bearly attaches the transcript as a Markdown file and focuses the message box — nothing is sent until you send it.
3. Type what you want done with it and send.



The file has the title, when it was recorded, its length, and every turn with its timestamp, plus the speaker's name when more than one person spoke. The summary isn't included; ask Bearly for one in the chat if you want it there.

**Add to project** uploads the same file to a project's files ("Transcript added"), so every chat in that project can search it. It needs write access to the project and the **File uploads** and **Project documents and data** controls. If you have no projects yet, the button is unavailable and its tooltip says "Create a Project before adding this transcript".

### What you can ask once the transcript is in a chat

```text
List every action item with its owner and due date, only where someone said them. Then draft a follow-up email to the attendees.
```

```text
Quote the three moments where the client pushed back on pricing, with timestamps.
```

```text
Turn this interview into a 600-word profile in the third person. Keep every direct quote word for word.
```

```text
What did we decide, what's still open, and who needs to hear about each decision?
```

Bearly reads the attached transcript with its [document tools](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/documents.md#research-your-documents-search-docs-read-doc-scan-doc), so it can cite and quote specific turns.

## Recovery

- If an upload can't finish, the recording stays on the device that made it, marked **Saved on device**, with **Retry upload** and **Discard**. **Discard** asks "Discard this saved recording?" and then permanently removes the audio and transcript stored on that device.
- Bearly deletes the local copy only after it confirms the uploaded file is complete.

For an audio or video file you already have, use [Transcribe](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/images-video-audio.md#transcribe) in a chat instead.

## Try this

Each recipe starts from a finished recording.

### Turn a meeting into action items and a follow-up email

You get a list of action items with stated owners, a drafted follow-up, and the email sent.

**Why it works:** **Add to chat** puts the full transcript in the chat as a file, so Bearly can pull out what each person agreed to. Send Email can then send the draft to you, or to teammates Bearly can find on your team, from the same chat.

**You need:** any plan. Admin controls **Meetings** and **Email notifications**.



1. Open the recording, select **Add to chat** → **New chat**.
2. Ask for the actions and the draft — check names and dates against the transcript.

   ```text
   List every action item with its owner and due date, only where someone said them. Then draft a short follow-up email that thanks the group and lists the actions.
   ```

3. Send it.

   ```text
   Email that follow-up to me with the action list.
   ```



**Swap in:** a text message to yourself instead of email; a [Page](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md) instead of an email for a longer recap; [Fix Grammar](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/documents.md#fix-grammar) on the draft before sending. See [Email and text](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/email-and-text.md) for recipients and limits.

**If it stalls:** if the transcript shows generic speaker labels instead of names, name the speakers on the recording first, then add it to the chat again.

### Publish the decisions to your team as a restricted Page

You get a one-page summary of decisions at a link only named colleagues can open.

**Why it works:** the transcript in the chat feeds a Page, and a **People and teams** audience limits the published link to the people and teams you choose.

**You need:** any plan. Admin controls **Meetings**, **Sharing**, and **Share with people and teams**.



1. Select **Add to chat** → **New chat** on the recording.
2. Build the Page.

   ```text
   Turn the decisions from this meeting into a one-page Page: decision, reason, owner, and date, with open questions at the end.
   ```

3. Open the Page and select **Share file** → **Prepare share**. When the prepared Page opens, select **Publish**, set **Audience** to **People and teams**, and select **Publish**. Then use **Manage viewers** to add the people and teams who can open it.



**Swap in:** **Anyone with the link** for a client recap (needs the **Public links** control); `/share` to publish the substance of the whole chat.

**If it stalls:** if **People and teams** isn't offered, your organization hasn't allowed restricted links; ask an admin.

### Build a searchable library of your meetings

You get a project where you can ask questions across every meeting you've added, with citations.

**Why it works:** **Add to project** saves each transcript to the project's files, and with **Always Search Documents** on, every chat in the project searches those files before answering.

**You need:** any plan. Admin controls **Meetings**, **File uploads**, and **Project documents and data**. Write access to the project.



1. After each meeting, open the recording and select **Add to project**, then pick the project.
2. In the project's settings, turn on **Always Search Documents** — see [Work with files and documents](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/documents.md#always-search-documents-projects).
3. Ask across meetings in any chat in the project.

   ```text
   Across our weekly syncs this quarter, how did the launch date change and who raised each concern? Cite the meeting for each point.
   ```



**Swap in:** one project per client or per course; lecture recordings instead of meetings.

**If it stalls:** search doesn't run in end-to-end encrypted chats, so use a standard chat in the project.

### Turn a voice note into a draft you can edit

You get a structured draft from a spoken idea, ready to polish.

**Why it works:** **In person or voice note** works on any device, the transcript goes to a chat as a file, and Canvas lets you revise the draft section by section.

**You need:** any plan. Admin control **Meetings**.



1. Record with **In person or voice note** while you talk the idea through, then **Stop recording**.
2. Select **Add to chat** → **New chat** and ask for the draft.

   ```text
   Turn this voice note into a blog post outline and a first draft. Keep my examples and phrasing where they're strong.
   ```

3. Select **Edit in Canvas** under the draft and revise it.



**Swap in:** a project plan, a talk outline, or a list of tasks instead of a post.

**If it stalls:** a recording with one speaker shows as a "Voice memo" without speaker names; that's expected.

- [Projects](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects.md)
- [Email and text](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/email-and-text.md)

description: "How a Bearly desktop chat browses sites in Bearly Browser, works in your signed-in Chrome tabs, reads and edits files and runs commands with Work locally, and operates apps with Computer Use, including the approvals each asks for and how to choose between them."
canonical: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/browser-and-computer/"

# Let Bearly use a browser, your files, and your computer

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/browser-and-computer/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/browser-and-computer/)

> How a Bearly desktop chat browses sites in Bearly Browser, works in your signed-in Chrome tabs, reads and edits files and runs commands with Work locally, and operates apps with Computer Use, including the approvals each asks for and how to choose between them.

In the desktop app, a chat can do more than answer: it can open and use websites, work in the Chrome you already use, read and change files in a folder you pick, run commands, and click and type in the apps on your screen. Each tool asks before it acts unless you've turned on [Full access](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/permissions.md), and each one can be turned off by your organization.

All four tools need the desktop app for Mac, Windows, or Linux. On the web and on phones, chats that used them stay readable, but Bearly can't look at or change anything on your computer until you return to that chat in the desktop app. See [Get Bearly on every device](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/get-bearly.md).

## Which one to use

| You want Bearly to | Use | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Read or act on websites that don't need your everyday sign-ins | [Bearly Browser](#bearly-browser) | It's a separate browser with its own sign-ins, and routines can use it too |
| Work in a site you're already signed in to in Chrome, or inspect a page's console and network activity | [Control Chrome](#control-chrome) | It uses your real Chrome profile and its sessions |
| Read, create, or change files, run commands, or process a folder | [Work locally](#work-locally) | Direct file and command access is faster and more reliable than clicking through apps |
| Use a native app that has no other route in, such as a desktop accounting or design app | [Computer Use](#computer-use) | It sees the screen and controls the mouse and keyboard |
| Summarize the page you're reading, without the desktop app | The browser extension's side panel | See [Browser extension](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/get-bearly.md#browser-extension) |

| | Bearly Browser | Control Chrome | Work locally | Computer Use |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Works in | The desktop app | The desktop app plus Chrome with the Bearly Companion extension | The desktop app | The desktop app |
| Uses your sign-ins | Only ones you sign in to or import into Bearly Browser | Yes, your Chrome profile | Whatever your computer account can reach | Whatever is on screen |
| Standard asks before | Each new site, submissions, visual clicks, downloads, website tools | Reading tabs, controlling tabs, or using DevTools | Each read, write, edit, file transfer, and command | The task, then consequential actions |
| In routines | Yes, with browser access on listed sites | No | Yes, with unattended local access | No |
| Organization control | **Browser** | **Chrome extension browser** | **Work locally** | **Computer Use** |

Bearly picks the tool from your request. Name the one you want ("in Chrome", "in Bearly Browser", "in this folder") when it matters.

## Bearly Browser

Bearly Browser is a browser built into the desktop app. Chats work in their own tabs in the background; you can open the panel to watch, browse yourself, or take over a tab. Its sign-ins belong to this Bearly account on this device and are separate from your everyday browser.

**Use it when:** comparing public pages, checking a site you've signed in to inside Bearly Browser, testing a site running on your computer (`http://localhost`) or local network, or giving a routine a browser.
**You need:** the desktop app; your organization allows **Browser**.



1. Open **App Settings** → **Desktop** → **Browser** and turn on **Enable Browser** and **Allow chats to use Browser** — the first makes the browser available, the second lets chats drive it. Both are on by default.
2. Optional: under **Import browser cookies**, choose a **Browser profile** and select **Import** — this copies current site sign-ins from Chrome or Edge into Bearly Browser. Only cookies are copied, never passwords, history, bookmarks, or autofill.
3. Ask in a chat, naming the site and the boundary:

   ```text
   In Bearly Browser, open the pricing pages for these three tools and compare their plans in a table. Don't sign in, start a trial, or submit anything.
   ```

4. At **Let this chat use this site?**, check the site, pick an **Approval duration**, and select the Allow button — approval covers that one site for this chat. Another site asks separately.
5. To watch, select the **Open Browser** globe in the title bar. Tabs a chat opened show a robot icon, and their tooltip reads **Agent-owned tab**; tabs you opened show a globe. Select **Take over this tab** to pause the agent while you work on the page, and **Resume this tab's agent** to hand it back.



**What you get:** the answer in the chat, plus open tabs you can inspect. Bearly can open, read, screenshot, click, type, choose options, scroll, press keys, and save a download into the chat.
**Limits:** up to eight tabs across your chats. In Standard, these also ask first: **Confirm this Browser action** before a form submission or other consequential action, **Allow this visual Browser action?** for clicks on canvases and embedded frames, and **Save this website file to chat?** for downloads (one file up to 50 MiB, never opened or run). Passwords, payment details, file uploads, CAPTCHAs, and system permission prompts stay with you. Only you can decide on a certificate warning; neither an approval nor Full access can skip it. A page load Bearly started that takes more than 10 seconds is stopped.
**Combines with:** [Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md#browser-access) for scheduled site checks on exact sites; [Work locally](#work-locally) to fix a site you're building.

To point at part of a page, select **Capture and annotate page**, click an element or drag over a region, answer **What should Bearly change or inspect?**, and select **Attach** (<kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd> or <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd>). The capture goes into your message.

Two reset actions sit at the bottom of the Browser settings. **Reset open tabs** → **Reset tabs** closes tabs and clears their history and site approvals; sign-ins stay. **Delete browser data** → **Delete data** signs this account out of sites in Bearly Browser and deletes its cookies, cache, and site storage on this device.

## Control Chrome

With the Bearly Companion extension installed, a desktop chat can work in your everyday Chrome: list and switch tabs, open pages, read them and their console output, take screenshots, click, type, scroll, and, with the highest approval, inspect network activity and run DevTools commands. It uses your Chrome profile, so sites you're signed in to stay signed in.

**Use it when:** the work is in a site you're already signed in to, you want to point at a tab you have open, or you're debugging a page with the console and network log.
**You need:** the desktop app, Chrome itself (not another browser) with the Bearly Companion extension, and your organization allows **Chrome extension browser**. The extension doesn't need to be signed in to Bearly for this.



1. In the desktop app, open **App Settings** → **Desktop** → **Chrome Extension** — the pane is titled **Control Chrome from chat** and shows the connection status.
2. If it says **Chrome extension not connected**, select **Install Chrome extension** and add it to Chrome — or turn the extension back on if it's off.
3. Keep Chrome and the desktop app open until the status reads **Connected to Chrome** — pairing happens on its own, and it survives restarting either app.
4. Ask in a chat and name Chrome:

   ```text
   In Chrome, read the pull request tab I have open and list what changed and what still needs review. Don't post anything.
   ```

5. Approve the level Bearly asks for — a higher level covers the lower ones.



| Prompt | What it allows |
|---|---|
| **Read your Chrome tabs** | The tab list, page text, screenshots, console output, and page events |
| **Control Chrome** | Everything above, plus opening and closing tabs, navigating, clicking, typing, scrolling, and key presses |
| **Use Chrome DevTools** | Everything above, plus network requests and responses, storage, running JavaScript, debugging, profiling, and request interception |

**What you get:** results in the chat, from the tabs you see. While Bearly is attached to a tab, Chrome shows its own debugging banner.
**Limits:** page reads blank out password and payment-card field values, and Bearly can never open other extensions' pages. Tabs Bearly opened stay open when the chat finishes. Finishing, stopping, or closing a chat doesn't detach Bearly from your tabs; quitting the desktop app or Chrome does. Routines can't use Chrome. To use Chrome with Full access, keep the extension up to date; an older one shows "Update the Bearly browser extension to use Chrome with Full access, or switch this chat to Standard."
**Combines with:** Deep Research, by having the chat read an internal page first and then research the public web about it (see [Research](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/research.md)); [Work locally](#work-locally) for debugging with DevTools.

> **Caution — This is your real browser**
> Bearly acts in your signed-in Chrome profile and sees whatever those tabs show. At the DevTools level that includes request contents and stored session data. Page text can contain instructions written to mislead an assistant. State limits in your request, and use Bearly Browser when the task doesn't need your own sign-ins.


## Work locally

**Work locally** lets a chat read, write, and edit files and run commands on your computer. You pick a working folder; Bearly shows each action and asks before it runs.

**Use it when:** cleaning or combining a folder of spreadsheets, converting or renaming files, editing a code project, or producing a file you want saved on your computer.
**You need:** the desktop app; your organization allows **Work locally**.



1. In a desktop chat, select the **Work locally** chip next to the message box, then **Choose folder…** and pick the folder — the chip then shows the folder's name. Recent folders appear in the same menu.
2. Describe the result and what must not change:

   ```text
   Read the CSV files in this folder and make one monthly summary workbook. Don't change the source files. Add the finished workbook to this chat.
   ```

3. Review each card: it shows the command or the file. Open **Details** for the working folder, the timeout, or whether a write overwrites a file, then pick an **Approval duration**. For reads, the Allow button then reads **Allow reads for this response**, **Allow reads for now**, or **Allow reads this chat**; an approval for writes, edits, or commands also covers reads.
4. Check the result. Ask Bearly to reopen a file it made, or to run a check. Ask it to "add it to this chat" to keep a file with the conversation.
5. Select **Stop** to stop the response; a running command stops with it.



**What you get:** files changed or created on your computer, and chat files when you ask for them. The tools appear in the chat as **Shell**, **Read File**, **Write File**, **Edit File**, **Attach File** (a local file into the chat), and **Save Locally** (a chat file onto your computer).
**Limits:** a command times out after 2 minutes unless Bearly sets a longer limit, up to 10 minutes. Bearly won't start servers, watchers, or anything meant to keep running; start those yourself. **Attach File** and **Save Locally** handle files up to 50 MB. If you deny a request, Bearly doesn't retry the same one. Bearly installs the runtimes it needs for scripts the first time a task uses them; you can see them under **App Settings** → **Desktop** → **Runtime**.
**Combines with:** [Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md#unattended-local-access) with unattended local access; [skills](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/connectors-and-skills.md#skills) whose scripts run on your computer; [Pages](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md) built from what it computes.

The chip's menu also has **Clear folder** (go back to a private folder for this chat), **Off for this chat**, and **Turn on for this chat**. Without a chosen folder, Bearly works in a private folder of its own for that chat. The folder and any chat-length approvals belong to this chat on this device.

> **Caution — The folder is not a sandbox**
> The working folder is where Bearly starts, not a boundary. An approved command can reach any file your computer account can. Use a folder that holds only what the task needs, and keep backups of anything important.


## Computer Use

Computer Use lets Bearly complete a task in the apps on your screen. It takes screenshots, reads the apps' accessibility text, and clicks, types, scrolls, drags, and uses keyboard shortcuts. Computer use runs on your device; the screenshots it takes while working go to the model you picked.

**Use it when:** the task lives in a desktop app with no file or web route, such as reading figures from an open report window, changing a setting in an app, or moving data between two apps.
**You need:** the desktop app on Mac, Windows, or Linux; your organization allows **Computer Use** and at least one of the models Computer Use supports. On a Mac, Bearly needs **Accessibility** and **Screen Recording** permission in **System Settings** → **Privacy & Security**.



1. Open the app and window involved and leave it visible — there's no mode to switch on.
2. Ask for the whole task and where to stop:

   ```text
   In the spreadsheet that's open, sort the Budget tab by due date. Stop before saving.
   ```

3. At **Use your computer**, open **Details** to see the goal, the model, the data that goes to it (relevant conversation context, attachments, screenshots, and app text captured while working), and the access (the full desktop: screenshots, focus, mouse, and keyboard). Pick an **Approval duration** and allow.
4. Watch the **Computer** chip by the message box: it shows the status, such as **Looking** or **Working** in an app. Select **Stop** on the chip to end the task.
5. Read the **Computer Use** step in the answer: it lists the screen, the apps, and the actions taken.



**What you get:** the task done in the app, with a record of each action.
**Limits:** one Computer Use task at a time per chat. Bearly's own windows are never a target. Routines never see or control your screen. Before a consequential action your request didn't already authorize, such as sending, publishing, buying, deleting, or changing account or security settings, Bearly asks **Confirm this computer action**; Full access covers this one. A safety check raised during the task also shows **Confirm this computer action**, and that one always asks, even with Full access.
**Combines with:** [Run Python and charts](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code.md) after it reports what it read; [email](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/email-and-text.md) to send the result.

On a Mac, if a permission is missing, Bearly says "Computer Use needs Accessibility and Screen Recording access." Turn on **Bearly** under both in **System Settings** → **Privacy & Security**, then send the request again. On Windows, Bearly may offer **Finish setting up Computer Use** for a small helper that controls protected and background windows more reliably; select **Finish setup** and approve the administrator prompt. Its status lives at **App Settings** → **Desktop** → **Runtime** → **Windows Computer Use helper**.

If your organization's model policy blocks every model Computer Use supports, Bearly says so instead of starting.

> **Caution — Computer Use sees the whole screen**
> Close or hide anything you don't want in a screenshot before you approve the task, and stay nearby while it works.


## Website tools

Some websites publish their own tools for assistants. When the page you're on offers them, Bearly can list and run them in Bearly Browser or in Chrome. Running one asks **Run this website tool?** and shows the site, the tool, and whether the site says the tool only reads or may change data. The site controls what its tool actually does, so treat the label as the site's claim.

## Try this

### Chart a table from a dashboard you're signed in to

A chart and a CSV of the table, built from a page only your Chrome sign-in can see.

**Why it works:** Control Chrome reads the page in your own signed-in profile, so the table's text lands in the chat. Bearly saves it as a CSV file, and charts work from CSV files in the chat, so the next step can plot it or compute with Run Python.
**You need:** the desktop app with Chrome showing **Connected to Chrome**; your organization allows **Chrome extension browser** and **Charts** or **Run Python**.



1. Open the dashboard in Chrome and set the filters and date range you want — Bearly reads what the tab shows.
2. In a desktop chat, ask:

   ```text
   In Chrome, read the monthly revenue table on the analytics tab I have open. Save it as a CSV in this chat, chart revenue by month, and point out the two biggest changes. Don't click anything.
   ```

   Bearly lists your tabs and asks **Read your Chrome tabs**.
3. Choose **This response** and allow — one approval covers the tab list, the page read, and a screenshot if it takes one.
4. Check the chart against the dashboard, then ask follow-ups in the same chat; the CSV stays in the chat's files.



**Swap in:** allow **Control Chrome** and say "click Next until the last page" for paginated tables; use Bearly Browser when the site doesn't need your everyday sign-in; ask for an interactive Page with `/visualize` instead of a chart.
**If it stalls:** if the table loads as you scroll, scroll it fully into view yourself or allow **Control Chrome**. If you see "Chrome extension control could not complete that action", ask Bearly to list your Chrome tabs again.

### Turn a folder of CSV exports into a one-page summary

A combined `summary.csv` in the folder and a one-page summary in the chat, without uploading the exports one by one.

**Why it works:** Work locally runs commands in your folder, so Bearly can combine the files with command-line tools or a short script it writes and runs. What the commands print comes back into the chat, where Bearly can build a Page from it.
**You need:** the desktop app; your organization allows **Work locally**.



1. Select **Work locally** → **Choose folder…** and pick the folder of exports — the chip shows its name.
2. Ask:

   ```text
   Combine every CSV in this folder by month and region without changing the source files. Write the totals to summary.csv in this folder, then make a one-page summary with a table and the three biggest month-over-month changes, and add it to this chat.
   ```

3. For the reads, choose **This response** and select **Allow reads for this response**. When the command and the write appear, open **Details**, confirm the path is `summary.csv` and nothing is overwritten, then allow.
4. Open the summary in the chat; `summary.csv` is in your folder.



**Swap in:** ask Bearly to **Save Locally** the summary Page into the folder too; put the same request in a routine with [unattended local access](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md#unattended-local-access) to refresh it every Monday; use `/visualize` for a chart you can filter.
**If it stalls:** a command that runs past its time limit stops; ask Bearly to work one file at a time. For very large files, ask it to read only the header and the first 100 rows before deciding on a plan.

### Find and fix a bug in a site running on your computer

A fixed page, confirmed in the same browser tab Bearly used to find the problem.

**Why it works:** Bearly Browser can open `http://localhost`, so Bearly can see the bug, and Work locally can edit the project, so it can fix it. Reloading the same tab confirms the change.
**You need:** the desktop app; your organization allows **Browser** and **Work locally**; your development server already running.



1. Start your development server yourself — Bearly won't start long-running processes.
2. Select **Work locally** → **Choose folder…** and pick the project folder.
3. Ask:

   ```text
   Open http://localhost:3000/settings in Bearly Browser and find out why the Save button does nothing. Then fix it in this folder and reload the page to confirm. Don't start any servers.
   ```

4. Approve the site, then the reads and edits Bearly shows.
5. Ask for a short summary of what changed and why.



**Swap in:** use Chrome with **Use Chrome DevTools** when you need console errors and failed network requests; select **Capture and annotate page** to point at the broken element.
**If it stalls:** if the page doesn't load, check the server is still running. If Bearly proposes starting the server, deny it and start it yourself.

### Read figures from a desktop app and chart them

A chart of numbers that only exist in a native app's window.

**Why it works:** Computer Use reads what's on screen and reports it back to the chat. Computer Use can't run Python itself, but the chat can, so the next step charts the figures it returned.
**You need:** the desktop app; your organization allows **Computer Use** and **Run Python** or **Charts**; on a Mac, Accessibility and Screen Recording permission.



1. Open the app and the report window with the figures.
2. Ask:

   ```text
   In the accounting app that's open, read the twelve monthly totals from the Reports window. Don't change anything. Then chart them and tell me which months stand out.
   ```

3. At **Use your computer**, check **Details** and allow **Once** — the task is short.
4. Watch the **Computer** chip; select **Stop** if it heads somewhere unexpected.



**Swap in:** if the app can export a CSV, use Work locally on the export instead; it's faster and exact. Add "then email me the chart" to send it (see [Send email and texts](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/email-and-text.md)).
**If it stalls:** bring the window into view and ask Bearly to continue. On a Mac, grant the permissions Bearly names and resend.

## Related

- [Approvals, permissions, and Full access](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/permissions.md) — every approval card and what Full access covers.
- [Automate with Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md) — what a scheduled run can do in Bearly Browser and on your computer.
- [Bearly Browser](https://bearly.ai/docs/features/browser.md), [Control Chrome](https://bearly.ai/docs/features/chrome-control.md), [Work locally](https://bearly.ai/docs/features/work-locally.md), and [Computer Use](https://bearly.ai/docs/features/computer-use.md) in the feature guides.

# Connect apps and add skills

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/connectors-and-skills/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/connectors-and-skills/)

> How to connect services and your own MCP servers to Bearly, turn a connector on for a chat or a routine, sign in on each device, and install and run skills, including the built-in Visualize and Bearly AI Models skills, with platform limits and approvals.

Connectors give a chat another app's own tools: find a page in your workspace, read an issue, check a deployment. Skills give Bearly a reusable method for a kind of work, with instructions and, sometimes, scripts it can run on your computer. Both are set up once in **App Settings** and then used by name in chats and routines.

## Connectors

A connector is an MCP server: a service that offers tools Bearly can call. Bearly comes with presets for common services, and you can add any server you trust.

**Use it when:** you want answers from your own work apps, such as open issues, recent deployments, or a workspace page, or you want Bearly to make a change there that you've asked for.
**You need:** your organization allows **MCP Connectors**; to add your own servers, it also allows **Personal MCP servers**. Connectors that need a service sign-in can't be connected in the iOS app (see [Where connectors work](#where-connectors-work)).

A connector goes through three states:

- **Installed** — it's in your list under **Your connectors**, but not signed in or checked yet.
- **Connected** — Bearly reached it; a green dot appears by its name.
- **Active** — you turned it on in a chat. A connected connector does nothing in a chat until you do.

### Add a built-in connector

The **Built-in connectors** list offers Notion, Linear, Calendly, Vercel, Cloudflare, GitHub, Stripe, Webflow, Atlassian, Sentry, and Intercom.



1. Open the account menu at the bottom of the sidebar, select **App Settings**, then **Connectors**.
2. Under **Built-in connectors**, select **Add** beside the service — it moves to **Your connectors**, not yet signed in.
3. Select **Connect** and finish the service's own sign-in in the window that opens — read the permissions it asks for before approving.
4. Back in Bearly, the green dot shows it's connected.



### Add your own MCP server



1. Open **App Settings** → **Connectors** and select **Add custom MCP server**.
2. Choose a **Transport**: **HTTPS** for a server on the web, or **npm package** to run a package on your computer (desktop app only). Give it a **Name**.
3. For HTTPS, enter the **Server URL** (a Streamable HTTP server over HTTPS) and pick **Authentication**: **OAuth (automatic)** for servers with MCP sign-in (optionally with **OAuth scopes**), **Bearer token**, **Custom headers**, or **No authentication**. For npm, enter the **npm package**, any **Arguments**, and **Environment variables**.
4. Select **Add and check** — Bearly saves the server, contacts it, and lists the tools it found. If it can't connect, the reason appears under the row.



Tokens and header values you enter are stored in this Bearly profile on this device. An npm server runs on your computer as you, so it can reach your files.

> **Caution — Only add servers you trust**
> A remote server sees what each tool call sends it and can return instructions along with its results. An npm package runs on your computer with your access. Add a server because you know who publishes it.


### Turn a connector on in a chat



1. In the chat, select **Connectors** next to the message box — rows show **Active**, **Off**, **Connect**, **Retry**, or **Web browser required**.
2. Select a connected service to make it **Active** — the chip then shows its name, or the number of active connectors.
3. Ask for a specific result and say what must not change:

   ```text
   Using Linear, list the issues assigned to me that changed this week, grouped by project. Don't edit anything.
   ```

4. At **Allow Linear?** (the card names your connector), pick an **Approval duration** and allow — the card warns that "This connected server controls what its tools do." One approval covers that server's tools, including ones that change data.



**What you get:** answers built from the service's own data, and changes made there when you ask for them. Bearly can do only what the server's tools and your account in that service allow.
**Limits:** a tool image over 20 MB is rejected. Approvals reset when you disconnect, sign in again, or change the server's settings. A server's own "read-only" labels don't limit an approval.
**Combines with:** [Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md#tools-and-skills) for scheduled digests; [Pages](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md) and `/visualize` to turn results into a page; [email](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/email-and-text.md) to send them.

You can also ask Bearly to find and connect a service for you, in a typed chat or a voice call: "connect Linear and show my open issues". Bearly asks with a card such as **Connect Linear?**, and that card always asks, even in Full access. If the service needs a sign-in, Bearly then shows **Continue sign-in**.

### Sign in on each device

A connection belongs to the Bearly profile where you set it up: this browser, the desktop app, or the iOS app. It isn't copied to your other devices. Connect the service again on each one you use. Routines use the connections on the device that runs them; if a selected connector isn't connected there, the run says so.

**Disconnect** drops the sign-in but keeps the connector in your list. The pencil (**Edit**) changes a server you added. The **Remove** X takes it out of your list. None of these delete anything in the service itself; to revoke Bearly's access there, use the service's own connected-apps settings.

### Servers from your organization

An organization administrator can add servers to a policy under **Team MCP servers**. They appear in your list with **Managed by your team policy**. You still sign in with your own account: select **Connect**, or the pencil (**Set up authentication**) to enter your own token or header value.

### Where connectors work

| Where | Servers with a token, headers, or no sign-in | Servers with a service sign-in | npm servers |
|---|---|---|---|
| Desktop app | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Web app and phone browsers | Yes | Yes | No |
| iOS app | Yes | No, shows **Web browser required** | No |
| A browser opened inside another app on Android | Yes | No | No |

npm servers off the desktop app show **Desktop required** and "npm MCP servers require the Bearly desktop app."

## Skills

A skill is a folder with an instruction file and, often, supporting files and scripts. When you ask for it, or when your request clearly matches what it's for, Bearly opens the skill and follows its method. Scripts in a skill run on your computer, with your approval.

**Use it when:** you want the same method every time, such as a house style for documents, a review checklist, a report built the same way each week, or a workflow your team shares.
**You need:** the desktop app to install skills and run their scripts; your organization allows **Skills**, and **Add Skills** to install them. Built-in skills work on every platform.

### Built-in skills

- **Visualize** — type `/visualize` and describe what you want to see. It turns an idea, layout, process, or dataset into a focused visual: an interactive planner or simulation when controls help, and a generated picture only when you ask for one.
- **Bearly AI Models** — lets scripts Bearly writes call Bearly's models with a short-lived token: chat models, image generation, document text recognition, web search, HTML-to-PDF, and audio transcription. In Work locally and skill scripts, the token is supplied to each run automatically and lasts about five minutes. See the recipe [Classify a folder of files with Bearly's models](#classify-a-folder-of-files-with-bearlys-models).

### Install a skill



1. In the desktop app, open **App Settings** → **Skills** — the **Skill Store** opens.
2. Under **Skill repositories**, expand a repository to see its skills, and select **Install** beside one.
3. Read **Install third-party skill?** — it shows the source and warns that a skill "can include instructions and scripts. It may be wrong or malicious, and local execution can modify or delete data". Select **I understand the risks** only for a source you trust.
4. The skill appears under **Installed** as **Enabled**.



To install from somewhere else, use **Install from source**: **Git** with **Add Repository**, an HTTPS **URL ZIP** with its **SHA-256** and **Install**, or a **Folder** with **Import Folder** (copies it) or **Link Folder** (uses it in place). Under **Installed**, **Pause** hides a skill from chats, **Enable** brings it back, **Uninstall** removes a copied skill, and **Unlink** disconnects a linked folder without deleting it.

If your organization limits who adds skills, the pane says "Admins can add and remove Skills. You can browse repositories and pause Skills already available here."

### Use a skill in a chat



1. Type `/skill` in the message box — enabled skills appear in the list, each as `/skill-` followed by its name.
2. Pick one and describe the job — Bearly opens that skill before answering.
3. If the skill needs a script, a card titled with the skill and the script's path appears, showing the command. Open **Details** for the file, runtime, any packages it installs, and the timeout. Pick an **Approval duration** and allow, or **Deny**.



**What you get:** work done the skill's way. A longer approval is saved as "Run" plus the skill's name plus "scripts" and covers that skill's scripts.
**Limits:** scripts run as you on your computer; it isn't a sandbox. Installed skills and their files are on this desktop only; on the web and phones, chats that used one stay readable, but Bearly can't open its files there.
**Combines with:** [Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md#tools-and-skills), where a skill's **Allow scripts** switch lets its scripts run unattended; [Work locally](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/browser-and-computer.md#work-locally); [Projects](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects.md), where owners and writers can attach skills to every chat in the project.

Your organization can also turn off **Skill scripts** separately from **Work locally**.

## Try this

### Get a weekly issue digest by email

Every Friday afternoon, an email listing what closed and what's still open in your tracker.

**Why it works:** a routine pins the connectors you pick, with **Read-only** keeping the run to tools that only look things up. The run's answer becomes the email, because **Email each result** sends each finished result to you.
**You need:** Pro or higher; the desktop app open on the device that runs the routine; Linear (or another tracker) connected on that device; your organization allows **MCP Connectors**, **Routines**, and **Email notifications**.



1. Connect Linear in **App Settings** → **Connectors** in the desktop app — routines use that device's connections.
2. Open **Routines** and type into **Create a routine**, then select **Draft routine**:

   ```text
   Every Friday at 4 PM, list the Linear issues closed this week and the ones still open in the current cycle, grouped by project, with owners.
   ```

3. In the editor, open **Tools and skills** → **Plugin** → **Connectors**, pick Linear, and set its **Tools** to **Read-only**.
4. Turn on **Email each result** and select **Create routine**. To see the first digest now, open the routine again from **Your routines** and select **Run now**.



**Swap in:** GitHub instead of Linear for pull requests; **Text each result** for a two-line summary instead of an email; a **Notification filter** such as "only if something is overdue".
**If it stalls:** if the run says the connector isn't connected, connect it on the device shown in **Run location**. If **Read-only** leaves no tools for the job, set **Tools** to **All** and keep "don't change anything" in the instructions.

### Turn open pull requests into a status page

A one-page view of open pull requests, grouped by reviewer and age, that you can share.

**Why it works:** the connector returns the pull requests as data in the chat, and `/visualize` turns data already in the chat into a Page.
**You need:** GitHub connected and turned on in the chat; your organization allows **MCP Connectors**.



1. Select **Connectors** by the message box and make GitHub **Active**.
2. Ask:

   ```text
   Using GitHub, list the open pull requests in our main repository with author, reviewers, and days open. Don't comment or change anything.
   ```

   Approve **Allow GitHub?** for **This response**.
3. Type `/visualize` and ask:

   ```text
   Make a status page from these pull requests: group them by reviewer, flag anything open more than 7 days, and add a filter by author.
   ```

4. Open the Page in the chat, and publish it if you want a link (see [Pages and canvas](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md)).



**Swap in:** Sentry issues or Vercel deployments instead of pull requests; email the page to yourself afterward.
**If it stalls:** the connector's tools decide what Bearly can list. If it can't filter the way you asked, ask for everything first and filter in the next turn.

### Classify a folder of files with Bearly's models

A `labels.csv` that tags hundreds of text files, made by a script that calls Bearly's models for each one.

**Why it works:** Work locally runs a script Bearly writes in your folder, and the built-in Bearly AI Models skill gives that script a short-lived token for Bearly's models, so each file gets its own model call. You don't create or paste an API key, and usage counts on your Bearly account.
**You need:** the desktop app; your organization allows **Work locally** and **Skills**.



1. Select **Work locally** → **Choose folder…** and pick the folder of files.
2. Ask:

   ```text
   Use the Bearly AI Models skill. Write a script that reads every .txt file in this folder, asks a fast model to label each as bug, billing, or how-to, and writes filename and label to labels.csv. Try it on 5 files first, show me the results, then run it on the rest.
   ```

3. Review the script and the command in **Details**, then allow — the trial run on 5 files shows the labels before the full run.
4. Open `labels.csv`, or ask Bearly to add it to the chat.



**Swap in:** summaries instead of labels; image generation or transcription for a folder of prompts or recordings; a [routine with unattended local access](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md#unattended-local-access) to label new files every morning.
**If it stalls:** a script that runs longer than its time limit stops; ask Bearly to process the files in batches. If calls start failing after a few minutes, the token expired; ask Bearly to get a fresh one and continue.

## Related

- [Approvals, permissions, and Full access](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/permissions.md) — what each connector and script approval covers.
- [Use Bearly from scripts and agents](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/api-and-agents.md) — API keys for code that runs outside Bearly.
- [Connectors](https://bearly.ai/docs/features/connectors.md) and [Skills](https://bearly.ai/docs/features/skills.md) in the feature guides.

title: "Automate with Routines"
description: "How to create, schedule, equip, and deliver Bearly routines, including the Pro requirement, where routines run, exactly which tools a scheduled run can use, connectors, skills, Deep Research, permission mode, unattended local access, browser access, the notification filter, email and text delivery, and run history."
canonical: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines/"
