---

# Automate with Routines

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines/)

> How to create, schedule, equip, and deliver Bearly routines, including the Pro requirement, where routines run, exactly which tools a scheduled run can use, connectors, skills, Deep Research, permission mode, unattended local access, browser access, the notification filter, email and text delivery, and run history.

A routine is a prompt Bearly runs for you on a schedule: a weekday news brief, a Friday roundup, a month-end recap. Each routine has its own chat, and every run is added to it. You can also have each result emailed or texted to you, or only the results that clear a bar you set.

**You need:** Pro or higher to create, edit, or run routines; the desktop app, open, on the device that runs them; your organization allows **Routines**. Below Pro, the Routines page shows "Routines are a Pro feature." with **Go Pro**, and existing routines are read-only.

> **Note — Routines run in the desktop app**
> Routines run in the desktop app, on the one device they're installed on, while the app is open. You can create and edit them on the web, a phone, or the iOS app; install them from the desktop app to run them. See [Run location](#run-location-run-now-and-recent-runs).


## How routines work

- **One chat per routine.** Every run is added to the routine's chat and marks it unread. While the app is open, a **Routine ran** notice with **Open** appears.
- **Synced everywhere, run in one place.** Your routines appear on every device you sign in on, but only the installed device runs them.
- **Schedules.** Each routine runs at one time on its scheduled days: **Daily**, **Weekdays**, **Weekends**, **Weekly** on the days you pick, or **Monthly** on a day from the 1st to the 28th. Times use your current timezone. For several checks a day, make several routines.
- **Up to 10 active routines.** Paused routines don't count.
- **One run at a time per device.** Routines due at the same time run one after another.
- **Up to 25 minutes per run.** A run that takes longer is stopped.
- **Catch-up.** If the app was closed or the computer was asleep at the scheduled time, the routine runs once for the most recent missed time when the app is back. Older missed times are recorded as "Skipped — device was away".
- **Auto-pause.** After two failed runs in a row, the routine pauses and shows **Routine paused**: "failed twice in a row. Open Routines to resume it."
- **Encryption.** A routine's chat uses Bearly's standard encryption, not end-to-end encryption, even if you encrypt your new chats end-to-end. An end-to-end encrypted chat can't be a routine's chat.

## Create a routine

There's no slash command for routines. Start one in any of three ways.



1. Open **Routines** in the sidebar, or press <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>K</kbd> (<kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>K</kbd> on Windows and Linux) and choose **New Routine**.
2. In **Create a routine**, describe the result and when you want it, then select **Draft routine** or press <kbd>Enter</kbd> — Bearly drafts the name, instructions, schedule, and, if you wrote "only tell me if…", a notification filter. The **Try** chips show examples. **Start from scratch** opens an empty editor instead.

   ```text
   Send me a concise AI news brief every weekday at 8 AM. Only tell me if there's a major model release.
   ```

3. Review the draft under **New routine** and change anything that's off — if drafting fails, the editor still opens with your text as the instructions and a weekday 8 AM schedule.
4. Select **Create routine** — the routine gets its own chat, and if you're in the desktop app, it's installed on this device. To see a first result without waiting for the schedule, open the routine again from **Your routines** and select **Run now**.



You can also ask in any chat, including the chat that just did the job well:

```text
Do this every weekday at 8 and email me the result.
```

Bearly sets up the routine and shows a card with its name, schedule, and instructions, plus which device runs it or a note to open the desktop app to choose one. It asks one short question when the task or the time is unclear. It doesn't turn on email, texts, connectors, skills, scripts, conditions, or paid research unless you ask for them. It turns on unattended local access or browser access only when the task clearly needs them, and then only through a warning card you approve. It can't turn on Full access; that happens only in the editor.

## The editor

Open a routine from **Your routines** to edit it. Changes take effect when you select **Save**.

### Routine

- **Name** — how the routine appears in lists and notices.
- **Instructions** — what to do each time. Make the first run self-contained; later runs can see earlier ones if **Build on previous runs** is on.
- **Model** — the model that runs the routine. It starts on Bearly's default for your plan, and the list follows your plan and your organization's model policy. See [Choose and compare models](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/models.md).

### Schedule

Pick the cadence, the days, and the time. The preview line shows the next run times and your timezone, such as "Next: Mon Oct 27 · 8:00 AM, then Tue, Wed".

### Tools and skills

Give each routine only what it needs. Under **Tools**, select **Plugin**:

- **Bearly** → **Deep Research** ("Uses plan allowance") — each run does one full Deep Research report on the instructions and emails you the PDF. Choose the **Tier**: **Standard** needs the Analyst plan or higher, **Ultra** needs Research. The plan is checked when the run starts. See [Research](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/research.md).
- **Connectors** — pick connected services. Each has a **Tools** setting: **All**, or **Read-only** to keep the run to tools that only look things up. Selected connectors are used without asking during runs. Connect them on the device that runs the routine; a run notes when one isn't connected there.
- **Skills** — pick installed skills. A skill with scripts has **Allow scripts** ("Runs this skill's scripts unattended, with your Bearly token."). Leave it off unless you trust the skill to run without you.
- **Manage connectors…** and **Manage skills…** open their settings. See [Connect apps and add skills](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/connectors-and-skills.md).

### What a scheduled run can use

A run works with nobody watching, so it gets a fixed, smaller set of tools than a chat. Your organization's policy still applies to each one.

| Always available in runs | Only when you turn it on for the routine |
|---|---|
| Web search, image search, and reading web pages | Connectors you select |
| Reading public posts on X | Skills you select; their scripts only with **Allow scripts** or unattended local access |
| Scholar paper search | Deep Research, through the plugin |
| Reading and searching the routine chat's files | Local files and commands, with **Unattended local access** |
| Writing files, including CSV files and HTML Pages | Bearly Browser on listed sites, with **Browser access** |
| Sending you email and texts | |

Never available in runs: Computer Use, Chrome, Run Python (the cloud interpreter) and the document editor that uses it, image generation, video generation, transcription, text to speech, voice, Council, watching and cutting videos, Best Pick, People Search, maps, weather, SEO data, domain research, chart authoring, canvas, prompt templates, memory recall, chat management, video downloads, grammar fixes, Browse Files, and remote modules.

To get numbers, tables, or visuals from a routine, ask it to write a CSV file or an HTML Page, then open the routine's chat and keep going there: it's an ordinary chat with every tool you normally have. With unattended local access, a run can also compute with commands on your computer.

### Permission mode

**Permission mode** sets how a routine's selected tools get approval while you're away.

- **Standard** — the default. Of the tools that would ask for approval in a chat, a run can use only the ones you turned on for this routine: selected connectors, skill scripts you allowed, local files and commands with unattended local access, and Bearly Browser within its approved sites. Anything else that needs approval is denied, and the run continues without it.
- **Full access** — "Selected tools can act without additional approval" on the device that runs the routine, "including scheduled runs while you're away. This stays on across app restarts."



1. Create the routine in Standard and save it.
2. On the desktop device shown in **Run location**, open it and select **Enable full access** under **Permission mode**.
3. Review the tools it lists and confirm — enabling it stops any current run and leaves the schedule unchanged.



Full access in a routine doesn't add Computer Use, Chrome, or DevTools, and never widens the sites or actions in browser access. Saving a material change, such as the instructions, schedule, model, delivery, or tools, sets the routine to **Full access needs review**; select **Review full access**, or **Use Standard**. Copied and moved routines start in Standard. Full access in a chat never carries over to a routine. See [Approvals, permissions, and Full access](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/permissions.md).

### Unattended local access

**Unattended local access** "Lets scheduled runs use Local Exec and approved skill scripts without waiting for you." Local Exec is Work locally's tools: shell commands, and reading, writing, and editing files.



1. In the desktop app, turn on **Unattended local access** in the routine's **Tools** section.
2. Read **Make sure you know what this allows** — it lists every risk.
3. Select **Allow unattended access**, then **Save** (or **Create routine** for a new one).



The risks it lists: the routine "can permanently delete production data if this machine has access to it", "can permanently delete or overwrite your files", can be steered by "prompt injection hidden in websites, documents, email, or connector data", and "can expose credentials, secrets, and private files to the model or this chat." The safer setup: "Use a dedicated working folder with the least access possible, keep current backups, and do not enable this on a machine with production access unless you accept that risk."

If you ask a chat to turn this on, or to rewrite the instructions of a routine that has it, the same warning appears in the chat as a card you must approve before anything is saved. You can't approve it by voice.

> **Danger — Unattended means no one checks each command**
> With this on, a run can run commands and change or delete any file your computer account can reach, without asking. Use it only when the routine needs local work, and test with **Run now** first.


### Browser access

**Browser access** "Lets scheduled runs use a browser on the sites you list while you're away." Routines use Bearly Browser only; they can't use your Chrome.

| Switch or field | What it does |
|---|---|
| **Bearly Browser** | Turns browser access on. "Uses the sign-ins you imported into Bearly Browser. The Browser panel stays closed." |
| **Sites** | "Only these exact sites. A subdomain, like pay.example.com, is a separate site." Type a site and select **Add**; `www.` is optional. Sites named in your instructions appear under **From the instructions:** for one-click adding. Up to 20 sites. |
| **Click, type, and submit** | "Click buttons, type, choose options, and submit forms on these sites. When off, it only reads pages and follows links." |
| **Download files** | "Save files from these sites into this routine's chat." Available only with **Click, type, and submit** on. |



1. Sign in to the sites in Bearly Browser on the device that runs the routine, or import your sign-ins under **App Settings** → **Desktop** → **Browser** → **Import browser cookies** — a run never types into password or payment fields.
2. In the routine's **Tools** section, turn on **Bearly Browser** under **Browser access** and add the sites.
3. Leave **Click, type, and submit** off unless the routine has to change something.
4. Select **Save** (or **Create routine** for a new one), read **Make sure you know what this allows** (it lists what's new since you last approved), and select **Allow browser access and save**.
5. Open the saved routine, select **Run now**, and check the result before relying on the schedule.



**What a run can do there:** open up to 3 tabs of its own, in the background, closed when the run ends; read pages and follow links on the listed sites; with **Click, type, and submit**, take the actions its instructions ask for, including submitting forms, without asking again.
**Limits:** the desktop app stops any request to another site before it's sent, including link clicks, redirects, pop-ups, and form posts. A run never touches password or payment fields, never uploads files, closes a tab with an invalid certificate, and stops at a CAPTCHA. The approval is tied to the instructions: if the instructions or these settings change without a new approval, runs go without a browser until you save and approve again. Narrowing access saves without asking.
**You also need:** the desktop app; **Enable Browser** and **Allow chats to use Browser** on in **App Settings** → **Desktop** → **Browser**; your organization allows **Routines**, **Browser in Routines**, and **Browser**.

The approval warns that on these sites the routine "acts as you", can be tricked by "instructions hidden in a web page, email, or document", and "can read private pages on these sites and copy what it finds into this routine's chat, email, or texts."

When a run couldn't use the browser, its note says why, for example: "It stopped at … because that site isn't approved for this routine. To allow more, edit the routine's browser access." or "Bearly Browser is off in this device's settings, so this run didn't use it."

### Results and delivery

- **Notification filter** — "Every run stays in its chat. Add a condition when you only want alerts for meaningful changes," such as "a competitor ships something new". Each run still happens and lands in the chat. When the result doesn't meet your bar, the run stays quiet: no unread mark, no notice, no email, no text. **Recent runs** marks it **quiet** and says why it stayed quiet. Bearly compares each result with the previous one, and delivers whenever it's unsure, when a tool failed, or when the result is empty. Every seventh quiet run in a row checks in anyway, so silence never means a broken routine.
- **Build on previous runs** — on by default for new routines. Each run sees the earlier runs in the chat, so instructions like "tell me what changed since yesterday" work. Turn it off when every run should start fresh.
- **Email each result** — "Send the completed result to your Bearly account email." Only to your own address. Files the run created, such as a Page or a CSV, can be attached; attachments are limited to 20 MB in total.
- **Text each result** — "Send a short AI summary to your verified phone number." Needs a verified US or Canadian mobile number in **App Settings** → **Phone & SMS**; turning it on without one opens that pane. Routine texts are capped at 3 per 24 hours across all your routines, arrive at the scheduled time, and leave end-to-end encryption, as the switch notes. See [Send email and texts](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/email-and-text.md).

### Run location, Run now, and recent runs

**Run location** appears once the routine is saved and shows "Runs on this device.", another device's name, or "Not installed anywhere — it won't run." A routine you create in the desktop app is installed on that device. In the desktop app, **Install on this device** claims a routine created elsewhere, and **Move to this device** takes one over from another computer. On the web and phones it reads "Runs in the desktop app." with **Get it**.

**Run now**, in the desktop app once the routine is saved, runs the last saved version right away on the device you're using, even if another device owns the schedule. While a run is going, the button becomes **Stop run**, which stops that run and keeps the schedule.

**Recent runs** lists each run as "Ran on schedule", "Catch-up run", or "Manual run", with skips and failures and why, and **Open run** to jump to that result in the chat. Bearly keeps the last 30 runs of each routine.

## Manage your routines

The Routines page lists **Your routines** and a **Next seven days** strip of what your active routines will do next. Each routine's menu has **Run now** (or **Stop run**), **Open chat**, **Pause** or **Resume**, **Install on this device** or **Move to this device**, and **Delete**. Badges show **Paused**, **Failing**, **Not installed**, and **Overdue**.

- **Pause** stops the schedule and keeps the settings and history. **Resume** starts it again.
- **Delete** asks to confirm: "The schedule stops everywhere. Its chat and past runs stay in your history." Select **Delete routine**.
- If you delete a routine's chat, its next run starts a new chat with the routine's name. If that fails, the routine pauses with "Paused — its chat was deleted."

A skipped run says why: "Skipped — previous run was still going", "Skipped — device was away", "Skipped — another device took this routine over", "Skipped — the chat is locked on this device", "Skipped — the chat was deleted", "Skipped — turned off by your team's policy", "Skipped — plan no longer covers routines", "Skipped — device was offline", or "Skipped — signed out".

## Try this

### Get a weekday page of what changed on two sites you're signed in to

Every weekday morning, an email with a one-page summary of yesterday's numbers from two sites that need your sign-in.

**Why it works:** browser access lets the run read signed-in pages in Bearly Browser on exactly the sites you list. Runs can write HTML Pages, and the email a run sends can attach files the run created, so asking for the Page by email puts it in your inbox. **Build on previous runs** gives each run yesterday's result to compare against.
**You need:** Pro or higher; the desktop app open on the installed device; signed in to both sites in Bearly Browser; your organization allows **Routines**, **Browser in Routines**, **Browser**, and **Email notifications**.



1. In the desktop app, sign in to both sites in Bearly Browser, or import your sign-ins under **Import browser cookies**.
2. On the Routines page, draft it:

   ```text
   Every weekday at 7:30 AM, open dashboard.example.com and partners.example.com, collect yesterday's signups and open support tickets, and build a one-page HTML summary comparing them with the previous run. Email it to me.
   ```

3. In **Browser access**, turn on **Bearly Browser** and add both sites from **From the instructions:** — leave **Click, type, and submit** off, since it only reads.
4. Turn on **Email each result**, select **Create routine**, and approve **Allow browser access and save**.
5. Open the routine again from **Your routines**, select **Run now**, and open the email.



**Swap in:** **Text each result** for a two-line summary instead of the Page; a **Notification filter** like "only if signups dropped more than 20%"; a connector instead of a site when the service has one.
**If it stalls:** if a note says a site isn't approved, add the exact host it stopped at, such as a separate sign-in subdomain. If the page asks you to sign in, sign in again in Bearly Browser on that device.

### Get a text only when something changes

A text when an order ships or a status page changes, and silence the rest of the time.

**Why it works:** the **Notification filter** lets each run decide whether the result meets your bar, compared with the previous run, and the text goes out only when it does. Each run still lands in the chat, so you can look any time.
**You need:** Pro or higher; the desktop app open on the installed device; a verified US or Canadian mobile number; your organization allows **Routines** and **Text messages**, plus **Browser in Routines** and **Browser** for a signed-in page.



1. Verify your phone in **App Settings** → **Phone & SMS** if you haven't.
2. Draft the routine:

   ```text
   Every weekday at 9 AM, check my order status page on store.example.com. Only tell me if anything shipped or was delayed.
   ```

   "Only tell me if…" becomes the **Notification filter**.
3. Add `store.example.com` under **Browser access** if the page needs your sign-in, turn on **Text each result**, keep **Build on previous runs** on, and select **Create routine**.
4. Open the routine again and select **Run now**; **Recent runs** shows whether it ran and delivered, or stayed **quiet** and why.



**Swap in:** a public page with no browser access; **Email each result** instead of a text; several routines at different times for more checks a day.
**If it stalls:** texts stop at 3 per 24 hours across all routines, so a chatty filter can use them up; tighten the condition.

### Get a research report every Monday

A full Deep Research report on a topic you follow, as a PDF in your inbox each week.

**Why it works:** the **Deep Research** plugin lets a run do one paid Deep Research report on the instructions, and the run emails you the PDF.
**You need:** the Analyst plan or higher for **Standard**, Research for **Ultra**; the desktop app open on the installed device; your organization allows **Routines** and **Deep Research**.



1. Draft the routine:

   ```text
   Every Monday at 8 AM, research new regulations affecting telehealth in the US from the past week and what they mean for small clinics.
   ```

2. Select **Plugin** → **Deep Research** and choose the **Tier** — "Each run uses your plan allowance and emails you the PDF report."
3. Select **Create routine**, then open the routine again and select **Run now** to check the first report.



**Swap in:** a weekly competitor scan; add a connector so the run checks your own tracker against the findings.
**If it stalls:** a plan below the tier's requirement stops the research with an upgrade message in the chat. Each run uses your allowance, so weigh that before a daily schedule.

### Turn a chat that worked into a routine

A routine that repeats a job you just did by hand, with the same instructions.

**Why it works:** the chat already holds the instructions that worked, and Bearly can save them as a routine from that same chat.
**You need:** Pro or higher; your organization allows **Routines**; the desktop app to run it.



1. In the chat that did the job, ask:

   ```text
   Do this every weekday at 8 AM and email me the result.
   ```

2. Read the card Bearly shows — it gives the schedule and instructions, and says which device runs the routine or that you need to open the desktop app to choose one.
3. In the desktop app, open **Routines**, open the new routine, and select **Install on this device** if it shows **Not installed**.
4. Select **Run now** to confirm the instructions work without the chat's earlier context.



**Swap in:** "only tell me if…" to add a filter; "text me" instead of email.
**If it stalls:** if the first run misses context the chat had, edit **Instructions** so the job is self-contained.

## Related

- [Approvals, permissions, and Full access](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/permissions.md) — how routine approvals differ from chat approvals.
- [Let Bearly use a browser, your files, and your computer](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/browser-and-computer.md) — Bearly Browser and Work locally in chats.
- [Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/features/routines.md) in the feature guides.

---

---
title: "Send email and texts"
description: "How Bearly emails you or your teammates and texts your verified phone from a chat or a routine, how to set up Phone & SMS, who can receive messages, attachment and rate limits, and what to do when a send fails."
canonical: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/email-and-text/"
markdown: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/email-and-text.md"
---

# Send email and texts

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/email-and-text/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/email-and-text/)

> How Bearly emails you or your teammates and texts your verified phone from a chat or a routine, how to set up Phone & SMS, who can receive messages, attachment and rate limits, and what to do when a send fails.

Ask Bearly to email or text you from any chat, and it sends the message as part of its answer. Email can go to you or to teammates in your organization, with files from the chat attached. Texts go only to your own verified phone. Routines use the same tools to deliver their results.

**You need:** your organization allows **Email notifications** and **Text messages**. Both work in the web app, phone browsers, the desktop app, and the iOS app.

There's no approval card for these: asking is the go-ahead. Bearly sends when that step of the answer runs, and a sent message can't be edited or recalled from Bearly. Check the recipient and the contents in your request.

## Send Email

**Send Email** writes a formatted email and sends it to your account address, or to teammates you name.

**Use it when:** you want a summary, checklist, or file from a chat in your inbox; you want to hand action items to colleagues; or a routine should deliver its result.
**You need:** to email teammates, a managed organization whose members share your work email domain.



1. Get the content ready in the chat first: the summary, the draft, or the files to attach — Bearly can attach files that are in the chat, including Pages and files it created.
2. Ask for the email, naming the recipient, subject, and attachments. Typing `/email` and choosing **Send Email** makes the intent explicit, but a plain request works too:

   ```text
   Email me the decision summary from this chat with the subject "Launch review". Attach the budget CSV.
   ```

3. Check the **Send Email** step in the answer — it reads "Email sent to" followed by the recipient (or the number of recipients) and the subject.
4. Open the email to check the formatting and attachments.



**What you get:** an email with Markdown formatting (headings, lists, tables, links) and up to 10 attachments from the chat. It comes from "User message via Bearly", and the subject starts with "[User message via Bearly from" followed by your name. Replies go to your address.
**Limits:** each attachment and all attachments together can total at most 20 MB. Up to 25 recipients per email, and up to 20 emails an hour from your account. If every attachment fails to prepare, nothing is sent.
**Combines with:** [Pages](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md) and data files to attach; [Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md#results-and-delivery) with **Email each result**; [Meetings](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/meetings.md) for follow-ups.

### Who can receive your email

- **You.** With no recipient named, Bearly sends to your account email.
- **Teammates.** On a managed organization, Bearly uses **Find Contacts** to search your organization's members by name or email (give it at least two characters), then sends only to exact matches. It can't email outside addresses. When you email teammates, you're copied and replies come to you.
- If your organization's administrator turned off the member directory for members, you can email only yourself.

```text
Email Maya Chen and Sam Ortiz their action items from this meeting, one list each, subject "Thursday follow-ups". Keep it under 150 words.
```

## Send SMS

**Send SMS** texts a short, plain-text message to the verified phone on your account. Bearly can't text any other number, and it won't take a number you type in the chat.

**Use it when:** you want a result on your phone when you're away from your desk, or a routine should ping you only when something matters.
**You need:** a verified US or Canadian mobile number in **Phone & SMS**.



1. Ask for the text, or type `/sms` and choose **Send SMS**:

   ```text
   Text me the three launch decisions from this chat. Keep it under 250 characters.
   ```

2. If your phone isn't ready, the chat shows a **Set up text messages** card; select **Open Phone & SMS** and finish setup (below), then ask Bearly to send it again.
3. The **Send SMS** step reports the text was sent to your verified phone. Check that it arrived before asking again, so you don't get a duplicate.



**What you get:** a plain-text message, with no formatting or attachments.
**Limits:** US and Canadian numbers only. Texts you ask for in chats are limited to 20 a day. Texts from routines are capped at 3 per 24 hours across all your routines. Long messages can be rejected; ask for a shorter one without emoji.
**Combines with:** [Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md#results-and-delivery) with **Text each result** and a notification filter; any long task you want to hear about when it's done.

### Set up Phone & SMS



1. Open **App Settings** → **Phone & SMS**. **Set up text messages** reads "Add a US or Canadian mobile number for verification codes and notifications."
2. Choose **US +1** or **Canada +1**, enter your **Phone number**, read the texting terms, and select **Agree & send code**.
3. Under **Check your phone**, enter the code Bearly texted and select **Verify code** — the pane shows **Phone verified** and a confirmation text arrives.



Once verified, the pane reads **Texts are on** with your masked number. **Send test text** checks delivery, **Change number** verifies a different one, **Turn off text messages** "Stops codes and notifications. Same as replying STOP.", and **Remove phone number** "Deletes the number from your Bearly account." After you turn texts off, **Turn texts back on** verifies your number again.

## When a send fails

| What you see | What to do |
|---|---|
| The **Send Email** or **Send SMS** step reports a failure | Read the message in the step. Retry once; if it fails again, wait a few minutes. |
| The step's error mentions "managed-domain team" or "not an eligible team contact" | Bearly can email only exact members of your managed organization. Check the name, or send it to yourself and forward it. |
| The step's error mentions "total attachment size" | Keep attachments under 20 MB in total. Ask for a smaller export, or split it across emails. |
| The step's error mentions "max 20 emails per hour" | Wait, then try again. |
| **Set up text messages** appears | Select **Open Phone & SMS**, verify your number, then ask again. |
| A text doesn't arrive | Use **Send test text** in **Phone & SMS**. If texts are off, select **Turn texts back on**. |
| You don't see the email | Check spam for "User message via Bearly". Confirm the step shows the address you expected before asking again; a retry sends a second copy. |


Once the step says sent, the message is out. Send a corrected follow-up if something was wrong.


## Try this

### Email yourself a finished report with its data

A formatted summary in your inbox, with the Page and the CSV it was built from attached.

**Why it works:** files Bearly creates in a chat, such as a Page or a CSV, are chat files, and Send Email can attach any chat file. The email body is written in Markdown, so tables and headings survive.
**You need:** your organization allows **Email notifications**.



1. Build the report in the chat — for example, ask for a one-page summary of an attached spreadsheet and a cleaned CSV.
2. Ask:

   ```text
   Email me a short summary of these findings with a table of the top five rows, subject "Q3 numbers". Attach the Page and the cleaned CSV.
   ```

3. Check the **Send Email** step, then open the email.



**Swap in:** teammates instead of yourself (managed organizations); a routine with **Email each result** to get it weekly.
**If it stalls:** if the attachments total more than 20 MB, ask for a smaller CSV (for example, only the columns you need).

### Send each teammate their own action items

Separate, short emails to each person with only their tasks from a meeting.

**Why it works:** Find Contacts turns names into exact work addresses in your organization, and Send Email sends to those addresses with you copied, so replies come back to you.
**You need:** a managed organization with the member directory available; a meeting transcript or notes in the chat; your organization allows **Email notifications**.



1. Open the chat with the meeting notes or transcript (see [Meetings](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/meetings.md)).
2. Ask:

   ```text
   List the action items by owner. Then email each owner only their items, subject "From Thursday's meeting", one email per person.
   ```

3. Check the **Send Email** steps — each shows who it went to.



**Swap in:** one combined email to everyone; a text to yourself listing what you own.
**If it stalls:** if a name matches several people, Bearly can't pick; give the work email instead.

### Get a text when a long task finishes

A text on your phone when a long research or file job is done, so you can walk away.

**Why it works:** Send SMS is a step Bearly can run at the end of an answer, so the text goes out after the work earlier in the same answer finishes. This covers work Bearly does inside its answer, such as web searches or file processing. Deep Research is different: its report runs only after you select **Run Research** on its card, and it emails you the PDF itself when it's done.
**You need:** a verified phone; your organization allows **Text messages** and the tools the task uses.



1. Start the long task and add the text to the same request:

   ```text
   Search the web for current heat pump incentives in Ontario, from federal, provincial, and utility programs, and put them in a table with amounts and deadlines. When it's done, text me one line with the biggest incentive.
   ```

2. Leave the chat open and do something else — the text arrives when the answer reaches that step.



**Swap in:** "email me the table" for the full result; a [routine](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md) when it should happen on a schedule.
**If it stalls:** if the task stops early, no text is sent. Check the chat for the reason.

## Related

- [Automate with Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md) — email and text delivery on a schedule.
- [Send email or text messages](https://bearly.ai/docs/features/send-email.md) in the feature guides.

canonical: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/permissions/"

# Approvals, permissions, and Full access

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/permissions/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/permissions/)

> How Bearly asks before using tools outside the chat, how long each approval lasts, how to review and revoke saved approvals in the Permissions control, how Standard and Full access differ, what always asks, starting new chats in Full access, organization Agent access, routine permission mode, and spoken approvals.

When a chat wants to reach outside itself, such as reading a local file, opening a site, working in Chrome, using a connected service, running a skill script, or controlling your screen, Bearly shows an approval card first. You decide how long each approval lasts. If you'd rather not be asked, **Full access** lets one chat on one device use its enabled tools without asking, with a short list of checks that always ask anyway.

## Approval cards

A card shows a title, the reason, and, for most tools, a **Details** disclosure with the specifics, such as the file path, site, or connector. A command shows on the card itself. Its buttons are **Deny**, **Stop** where offered, an **Approval duration** menu when longer approvals are offered, and the Allow button. Risky actions get a red Allow button.

| Approval duration | Allow button reads | Lasts |
|---|---|---|
| **Once** | **Allow** | This one action |
| **This response** | **Allow for this response** | "until this response ends", including if it errors or you stop it |
| **This session** | **Allow for now** | "until this chat reloads or you revoke access" |
| **This chat** | **Allow for this chat** | "in future responses in this chat on this device until you revoke access" |

Work locally uses its own labels for read-only approvals: **Allow reads for this response**, **Allow reads for now**, and **Allow reads this chat**. Some tools word their button differently, and some show no duration menu because they approve one action at a time.

After you decide, the card shows **Allowed**, **Denied**, **Stopped**, or **Allowed by Full access**.

**Deny** blocks that action, and Bearly continues without it where it can. **Stop** ends the whole task.

### What Standard asks for, by tool

| Tool | What asks first |
|---|---|
| [Work locally](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/browser-and-computer.md#work-locally) | Every read, write, edit, file transfer, and command, with the command or file on the card |
| [Bearly Browser](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/browser-and-computer.md#bearly-browser) | **Let this chat use this site?** for each new site; **Confirm this Browser action** before a submission or other consequential action; **Allow this visual Browser action?**; **Save this website file to chat?**; **Run this website tool?** |
| [Control Chrome](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/browser-and-computer.md#control-chrome) | **Read your Chrome tabs**, **Control Chrome**, or **Use Chrome DevTools**; a higher level covers the lower ones |
| [Computer Use](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/browser-and-computer.md#computer-use) | **Use your computer** for the task; **Confirm this computer action** before a consequential step your request didn't cover, or when a safety check is raised |
| [Connectors](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/connectors-and-skills.md#connectors) | **Connect** plus the service's name, when Bearly connects a new service for you; **Allow** plus the connector's name before using its tools |
| [Skill scripts](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/connectors-and-skills.md#skills) | A card titled with the skill and the script's path that shows the command, with the runtime and timeout in **Details** |
| Chat management | Listing your chats, reading other chats, and **Delete this chat?** before deleting |
| People Search | **Search for people?**, **Build the brief?**, and the other People Search confirmations before lookups that use your plan allowance |
| [Email and texts](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/email-and-text.md) | Nothing: asking for the email or text is the approval |

## The Permissions control

Next to the message box, the **Permissions** control shows this chat's mode and saved approvals. It reads **Full access** while Full access is on, with a **Paused** badge when it's paused. In the iOS app on a phone, it shows only a shield icon.

Open it to see:

- **Permissions** and "This chat · this device" — everything here applies only to this chat on this device.
- **Standard** — "Ask for approval when needed. Saved approvals still apply."
- **Full access** — "Use enabled tools without asking each time."
- The saved approvals, each with its duration and **Revoke**. With none: "No saved approvals in this chat."
- **Revoke all saved approvals**, and **Clear all permissions**, which also switches the chat to Standard.

Revoking stops the work in progress and makes Bearly ask again next time. Stopping a response doesn't remove approvals.

Saved approvals never sync to your other devices and never apply to routines. Each open window keeps its own copy, so if a chat is open in two windows, revoke in each. Approvals for a chat are deleted with the chat. Disconnecting, signing in again, or changing a connector's settings resets its approvals.

## Full access

Full access lets a chat use its enabled tools without asking each time. It covers only tools that are already on for the chat. It never turns on a tool, connects an account, grants a system permission, or removes the checks listed under [What always asks](#what-always-asks).

**Use it when:** you trust the task and the content it touches, and approving each step slows you down, for example a long refactor in a dedicated project folder.
**You need:** Full access available to your account and app version. On a managed organization, it's allowed unless an administrator turned it off. In the desktop app it covers every tool below; on the web, phones, and the iOS app it covers connectors only.



1. In the chat, select **Permissions** next to the message box, then **Full access**.
2. Read **Enable full access?** — "Bearly can use enabled tools to read, change, or delete files, run programs, and act in signed-in apps and websites without asking each time. Mistakes or instructions in untrusted content can cause unintended changes." It lists the enabled tools and says it "Applies to this chat on this device."
3. Select **Enable full access**, or **Cancel** to stay in Standard.



**What you get:** the control reads **Full access**, and actions run without cards; each shows **Allowed by Full access**. Tools and accounts you enable later in the chat are included.
**Limits:** "Organization rules, operating-system permissions, and required safety checks still apply." It's one chat on one device; other chats, other devices, and copies of the chat don't inherit it.
**Combines with:** [Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md#permission-mode), which have their own, separate Full access.

The tools Full access can cover appear in the dialog as "Local files and commands", "Enabled skill scripts", "Computer control", "Bearly Browser", "Connected Chrome", and "Connected tools" (connectors). With nothing enabled it says "Enable an external tool in this chat to use Full Access."

> **Caution — Full access acts without checking with you**
> In Full access, Bearly can change or delete files, run programs, send messages, and act in signed-in sites without a card. Instructions hidden in a web page, file, or email can steer it. Use it in chats where you know what the task will touch.


### How long it stays on

Full access stays on in that chat on that device until you choose **Standard**: "Stays on in this chat on this device until you choose Standard or revoke it." Restarting the app, sleep, time away, and losing your connection don't turn it off.

It pauses only when Bearly or your device can't allow it: your organization turned it off, your account isn't eligible, or the desktop app needs an update. The control then shows the **Paused** badge, and the chat uses Standard approvals in the meantime ("Full access is paused. Approvals apply until it turns back on."). The pause notice says why:

- "Update Bearly to keep using Full access on this device. Until then, Bearly asks before using tools."
- "Your team turned off Full access. Bearly asks before using tools until your team turns it back on."
- "Full access isn't available right now. Bearly asks before using tools until it is, then turns Full access back on."

A paused chat keeps your choice. Once Full access is allowed again, it turns back on by itself when you send the next message or when the window comes back into focus. You can also open **Permissions** and select **Resume full access**.

To turn it off, open the control and select **Standard**. Bearly stops current work where it can; actions already finished elsewhere aren't undone. Your saved approvals stay.

### Start new chats in Full access

**App Settings** → **Privacy & Security** → **Start new chats in Full access**: "New chats on this device use your enabled tools without asking. Not recommended unless you know what you're doing."

Turning it on asks **Start every new chat in Full access?**, which warns that agents make mistakes and that "instructions hidden in web pages, files, or emails can steer them", and notes it "Applies on this device only. You can switch any chat back to Standard from Permissions." Select **Turn on**. It doesn't change existing chats, chats where you chose Standard, or routines. If Full access isn't available, the setting says why, for example "Your team administrator turned off Full Access."

## What always asks

These keep asking even in Full access:

- **Connecting a new service** — the **Connect** card for a connector.
- **Chat management** — listing your chats, reading other chats, and deleting chats.
- **Computer Use safety checks** — the **Confirm this computer action** card raised by a safety check during the task.
- **People Search lookups that use your plan allowance** — such as **Search for people?** and **Build the brief?**.
- **Routine consent** — turning on unattended local access or browser access for a routine.
- **Certificate warnings** in Bearly Browser — only you can decide.

Your computer's own permission prompts, such as macOS **Accessibility** and **Screen Recording**, or a Windows administrator prompt, are always yours to answer.

## Approvals in a voice call

During a voice call, with voice models that support it, Bearly reads a pending approval aloud and takes your spoken answer; with other voice models, the card stays on screen for you to answer. Say a complete reply such as "yes", "approve", "allow once", or "sure, go ahead". "Don't ask me again" or "allow for this chat" approves for this chat when that duration is offered. "No" or "deny" skips the action; "stop" ends the task and the call. Questions, conditions, and blanket approvals leave the card waiting. Routine consent cards always need the on-screen card. This listens for words, not for your voice, so use the card when others nearby could answer.

## Routines

A routine doesn't use the chat's approvals or the chat's Full access. In **Standard**, of the tools that would ask in a chat, a scheduled run can use only what you turned on for that routine: selected connectors, skill scripts you allowed, unattended local access, and Bearly Browser on the sites you approved. Anything else that needs approval is denied, and the run continues without it. A routine's own **Full access** is enabled in its editor on the desktop device that runs it, and a material edit sends it back to **Full access needs review**. See [Automate with Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md#permission-mode).

## For organization administrators

On a managed organization, Full access eligibility is a separate setting from feature policies. Open **Organizations**, choose the organization, and on **Overview** find **Agent access**: "Control who can let agents use enabled external tools without asking for approval each time."

- **Allow full access by default** — on unless an administrator turned it off. "Each member must still enable it for each chat or routine on its device."
- **Turn off full access for everyone** — "stops within 60 seconds", turns off the default, and removes individual exceptions. Saved tool approvals remain. Confirm with **Turn off for everyone**.
- Per member, in **Members** → **Member settings** → **Full access**: **Use organization default**, **Allow full access**, or **Do not allow full access**.

Group administrators can't grant Full access. Allowing it doesn't turn on tools your policies turn off. See [Organizations](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/organizations.md).

## Try this

### Approve a batch of edits once

A folder of files renamed and updated with one approval instead of forty.

**Why it works:** a **This response** approval covers every matching action until the answer ends, then expires on its own, so you review the plan once and nothing carries over to the next request.
**You need:** the desktop app; Work locally on for the chat with the folder chosen.



1. Ask for the plan before any change:

   ```text
   List the files in this folder you'd rename to the pattern YYYY-MM-DD-title.pdf, and what each new name would be. Don't change anything yet.
   ```

2. Check the list, then ask: "Go ahead with exactly that list."
3. At the first card, open **Details**, choose **This response**, and select **Allow for this response** — the rest of the renames run without cards.
4. When the answer ends, the approval ends with it.



**Swap in:** **This chat** when you'll keep working in the same folder for days; **Once** when each action needs its own look.
**If it stalls:** if a card asks for something outside the plan, deny it and ask Bearly why.

### Give one chat Full access for one folder

A chat that can work through a project folder without stopping, while every other chat keeps asking.

**Why it works:** Full access belongs to one chat on one device, and covers only the tools enabled in that chat. Pairing it with a dedicated working folder keeps the work focused.
**You need:** the desktop app; Full access available to your account; your organization allows **Work locally**.



1. Start a new chat for this work only, select **Work locally** → **Choose folder…**, and pick the project folder — keep backups of anything important.
2. Select **Permissions** → **Full access**, read the dialog, and select **Enable full access**.
3. Ask for the job and its boundary:

   ```text
   Update every import of the old logger to the new one in this folder, run the tests, and fix what breaks. Don't touch anything outside this folder or push anything.
   ```

4. When you're done, open **Permissions** and select **Standard**.



**Swap in:** turn on Bearly Browser in the same chat to check a local page after each change; keep Standard and use **This chat** approvals instead when you want a record of each step.
**If it stalls:** if the control shows **Paused**, read the notice; Full access comes back by itself once it's allowed again.

### Check what a chat can do, and take it back

A clean slate in a chat where you approved more than you meant to.

**Why it works:** every saved approval for a chat on this device is listed in one place, and revoking stops current work at once.
**You need:** nothing beyond the chat.



1. Select **Permissions** next to the message box — saved approvals are listed with their durations.
2. Select **Revoke** beside one, or **Clear all permissions** to switch to Standard and remove them all.
3. If the chat is open in another window, repeat there.



**Swap in:** **Revoke all saved approvals** to keep Full access but drop the saved approvals.
**If it stalls:** for a routine, change its own settings in the routine editor; chat approvals never applied to it.

## Related

- [Let Bearly use a browser, your files, and your computer](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/browser-and-computer.md) — the tools these approvals cover.
- [Privacy and security](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/privacy-and-security.md) — how Bearly handles your data.
- [Choose how Bearly uses external tools](https://bearly.ai/docs/settings/agent-permissions.md) in the feature guides.

title: "Building blocks: how Bearly's features connect"

canonical: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/building-blocks/"
markdown: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/building-blocks.md"
---

# Building blocks: how Bearly's features connect

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/building-blocks/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/building-blocks/)

> The composition model behind Bearly — what each capability takes in and produces, which outputs feed which tools, where each block runs, which plans and Routines support it, and what breaks a chain — so a person or an AI can design new multi-step workflows.

Every Bearly capability is a building block: it takes something in, does one job, and leaves its result in the chat. Later steps can use anything earlier steps left there, so blocks chain into workflows nobody wrote down. This page lists the blocks, the hand-offs between them that are known to work, and the limits that break a chain.

## How blocks connect

**Every result lands in the chat.** A block leaves its output in one of five places:

- **Text in the conversation**: the answer, plus what tools return to the AI, such as search results, page text, or data.
- **Files in the chat**: uploads and everything Bearly saves or makes, such as Pages, CSV and Markdown files, images, audio, video, PDFs, and transcripts. They're listed in **Browse files** in the chat header, under **Uploaded** and **Generated**, and each has an ID the AI can hand to the next tool.
- **Cards**: interactive results such as a Deep Research report, a **Chart**, a YouTube video card, a Best Pick card, or the image editor. Some cards also save files.
- **Links**: a Page you publish gets a stable bearly.sh link.
- **Messages outside Bearly**: an email to you or a teammate, or a text to your phone.

**Later steps can use what earlier steps left.** The AI reads the conversation on every turn and can open any file in the chat by its ID. A finished Deep Research report is passed to later turns in full. In long chats, the **History** setting decides how older messages are kept: **Chat (Dynamic)** keeps the newest messages in full and shortens older ones, and **Agentic (Compaction)** writes a summary of older history and keeps the recent part word for word. Files stay in the chat either way, and the AI can still look at an image after the message that added it has been shortened. See [Tune a chat](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/settings.md).

**References point at exact files and tools.** Type `/` at the start of a message or `@` anywhere in it to pick a tool, skill, prompt template, or file by name. A message can start with one `/` command and hold any number of `@` references, so one message can name a file and a tool together. A reference only selects a tool you already have; it never turns on one your plan or organization has turned off. See [Write, attach, and send](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/compose.md#reference-tools-files-and-skills).

**Projects share files across chats.** Files in a project's **Docs & Data** are available to every chat in that project: they appear in the `@` menu, and the document tools search them. Turn on **Always Search Documents** in the project's chat settings and every answer searches them first. See [Projects](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects.md).

**Routines replay a chain on a schedule.** A routine runs a saved prompt in its own chat at the times you set, with a smaller set of tools, and each run can build on the ones before it. See [Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md) and [Routines run a smaller toolset](#routines-run-a-smaller-toolset).

## The block catalog

How to read the tables:

- **Block** is the name the app shows, with the `/` or `@` label you type. Most blocks also run when you simply ask in plain words.
- **Plan** is the lowest plan that can use the block. **Any** means every plan, including Free; usage still counts against your plan's allowance. See [Plans, credits, and limits](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/plans-and-credits.md).
- **Where**: **All** means the web app, a phone browser, the desktop app, and the iOS app. **Desktop** means the desktop app on Mac, Windows, or Linux.
- **In Routines** says whether a scheduled run can use the block.
- **Council experts** says whether the models answering a `/council` question can use the block while they work.
- **—** means the column doesn't apply.

### Research

Details: [Research](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/research.md).

| Block | Takes | Produces | Plan | Where | In Routines | Council experts |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Web Search** `/web-search` | A question; runs when the chat's **Web** setting is **Auto** | An answer with numbered source links, and sometimes an image gallery | Any | All | Yes | Yes |
| **Image Search** `/image-search` | A description of the images you want | A gallery of up to 20 images with their source pages | Any | All | Yes | No |
| **Read Site** `/read-site` | One web address | The page's text for the AI; long pages, documents, images, and media are saved as chat files | Any | All | Yes | Yes |
| **Deep Research** `/deep-research` | A text question, then a plan you review and can edit | A cited report with sources and figures, a PDF in the chat, and the PDF by email | Analyst (Standard), Research (Ultra) | All | Only through a routine's Deep Research plugin | No |
| **Scholar** `/scholar` | A question about research literature | An answer with superscript citations, a **Papers** card, and open-access PDF links when they exist | Any | All | Yes | Yes |
| **Council of Experts** `/council` (listed as Ask Experts) | One self-contained question that names any files it should use | Answers from 2 to 5 models side by side, then one combined reply | Pro | All | No | — |
| **Best Pick** `/bestpick` | A shopping question, such as the best stick vacuum for pet hair under a price | A card with one product to buy, a store link, the evidence, and follow-up chips | Pro | All | No | No |
| **People Search** `/people-search`, `/find-people`, `/work-emails`, `/lead-enrichment` | A named person plus one detail, a description of the people you want, names with a company domain, or known profiles | A candidate card, then a cited brief; lists and email lookups as tables in the reply | Analyst | All | No | No |
| **Recall** `/recall` | A mention of earlier work or a past decision | Matching excerpts from your past chats | Any | All | No | No |
| **Maps** `/maps` | Places, an area, or a route | A place or route card on the final answer | Any | All | No | No |
| **Weather** `/weather`, `/air-quality`, `/pollen` | A place and a time range | A weather card; past weather as text | Any | All | No | No |
| **SEO data** `/seo` | Keywords, sites, or a page to audit | Search data in the reply | Any | All | No | No |
| **Domain research** `/domains` | A naming brief or one domain | Name ideas, and whether a domain is available to register | Any | All | No | No |

## Hand-off rules

Each rule names an output type and the blocks that can take it next. These connections are known to work; anything not listed here may still work, but test it before you rely on it.

### Files feed tools

- **Any file in the chat or its project** → `@` it by name under **Assets**, so the next tool uses that exact file.
- **A document in the chat or project** (PDF, Office file, text) → **Search Docs**, **Read Doc**, **Scan Doc**, and **Summarize Doc**. These tools appear once the chat or its project has at least one file.
- **A spreadsheet or raw data file** → **Run Python** reads it and writes a chart-ready CSV → **Create chart**. Charts read data only from a CSV file in the chat: up to 10 columns and 2 MB, one row for a KPI, up to 100 rows for a bar chart, and up to 2,000 rows for a line chart. They never sample or cut rows to fit.
- **A CSV that's already chart-ready** → **Create chart** directly, with no Python step.
- **Run Python output** (up to 5 files per run) → files in the chat → **Create chart**, a **Page** with the results baked in, or **Send Email** attachments. Each run starts fresh, so name the files a later run should use.
- **Any image in the chat**, whether uploaded, generated, or saved by Read Site → **Touch Up** as a source, or **Make Video** as the start or end frame.
- **A generated video** → **View Media** watches it → **Edit Video** changes it, if the clip is 3 to 10 seconds and under 50 MB.
- **Audio or video files**, whether uploaded, saved by **Get Video**, or a post's video from **X** → **Transcribe**. Video files also → **View Media**, which looks at images and video but not audio-only files.
- **A transcript** → **Transcribe** also saves it in the chat as an `.srt` file named after the original → later turns read it, summarize it, or hand the summary to **Read Aloud**.
- **PDF and Office files** → **Document editor** fills forms, adds tracked changes to .docx files, or builds decks and spreadsheets → new files in the chat.
- **Files in the chat** → **Send Email** as attachments (up to 10, with 20 MB per file and 20 MB in total), or **Share file** to give one file a Page of its own (files under 300 MB).
- **A file on your computer** (desktop app) → **Work locally** attaches it to the chat (up to 50 MB). The reverse also works: it saves a chat file to disk.

### The web feeds files

- **Read Site** → saves long pages as Markdown files, documents (PDF, Office, OpenDocument, EPUB, RTF) as chat files with a short text preview, and images and media as chat files → the document tools, **Touch Up**, and **View Media**.
- **Image Search** → shows images the AI hasn't looked at yet → **Read Site** saves the one you pick into the chat → **Touch Up** or **View Media**.
- **Scholar** → gives an open-access PDF link when a paper has one → **Read Site** saves the paper as a document → **Search Docs** or **Read Doc** for exact quotes with page citations.
- **An X post** → **Web Search** to check it against news sources; its video → imported as a chat file → **View Media** or **Transcribe**.
- **A YouTube card** → transcript lookups and custom cuts in later turns. Pressing **Check claims** saves a claims file → **Scholar** or **Web Search** to look into the figures sites disagreed on.
- **Bearly Browser** → a download is saved to the chat (one file, up to 50 MiB) → the document tools. **Capture and annotate page** attaches a marked-up capture to your next message.
- **Chrome control** or **Computer Use** → reads what's on screen into the conversation → **Run Python**, **Create chart**, or a **Deep Research** question. Computer Use's own agent can search the web and read documents mid-task but can't run Python, so the main chat does that step.

### The conversation feeds later turns

- **A Deep Research report** → passed in full to later turns → rewrite it, turn its numbers into a CSV and chart, build a Page, or email the report text. Bearly emails you the report's PDF on its own; the AI isn't told the PDF is in the chat, so **Send Email** doesn't attach it.
- **Deep Research takes only text** → put what earlier steps found (from a file, a Chrome tab, or a call) into the research question.
- **Council of Experts** → experts see only the question, not the chat or its files → name the files and facts they need in the `/council` question.
- **Any answer** → **Edit in Canvas** to revise it line by line, **Read aloud** to hear it (on answers that are all text), or the **Rerun** menu to get another model's version.
- **A voice call** → its transcript stays in the chat → later typed turns build on it.
- **Dictation** with **After dictation** set to **Send immediately** → runs a normal turn with all the chat's tools.
- **Share your screen** → every message carries a fresh capture → step-by-step help that stays current.
- **A Prompt Template's result** → `/share` turns it into a Page.
- **Find people** → **Find work emails** for the people you pick → a **People Search** brief on one of them.
- **A Best Pick card** → **Cheaper**, **Worth spending more**, or **Not this one** refine it → **Send Email** can send what the pick is and why, but not its store link: the AI gets the card's contents without links.
- **Recall** → excerpts from past chats → with **Chat management**, the AI can read the full earlier chat after you allow it. A very long chat it reads is saved as a Markdown file in this chat.

### Across chats and over time

- **A meeting transcript** → **Add to chat** attaches it as a Markdown file to a new or existing chat's draft, without sending → summaries, action items, a follow-up email, or a Page. **Add to project** puts the same file in a project's files → every chat in that project. The meeting's **Quick summary** isn't included in the file.
- **Project files** → every chat in the project, through `@` and the document tools; **Always Search Documents** makes every answer search them first.
- **A routine run** → lands in the routine's chat → with **Build on previous runs** on, the next run sees earlier ones. Files a run writes, such as a Page or a CSV, can go out with **Email each result**.
- **A chat that did the job once** → ask it to repeat on a schedule, for example "Do this every weekday at 8 and email me the result." Bearly sets up a routine from that chat.

## What breaks a chain

Check these before you plan a chain, and again when a step doesn't appear or stops.

### Plans

| Plan needed | Blocks |
|---|---|
| Pro or higher | Routines, Council of Experts, Best Pick, choosing a voice-call model |
| Analyst or higher | Deep Research (Standard), People Search |
| Research or higher | Deep Research (Ultra) |

Some chat models also need Pro or Analyst; the model picker opens **Billing** if you choose one above your plan. A block above your plan shows an upgrade card in the chat instead of running. See [Plans, credits, and limits](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/plans-and-credits.md).

### Organization controls

In an organization, an admin's policy can turn off any block. The `/` label then disappears, and a reference can't bring it back. Some controls switch off several blocks at once:

- **Web** off removes Web Search, Image Search, Read Site, Deep Research, Scholar, YouTube, and Best Pick.
- **Chat web search** off removes Image Search, Deep Research, Best Pick, Scholar's search of journal sites, and YouTube's **Find** and **Check claims**.
- **File uploads** off removes attaching files, screen sharing, Get Video, viewing X videos, and the files YouTube saves; it also blocks uploads to a project's **Docs & Data** and **Local Directory Sync**.
- **Run Python** off removes both `/python` and `/document`. Charts then work only on a CSV that's already prepared, or one made with **Work locally** in the desktop app.
- **Sharing** off removes Share. **Public links** and **Share with people and teams** decide who can open a published Page.
- **Memory Recall** off turns off Recall and **Memories**.

Most other blocks have their own control: **Images**, **Video**, **Transcription**, **Text to speech**, **Voice**, **Dictation**, **Meetings**, **Council**, **Deep Research**, **People Search**, **Charts**, **Routines**, **Work locally**, **Computer Use**, **Browser**, **Chrome extension browser**, **MCP Connectors**, **Skills**, **Email notifications**, **Text messages**, and **Chat management**. See [Organizations](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/organizations.md).

### Desktop-only blocks

These need the desktop app: Work locally, Computer Use, Bearly Browser, Chrome control, installed skills and their scripts, npm connector servers, running routines, recording a meeting's computer audio, Get Video for YouTube links, and **Local Directory Sync** for projects. On the web or a phone, most of them show a prompt to get the desktop app. See [Get Bearly](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/get-bearly.md).

### Routines run a smaller toolset

A scheduled run is a chat with nobody watching, so it gets a fixed set of tools:

- **Can use:** Web Search, Image Search, Read Site, X (but not importing a post's video), Scholar, the document tools (Search Docs, Read Doc, Scan Doc, and Summarize Doc), View Media, creating, reading, editing, and renaming chat files (including Pages), Send Email, Send SMS, the connectors and skills you add to the routine, and Deep Research through the routine's **Deep Research** plugin.
- **Only with an explicit routine setting:** Work locally (**Unattended local access**), a skill's scripts (that skill's **Allow scripts**), and Bearly Browser (**Browser access**, on the sites you list).
- **Can't use:** Run Python, the Document editor, Fix Grammar, images, video, transcription, text to speech, voice, Canvas, charts, Visualize, Share, Council of Experts, YouTube, Best Pick, Get Video, People Search, Maps, Weather, SEO data, Domain research, Chat management, Recall, Prompt Templates, remote modules, Computer Use, and Chrome.

A routine runs only in the desktop app, on the device it's installed on, while the app is open, and each run stops after 25 minutes. To get numbers or charts from a schedule, have the routine write a CSV or an HTML Page, or continue in the routine's chat afterwards.

### End-to-end encrypted chats

Turn on end-to-end encryption and your new chats are encrypted on your devices. Bearly stores and syncs only the locked copies. Each message, and any file a tool works on, still goes to the model and the services doing the work. Some blocks behave differently in these chats:

- **Search Docs** isn't available, and **Always Search Documents** has no effect. **Read Doc**, **Scan Doc**, and **Summarize Doc** still work.
- **Memories** and Recall aren't available. Encrypted chats don't appear in **Search chats and files**, and the chat-management tools in a regular chat can't see them.
- **Get Video** works only through Bearly's servers: no YouTube links, and up to 100 MiB per saved file.
- **Run Python** works, with up to 50 MiB of input files per run.
- Publishing a Page asks first ("Publish a readable copy?"), because the published copy isn't encrypted. Limits: 10 MiB for the Page, 2 MiB for the preview, and 25 MiB for an included file.
- A routine created from an encrypted chat keeps its instructions and results in standard storage.

See [Privacy and security](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/privacy-and-security.md).

### Zero Data Retention only

Turn on **Zero Data Retention only** and Bearly shows only zero-retention models, and automatic fallbacks stay on them too. It governs chat models. Council of Experts uses only models you're allowed, so its lineup can get shorter. It doesn't govern image, video, transcription, or voice-call models, or research services such as Web Search, Deep Research, People Search, and Read Site. Web search sends the search provider your query, never your name, account, or IP address. Image search runs on a provider that keeps nothing after it answers. See [Choose and compare models](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/models.md).

### Approvals and Full access

- In **Standard** permissions, Bearly asks before a tool reaches outside the chat: local files, desktop control, browsers, connectors, skill scripts, People Search, and listing, reading, or deleting your chats. The chain waits at that step until you answer. **Needs attention** in the sidebar lists chats waiting on you.
- Approvals you save stay on this device: **This response** lasts until the answer ends, **This session** until the chat reloads, and **This chat** until you revoke it. They never sync and never authorize a routine.
- **Full access** lets one chat on one device use your enabled tools without asking. It still asks before connecting a new service, before listing, reading, or deleting your chats, before People Search runs, before giving a routine browser access or unattended local access, and for Computer Use safety checks and certificate warnings.
- On the web and phones, Full access covers connectors only.
- Routines have their own **Permission mode**, turned on from the desktop device that runs them.

See [Permissions](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/permissions.md).

### Read-only chats

Project chats you can only read have no composer and no message controls. Files, images, Pages, and audio show their viewers only, Share isn't offered, and a People Search brief can be viewed but not run.

### Built-in limits worth planning around

- **Web Search**: up to 2 searches per answer, each with up to 8 queries.
- **Deep Research**: one run at a time per person, and no cancel button once a run starts.
- **Run Python**: up to 5 output files per run.
- **Council of Experts**: needs at least 2 models you're allowed to use.
- **YouTube**: up to 3 videos per message.
- **Message queue**: up to 4 waiting messages; it pauses after an error or at a usage limit.
- **Send Email**: up to 25 recipients and 20 emails an hour.
- **Routines**: up to 10 active; runs are at least 30 minutes apart.

## Design a new workflow

Use this procedure to build a chain nobody has written down, whether you're a person or an AI guiding one.



1. State the end artifact — a file, a card, a published Page link, an email, a text, or a routine. This fixes what the last block must produce.
2. Find the block whose **Produces** column matches it — that block is your last step.
3. Read that block's **Takes** column and find the block that produces it. Repeat until you reach something you already have: a question, a link, or a file — this gives you the chain in order.
4. Check every block against the gates above: plan, platform, organization controls, an encrypted chat, and the routine toolset if it will run on a schedule — swap any block that fails for another with the same output.
5. Write one prompt per step, naming the tool with `/` or `@` and the file with `@` — this removes the AI's guesswork about which tool or file you mean.
6. Run a step, check its output, then run the next — confirm the file appears in **Browse files**, the chart has the right rows, or the report finished. Queue later steps only once the chain is proven.
7. To repeat the chain, ask the chat to set it up as a routine — keep only blocks routines can run.



### Worked example

Goal: a dashboard Page of quarterly revenue, published to a link and emailed to you as a file.

- The last two blocks are **Share** (makes the link) and **Send Email** (sends the Page file). Both need a Page; a Page with real numbers needs clean data; clean data comes from **Run Python** reading your spreadsheet.
- Gates: Run Python, Pages, Share, and Send Email are on every plan; your organization must allow **Run Python**, **Charts**, **Sharing**, and **Email notifications**. Not for a routine, because routines can't run Python.

Where a prompt names a file with `@`, type `@` and pick the file from the menu.

```text
/python Read @sales-2026.xlsx and write a CSV of revenue by quarter, one column per region.
```

```text
/chart Make a grouped bar chart from that CSV.
```

```text
Build a one-page dashboard Page with the quarterly figures baked in, a region filter, and three sentences on the trend.
```

```text
/share
```

Review the Page and publish it, then:

```text
@email me the dashboard Page and the CSV as attachments.
```

Swap a block and the rest still holds: replace the spreadsheet with a **Deep Research** report as the data source, or replace **Send Email** with **Send SMS** for a one-line alert.
