# Recipes for research and analysis

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/research-recipes/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/research-recipes/)

> Step-by-step recipes that chain Bearly's research tools (Deep Research, Scholar, Read Site, document search, YouTube claim checks, X posts, Rerun, and Council of Experts) with Run Python, charts, Pages, and email to produce cited reports, checked figures, and published briefs.

These recipes chain Bearly's research tools so that one step's output becomes the next step's input: a finished report becomes a chart, a paper becomes a searchable file, and a video's figures go to a literature check. Each recipe names its building blocks and the hand-off between them, so you can swap one block for another that produces the same kind of output. For what each block takes in and gives back, see [Building blocks](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/building-blocks.md).

## Turn a Deep Research report into a chart, a Page, and an email

You end up with a cited report and its PDF in your inbox, a chart of its numbers, a published Page with the findings, and an email to a teammate with a summary and the Page link.

**Why it works:** Deep Research writes a cited report, saves a PDF of it in the chat, and emails the PDF to you. The finished report stays in the chat's context in full, so later messages can work from its numbers. Run Python writes those numbers to a CSV file, **Create chart** draws from that file, a Page is an HTML file Bearly writes into the chat, and Send Email sends what the chat wrote, with the link you paste.
**You need:** **Analyst** plan or higher (**Ultra** runs need **Research**). Any platform. Organization controls, on unless an admin turned them off: **Deep Research**, **Web**, **Chat web search**, **Run Python**, **Charts**, **Sharing** with **Public links** or **Share with people and teams**, and **Email notifications**. Emailing a teammate needs a managed organization; otherwise email yourself and forward it.



1. Start with the Deep Research command:

   ```text
   /deep-research How big is the US residential battery storage market each year from 2022 to 2030, by installed capacity and by revenue, and which companies lead installation?
   ```

   — Bearly shows a **Deep Research** card with an editable **Query**. Nothing runs yet; planning is free.

2. Select **Create Plan**, read the **Plan**, and type changes in **Notes**, such as "Prefer government and industry-association sources, and mark forecasts separately from actuals." Select **Revise Plan**, then **Run Research**. — Your edits become part of the run. Turn on **Ultra** first only when the question needs a deeper run and your plan includes it. When the run finishes you see the report with numbered citations, a **Sources** list, and "PDF ready" with **View PDF**. Bearly also emails you the PDF.

3. Ask for the numbers as a chart:

   ```text
   From the report, put every market-size estimate into a table with year, value, unit, source, and whether it's a forecast. Then chart revenue by year as a line chart, one line per source.
   ```

   — A **Run Python** step writes a CSV file and a **Chart** card draws it. Select **View data** and check the rows against the report before you go on.

4. Build the Page:

   ```text
   Build a one-page Page with the chart, the five most important findings in plain language, and a link to the source behind each number.
   ```

   — A live preview of the Page appears in the chat. Select **Open** to see it full size. Ask for changes in the chat; each edit saves a new version.

5. In the open Page, select **Share file** → **Prepare share**. When it's ready, the prepared Page opens on its own, marked "Ready to share"; select **Publish** in its header, choose the **Audience**, select **Publish**, then **Copy link**. — Bearly adds artwork and a link preview and publishes the Page at a bearly.sh link that stays the same when you publish updates.

6. Paste the link into one more message:

   ```text
   Email Dana Ortiz a 100-word summary of the report and this link: [paste the Page link].
   ```

   — Bearly finds Dana in your organization's contacts and sends the email. You're copied on it. To share the full report, forward Dana the PDF email Bearly sent you.



**Swap in:**
- **Scholar for Deep Research** (any plan): ask `/scholar` the same question. The cited answer stays in the chat, and steps 3 to 6 work the same, without the PDF. In step 4, ask for the paper behind each number by title and authors; Bearly doesn't get a link for every paper, and the links are on the **Papers** cards.
- **People and teams for a public link:** in step 5 choose **People and teams**, select **Publish**, then add viewers with **Manage viewers**, so only the people and teams you name can open the Page.
- **Attach the Page instead of publishing it:** skip step 5 and ask "Email me the Page." Send Email attaches the HTML file itself, which opens in any browser.
- **A schedule instead of a one-off:** a Routine can run Deep Research every week and email the PDF, but it can't run Python or draw charts. See [Schedule a weekly Deep Research digest](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/automation-recipes.md#schedule-a-weekly-deep-research-digest).

**If it stalls:**
- "You can't start another Deep Research run right now" means one of your runs is still in progress or you've reached a usage limit. Wait for the run to finish, or check your plan's usage. There's no cancel button once a run starts, and a run keeps going if you refresh or switch devices.
- Charts read a CSV of up to 10 columns, and a line chart draws up to 12 lines. If the chart fails, ask for fewer columns or fewer sources.
- If Bearly can't find your teammate, use their full name as it appears in your organization, or their work email.

## Read the strongest paper in full and quote it with page citations

You get a list of papers on a question, the best open-access one saved in the chat, exact figures quoted with page citations, and a one-page brief.

**Why it works:** Scholar's paper reader returns an open-access PDF link when a paper has one. Read Site downloads that PDF into the chat as a file. Once a chat has a file, Bearly's document tools can search it and read it page by page, and answers from a document search carry citations that open the document near the cited page. The Page at the end is written from everything above it in the chat.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization controls: **Scholar** and **Web**. The chat's **Web** setting on **Auto** (the default), which Read Site needs. Searching the saved paper needs a chat that isn't end-to-end encrypted; reading it page by page works in any chat.



1. Ask Scholar:

   ```text
   /scholar What do recent papers say about retrieval-augmented generation reducing hallucinations in question answering? Prefer peer-reviewed work and say where results conflict.
   ```

   — Bearly searches papers and answers with superscript citations. Each search shows a Papers card listing the papers it found, with links, and each paper it read shows a card marked **Read** with the passage it used.

2. Open one in full:

   ```text
   Open the strongest open-access paper from that list in full.
   ```

   — Bearly fetches the paper's PDF and saves it as a file in the chat. Only a paper with an open-access PDF can be opened in full; if the one you want has none, pick another paper or narrow the question.

3. Ask for exact figures:

   ```text
   From that paper, quote the exact datasets, the baseline, and the reported hallucination rates, with page numbers.
   ```

   — Bearly searches and reads the saved PDF. Passages it found with a document search carry citations you can click to open the PDF near the cited page.

4. Write the brief:

   ```text
   Turn this into a one-page Page: the question, what the evidence says, the key numbers in a small table, open questions, and a citation for every paper used.
   ```

   — A Page preview appears. **Open** shows it full size, and **Download** saves the HTML file.



**Swap in:**
- **Any public PDF for Scholar:** skip steps 1 and 2 and send `/read-site` with the PDF's address. Steps 3 and 4 work the same.
- **Related work before you pick:** after step 1, ask "Show papers that cite the strongest one." Scholar maps similar papers, citing papers, and references.
- **Council of Experts for the brief** (Pro): send `/council` with your question and name the saved PDF with `@` (pick it under **Assets**). Each expert can search and read it and search the web, and Bearly synthesizes their answers.
- **Listen instead of read:** ask for a 60-second plain-language summary and select **Read aloud** under the answer (needs **Text to speech**).

**If it stalls:**
- A read that shows "abstract only" means Scholar holds only that paper's abstract, so figures from it rest on the abstract. Open the paper in full in step 2 if it has an open-access PDF, or pick a different paper.
- If Scholar finds fewer than three papers in its index, it also searches academic journal sites on the web, when your organization allows **Chat web search**. If results are still thin, Bearly says the literature is thin rather than stretching it.

## Pull exact figures out of a long public report

A long report at a public link becomes a searchable file in the chat, and its figures become a checked table and a chart.

**Why it works:** Read Site saves PDFs, Word, PowerPoint, and Excel files, and long web pages, as files in the chat instead of pasting them in. Scan Doc finds exact words with their page and line, Read Doc reads up to 20 pages at a time, and Run Python writes the figures to a CSV that **Create chart** draws from.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization controls: **Web**, **Run Python**, and **Charts**. The chat's **Web** setting on **Auto**.



1. Save the report into the chat:

   ```text
   /read-site https://www.example.gov/reports/annual-energy-outlook-2026.pdf
   ```

   — Bearly saves the PDF as a file in the chat and shows it as a file card with a preview.

2. Find every mention of a term:

   ```text
   @find-in-document Find every mention of "capacity factor" in that report, with page numbers.
   ```

   — Scan Doc returns exact matches with the page, line, and surrounding text, ten at a time. Ask "show the next matches" to page through.

3. Build the table:

   ```text
   Read the pages with the regional tables and put capacity factor by region and year into a table. Give the page number for each row.
   ```

   — Bearly reads those pages and builds the table, with a page for every row so you can check it.

4. Chart it:

   ```text
   Chart capacity factor by year as a line chart, one line per region, and tell me which region changed most.
   ```

   — Run Python writes a CSV and a **Chart** card draws it. **Download PNG** saves the image; **View data** shows the rows.



**Swap in:**
- **Your own file for Read Site:** attach the report with **+** → **Attach files** instead. Steps 2 to 4 work the same.
- **A summary first:** `/summarize` gives a **Deep Summary** of a very long document. It summarizes evenly spaced sections rather than every line, so keep Scan Doc for exact figures.
- **An interactive Page for the chart:** ask for a Page with a region filter. Bearly builds the table into the Page so it works on its own.
- **Email for the chart:** ask "Email me the table as a CSV." Send Email attaches files from the chat.

**If it stalls:**
- Read Site reads public HTTPS addresses only and doesn't sign in or click. For a report behind a sign-in, use Bearly Browser or Chrome in the desktop app; see [Recipes for automation and agents](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/automation-recipes.md).
- If step 2 finds nothing, try a shorter phrase. Scan Doc matches exact text, ignoring case.

## Check a video's figures against the research literature

You get the figures a YouTube video states, how many websites agree with each one, and what published papers say about the disputed ones.

**Why it works:** A YouTube link opens a video card with a **Check claims** button. The check is saved as a file in the chat, so Bearly can read it in later messages and hand the disputed figures to Scholar. The video's transcript is available to the chat, which is how Bearly finds the minutes to cut.
**You need:** Any plan. Web app, phone browser, or desktop app. Organization controls: **YouTube**, **Web**, **Chat web search**, **Scholar**, and **File uploads** (the claims check is saved as a file). The video needs a transcript: readable captions, or one the desktop app fetched with **Get transcript**. **Check claims** turns off once you've used all of your plan allowance.



1. Paste the link with a question:

   ```text
   https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VIDEO_ID What's the main argument, and which numbers does it rest on?
   ```

   — Bearly opens a video card with a summary, chapters, and tabs such as **Summary** and **Insights**, and answers your question.

2. On the card's **Insights** tab, select **Check claims**. — Bearly checks up to 12 of the figures the video states against web results each time you press it, and the **Claims** tab labels each by how many sites give the same figure, a different one, or neither. It never labels a figure true or false.

3. Take the disputed figures to the literature:

   ```text
   For the figures that sites disagreed on, what does the research literature say? Cite papers.
   ```

   — Bearly reads the saved check and searches papers, with a **Papers** card and citations.

4. Cut the video to those moments:

   ```text
   Make a cut of just the parts of the video where those disputed figures come up.
   ```

   — A cut card plays only those ranges, back to back, in the same player.



**Swap in:**
- **Web Search for Scholar:** when the figures are prices, sports results, or news rather than research, ask Bearly to check them against current news sources instead.
- **The Mentioned tab:** select **Find** on the papers, books, or tools the video names to look each one up.
- **Deep Research for one figure** (Analyst): send `/deep-research` on the single most important disputed figure for a cited report.
- **A spoken verdict:** ask for a 30-second recap and select **Read aloud** under the answer.

**If it stalls:**
- If the video has no readable captions, Bearly digests it by watching it (only the first 90 minutes of a longer video), so times are approximate, there are no exact quotes, and **Check claims** doesn't appear. In the desktop app, **Get transcript** on the card can fetch a transcript.
- Videos that block embedding can't play on the card. The desktop app offers **Download to play here**.

## Verify a viral post on X

You get a post's thread, replies, and quote posts read for you, its video watched, and a verdict backed by news sources.

**Why it works:** Read X Post returns the post, its thread, quote posts, photos, and frames from any video. Bearly can import the video as a file and watch up to 10 minutes of it at a time. Everything it found stays in the chat, so the last step can test it against a web search.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization controls: **X (Twitter)**, **Web**, and **File uploads** (for watching the video). The chat's **Web** setting on **Auto**.



1. Read the post and its reactions:

   ```text
   /read-x Is this real? https://x.com/USER/status/POST_ID Read the thread and the quote posts and tell me what people are disputing.
   ```

   — Bearly reads the post, a sample of up to 100 posts from its conversation with replies ranked by likes, and its quote posts, and links the posts it cites.

2. Watch the video:

   ```text
   Watch the attached video. Describe what it shows and anything that suggests where or when it was filmed.
   ```

   — Bearly imports the video into the chat and analyzes its frames and audio.

3. Check the news:

   ```text
   Now check news sources from the last 48 hours. Give me a verdict (confirmed, unconfirmed, or contradicted) with sources.
   ```

   — Bearly searches the web and answers with numbered source links.



**Swap in:**
- **Find the first poster:** send `/search-x` with the video's key phrase. Search covers roughly the last 7 days and accepts operators such as `from:` and `-is:retweet`.
- **Check the account:** `/x-profile` reads the poster's public profile: verification, links, metrics, and recent posts.
- **Deep Research for the news check** (Analyst) when the claim needs more than a quick search.
- **Send the verdict on:** ask Bearly to email you the verdict with its sources.

**If it stalls:** Bearly reads public posts only. Likes, DMs, bookmarks, and private posts aren't available, and it can't post or follow.

## Pressure-test a hard question across several models

You see how different models answer the same question, then get one synthesized answer from a panel of up to five models that each looked for evidence.

**Why it works:** The rerun menu under a message produces a new version of the answer from any model you pick, and the version arrows flip between versions without changing the chat's model. Council of Experts sends one self-contained question to two to five models at once; each can search the web and read pages, and Bearly writes a synthesis from their answers.
**You need:** **Pro** plan or higher for Council of Experts. Rerun works on every plan, with the models your plan includes. Any platform. Organization control: **Council**. If **Zero Data Retention only** is on, the panel uses only zero-retention models.



1. Ask the question:

   ```text
   Should a 20-person B2B software company switch from monthly to annual-only pricing? Recommend yes or no, give the three biggest risks, and say what evidence would change your mind.
   ```

   — The chat's model answers.

2. Under the answer, open the rerun menu (it shows the model's short name, or **Rerun**) and pick a different model. Repeat with a third model. — Each rerun adds a version. Use the arrows under the message to flip between "1 / 3", "2 / 3", and "3 / 3". The chat keeps its own model.

3. Put it to a panel:

   ```text
   /council Should a 20-person B2B software company switch from monthly to annual-only pricing? Each expert: search for recent pricing studies or public company data, recommend yes or no with the three biggest risks, and say where you disagree with common advice.
   ```

   — A **Council of Experts** panel shows a tab for each model with its status and answer. Bearly's synthesis follows in the reply.

4. Decide what to test:

   ```text
   Which disagreement from the council matters most for a company our size, and what's the cheapest way to test it?
   ```

   — Bearly works from the synthesis, which is now part of the chat.



**Swap in:**
- **Choose the panel:** the **Council settings** gear on the panel opens "Council models", where you pick who sits on it. The choice syncs to your account.
- **Your own documents for the web:** name an attached file in the council question with `@`. Experts can search and read it. See [Review a contract clause with a panel of models, then redline it](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/business-recipes.md#review-a-contract-clause-with-a-panel-of-models-then-redline-it).
- **Papers for web sources:** ask the experts to cite research papers. They can use Scholar.
- **Skip the panel:** on any plan, steps 1 and 2 alone give you three answers to compare.

**If it stalls:**
- Experts don't see the chat's earlier messages or files unless the question includes them, so write the council question so it stands on its own.
- A panel needs at least two experts to answer. If your organization allows fewer models, ask your admin.
- If a model in the rerun menu needs a higher plan, Bearly opens **Settings** → **Billing** instead.

## Queue follow-ups while a long answer runs

You line up the next three steps of an analysis while the first is still running, and they send themselves in order.

**Why it works:** While Bearly is working, anything you send waits in a queue of up to four messages and goes out when the current answer finishes. Each queued message sees everything before it, so a chart request can build on an analysis that hadn't finished when you typed it.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization controls: **File uploads** for the spreadsheet, **Run Python** and **Charts** to chart, and **Email notifications** to email.



1. Attach a spreadsheet and send:

   ```text
   Analyze churn by plan and by month in the attached file. Flag any month where churn doubled.
   ```

   — Bearly starts working.

2. While it's still working, type each of these and press <kbd>Enter</kbd> after each one:

   ```text
   Chart monthly churn by plan as a line chart.
   ```

   ```text
   Write a five-bullet summary for the leadership team.
   ```

   ```text
   Email me that summary.
   ```

   — The send button reads **Queue message**, and each message joins **Up next** above the composer.

3. Let the queue run, or select **Send now** on a row to stop the current answer and send that message right away. — Messages go out in order as each answer finishes. To change one, empty the composer and select **Edit queued message**; **Remove from queue** drops it.



**Swap in:**
- **Redirect instead of queue:** <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd> (<kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd> on Windows and Linux) sends your draft right away, stopping the current answer and skipping the queue.
- **A Page for the chart:** queue "Build a one-page Page with the chart and the summary" instead of the chart request.
- **A text when it's done:** queue `/sms Text me "churn analysis is ready".` Bearly texts the phone you verified in **Settings** → **Phone & SMS** (US and Canadian numbers).

**If it stalls:** The queue pauses when an answer fails ("Queue paused — the last message failed.") or you reach a usage limit; a successful answer or **Send now** resumes it. Pressing **Stop** twice within two seconds pauses it on purpose. The queue isn't saved with the chat, so keep the chat open until it's empty. It holds four messages; when it's full, send, edit, or remove one first.

# Recipes for writing, content, and media

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/creative-recipes/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/creative-recipes/)

> Step-by-step recipes that chain Bearly's image, video, text-to-speech, transcription, YouTube, grammar, and Run Python tools to make narrated clips, product visuals, show notes, trailers, quote cuts, and proofread drafts in one chat.

These recipes chain Bearly's image, video, audio, transcription, and writing tools. Each tool saves what it makes as a file in the chat (an image, a video, an audio take, a transcript), and the next tool picks that file up, so you can go from one picture to a narrated clip, or from an episode to show notes and a trailer, without leaving the chat. Each image, video, and audio run uses your plan allowance. For what each block takes in and gives back, see [Building blocks](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/building-blocks.md).

## Make a narrated product clip from one image

You get a generated product image, a short video animated from it, a recorded voiceover, and one MP4 with the voiceover laid over the video.

**Why it works:** **Imagine** saves each image as a file in the chat. **Make Video** can use any image file in the chat as its first frame, and every video it makes includes generated sound. A `/say` take is saved as an audio file. Run Python takes chat files as inputs, has video tools installed, and saves its output back to the chat, so it can combine the two files into one.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization controls: **Images**, **Video**, **Text to speech**, and **Run Python**.



1. Make the image:

   ```text
   /image A matte black insulated water bottle standing on wet slate, soft morning side light, shallow depth of field, vertical product photo.
   ```

   — The image opens in an editor inside the answer. To compare, run other models from the editor's buttons (**Fast**, **Good**, or **All models**); each click makes one more image.

2. Animate it:

   ```text
   /video Animate that image into an 8-second video: slow push-in, light rain starting, drops running down the bottle.
   ```

   — Make Video uses the image as the first frame, so the clip keeps the image's shape. The video card shows its length and "Audio". Play it before you go on.

3. Record the voiceover:

   ```text
   /say Write a 20-word voiceover for this clip and record it in a calm documentary voice.
   ```

   — Bearly writes the line and records it as an audio file. The **Voice** studio opens below it. For another take, choose **Video voiceover** under "What are you making?", try voices, and select **Generate**. Each take is saved as its own audio file in the chat.

4. Combine them:

   ```text
   /python Merge the voiceover onto the video as one MP4. Keep the rain sound underneath at low volume and start the voice at 1 second.
   ```

   — A **Run Python** step saves the merged MP4 as a file in the chat. Play it there or download it. If you made several takes, type `@` and pick the one you want under **Assets** so Bearly uses that file.



**Swap in:**
- **Your own photo for Imagine:** attach a product photo and start at step 2 with "Animate the attached photo…".
- **An end frame:** name a second image as the last frame. Make Video then animates from the first image to the second.
- **Subtitles:** send `/transcribe` on the merged clip, then select **Download subtitles** in the transcript viewer for an `.srt` file to upload with the clip.
- **A fix pass:** ask "Watch the clip and list anything inconsistent." Bearly views the video, and `/edit-video` can change a clip of 3 to 10 seconds under 50 MB.

**If it stalls:**
- Make Video doesn't retry on its own, and every attempt counts as usage. If one fails ("The video model could not finish this request. Try again later."), ask again or change the prompt.
- Videos run 4 to 15 seconds. If the voiceover is longer than the clip, ask for a shorter script or a longer video.
- Run Python runs are time-limited, so keep merges to short clips.

## Turn a talk into quotes, a short cut, and a spoken recap

From one YouTube link you get the three strongest arguments with exact quotes and timestamps, a cut that plays only those moments, and an audio recap you can download.

**Why it works:** A YouTube link opens a video card, and Bearly can look up the transcript by time or by words, so quotes come with timestamps. A cut plays chosen ranges inside the same player. `/say` turns text into an audio file in the chat, and the **Voice** studio lets you choose the voice and delivery.
**You need:** Any plan. Web app, phone browser, or desktop app. Organization controls: **YouTube**, **Web**, **Text to speech**, and **File uploads** (the transcript is saved as a file). The video needs readable captions for exact quotes.



1. Paste the link with your request:

   ```text
   https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VIDEO_ID Give me the three strongest arguments in this talk, each with an exact quote and its timestamp.
   ```

   — The video card opens, and Bearly answers with quotes you can check in the card's **Transcript** tab.

2. Cut to those moments:

   ```text
   Make a cut of just those three moments.
   ```

   — A cut card plays those ranges back to back. A cut doesn't create a new video file.

3. Record the recap:

   ```text
   /say Write a 45-second recap of the three arguments for someone who hasn't seen the talk, and record it.
   ```

   — Bearly writes and records the recap as an audio file, and the **Voice** studio opens below it. For a different voice, choose **Podcast intro** or **Audiobook**, try voices, and select **Generate**. Select **Download audio** on the take you want.



**Swap in:**
- **Ready-made cuts:** on a long enough video, the card offers 2, 5, and 10-minute cuts. Pick one of those instead of a custom cut.
- **Several talks:** paste up to three links in one message and ask which one makes the strongest case.
- **A Page for the audio:** ask for a Page that lists each quote with its timestamp and a one-line comment, then publish it (needs **Sharing**).
- **Check the numbers first:** if the talk leans on figures, select **Check claims** on the card before step 3. See [Check a video's figures against the research literature](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/research-recipes.md#check-a-videos-figures-against-the-research-literature).

**If it stalls:** Without readable captions, Bearly watches the video instead (up to its first 90 minutes), so times are approximate and there are no exact quotes. In the desktop app, **Get transcript** in the card's **Transcript** tab can fetch a transcript. Each range in a cut must be at least 5 seconds long.

## Turn an episode into show notes, subtitles, and a trailer

From one audio or video file you get a speaker-labeled transcript, show notes with chapter times and pull quotes, a subtitle file, and a recorded trailer.

**Why it works:** **Transcribe** times every word, shows each speaker in its viewer, and saves the transcript as an `.srt` file in the chat, so later messages can quote it with times. `/say` records any text as an audio file.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization controls: **Transcription**, **Text to speech**, and **File uploads**.



1. Attach the episode and send:

   ```text
   /transcribe Transcribe this episode.
   ```

   — The transcript viewer opens with **Captions** and **Table** views and a name box for each speaker, so you can type who is who. A transcript `.srt` file is saved in the chat.

2. Write the show notes:

   ```text
   Write show notes: a two-sentence summary, chapters with start times, and three pull quotes with exact timestamps.
   ```

   — Bearly works from the transcript.

3. Record a trailer:

   ```text
   /say Write a 20-second trailer built around the best pull quote and record it.
   ```

   — The take is saved as an audio file. In the **Voice** studio, choose **Podcast intro**, then select the **Compare** button (it shows how many voices it will record) to hear the same script in the preset's suggested voices at once.

4. In the transcript viewer, select **Download subtitles**. — You get an `.srt` subtitle file to upload with the video.



**Swap in:**
- **A meeting recording for the episode:** record in **Meetings and Recordings**, select **Add to chat**, and start at step 2 with the transcript file attached (needs **Meetings**).
- **An article:** ask for a 600-word article from the transcript, then send it through `/grammar` to proofread it.
- **A video from a link:** send `/download-video` with a public video's link to save it in the chat, then transcribe it. YouTube downloads need the desktop app.

**If it stalls:** Files over 400 MB can't be transcribed, so split long recordings. If one of several files fails, Bearly says "Some assets could not be transcribed" and keeps the others.

## Turn one product photo into a banner and a short video

You place your product into a new scene, change one part of it, widen it for a banner, and animate it into a clip.

**Why it works:** The image editor that opens with an image answer can combine images, change a selected area, and extend the edges. Each result is an image file in the chat, which **Make Video** can use as its first frame.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization controls: **Images**, **Video**, and **File uploads**. **Change area** and **Extend edges** need a model that accepts a selected area; models that can't show "No area edits".



1. Attach your product photo and a photo of the setting, then send:

   ```text
   /edit-image Combine these: put the lamp from the first photo on the desk in the second photo, matching the warm evening light.
   ```

   — **Touch Up** blends the two, and the result opens in the image editor.

2. In the editor's thumbnail strip, select the combined image. Choose **Change area**, drag a rectangle over the wall behind the desk (or use **Select center**), and type:

   ```text
   Replace this with a bookshelf, slightly out of focus.
   ```

   — Run it with a model that doesn't show "No area edits". The edit targets the selected area.

3. Select the new image in the thumbnail strip, choose **Extend edges**, describe what should fill the new space (for example, "continue the room and the desk"), and run it with a model that doesn't show "No area edits". — Bearly adds a quarter of the image's width or height on every side for the model to fill, leaving room for banner text.

4. Animate the final image:

   ```text
   /video Animate the latest image into a 6-second video: the lamp switches on and its light warms the room.
   ```

   — Make Video starts from that image, so the clip keeps its shape.



**Swap in:**
- **Imagine for the setting photo:** make the scene first with `/image`, then combine your product into it.
- **Several models at once:** each model button starts its own run, so you can compare results side by side in the thumbnail strip.
- **A better prompt:** select **Refine** in the editor for a suggested rewrite, then **Use this**.
- **A different shape of video:** the clip follows the starting image, so to get a vertical clip, start from a vertical image.

**If it stalls:** A failed run's thumbnail reads "Failed"; select it, then **Try again**. Editor runs use exactly the model you chose and don't switch to another. If a model can't take several images, it says "One input image" or "Up to N input images".

## Go from a mood board to an original concept image

You see real examples of a style from the web, then get a new image based on the one you pick.

**Why it works:** Image Search shows a gallery of web images with their source pages, but Bearly hasn't looked at the images themselves. When you ask it to use one, it saves that image into the chat with Read Site, where it becomes a file that **Touch Up** can use as a reference.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization controls: **Image search**, **Web**, **Chat web search**, and **Images**. The chat's **Web** setting on **Auto**.



1. Search for examples:

   ```text
   /image-search Show me examples of brutalist concrete desk lamps.
   ```

   — A gallery shows the first 8 images (expand for up to 20). Each tile has a preview, download, and a link to its source page.

2. Pick one to build on:

   ```text
   Use the third image as a reference and make a warmer product-photo version in pale oak on a plain background.
   ```

   — Bearly saves the third image into the chat, and Touch Up makes the new image in the editor.

3. Make variations:

   ```text
   Make two more versions: one in dark walnut, one in white ceramic.
   ```

   — Each version opens as a new image you can edit further.



**Swap in:**
- **Your sketch for the search:** attach a sketch and ask Touch Up to render it in the style of the saved image.
- **Video for a still:** animate the final image with `/video`.
- **Your own photo for a web image:** attach a photo of your product as a second reference, and say which image sets the style and which shows the product.

**If it stalls:** If the gallery doesn't appear, check that **Web** is on **Auto** in the sliders menu. With **Web** off, the chat can't search images or save one with Read Site. Images from a search belong to their owners; open the source page before you use one beyond reference.

## Hear your draft before you send it

You fix grammar and style in a draft, then listen to it to catch what reading misses.

**Why it works:** **Fix Grammar** marks each issue with an explanation, lets you accept changes one at a time or all at once, and copies the corrected text. `/say` with nothing after it opens the **Voice** studio, whose **Proofread by ear** preset sets a voice and delivery for checking writing.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization control: **Text to speech**.



1. Check the draft:

   ```text
   /grammar [paste your draft]
   ```

   — A grammar editor marks each issue by category and severity. Select **Accept All** or accept items one at a time, then **Copy corrected text**.

2. Open the studio:

   ```text
   /say
   ```

   — The **Voice** studio opens below the message without recording anything.

3. Paste the corrected text into the script box, choose **Proofread by ear**, and select **Generate**. — Play the take; words are highlighted as they're read. Fix anything that sounds wrong and generate again.



**Swap in:**
- **Use reply for pasting:** if Bearly wrote the draft, select **Use reply** in the studio instead of pasting.
- **Read aloud for the studio:** for a quick listen, select **Read aloud** under any answer that's all text.
- **Your studio voice everywhere:** turn on **Use for Read Aloud** in the studio so **Read aloud** uses the studio's voice and delivery.
- **Canvas for a long draft:** select **Edit in Canvas** under an answer, select a passage, and use "Ask AI about this text..." to rewrite just that part before step 1.

**If it stalls:** Bearly doesn't retry a failed take, because audio may already have been made. Select **Generate** again.

## Related

- [Images, video, and audio](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/images-video-audio.md) for every media tool, its options, and its limits
- [Videos and posts](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/videos-and-posts.md) for YouTube and X
- [Meetings](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/meetings.md) for recording and transcripts
- [Data and code](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code.md) for Run Python

title: "Recipes for founders, sales, and operations"
description: "Step-by-step recipes that chain Bearly's Domain research, SEO data, People Search, Council of Experts, Document editor, Run Python, charts, Pages, and email for naming a product, meeting prep, contact lists, contract redlines, shareable dashboards, and search-gap content plans."
canonical: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/business-recipes/"

---

# Recipes for founders, sales, and operations

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/business-recipes/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/business-recipes/)

> Step-by-step recipes that chain Bearly's Domain research, SEO data, People Search, Council of Experts, Document editor, Run Python, charts, Pages, and email for naming a product, meeting prep, contact lists, contract redlines, shareable dashboards, and search-gap content plans.

These recipes chain research, data, document, and email tools for work that usually takes several apps: naming a product, preparing for a meeting, reviewing a contract, or sending a dashboard to the board. Results from each step stay in the chat, so the last step can draw on all of them. Lookups that draw on outside data (domain checks, search data, work emails, and People Search) use your plan allowance. People Search asks before it searches for a list of people, looks up work emails, enriches leads, or builds a brief. For what each block takes in and gives back, see [Building blocks](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/building-blocks.md).

## Shortlist product names with open domains and search demand

You get name ideas, which domains are free to register, how often people search for each name, which names companies already use, and a one-page shortlist.

**Why it works:** Domain research suggests names and checks one domain per request. SEO data measures monthly search volume for up to 100 keywords at once. Web Search looks for companies already using a name. Every result stays in the chat, so the Page at the end can pull from all three.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization controls: **Domain research**, **SEO Data**, and **Web**. The chat's **Web** setting on **Auto** (the default), which Web Search needs.



1. Get ideas:

   ```text
   /domains Suggest 20 short, easy-to-spell names with .com or .ai domains for a scheduling app for dental clinics.
   ```

   — Bearly lists names with domains. A suggestion doesn't mean the domain is free.

2. Check the best ones:

   ```text
   Check availability for the best five.
   ```

   — Each domain comes back available, not available, or unknown.

3. Measure demand:

   ```text
   /seo-measure-keywords Show US monthly search volume for each of those five names and for "dental scheduling software".
   ```

   — The numbers come back in the reply. A name that people already search for a lot may belong to someone else.

4. Look for existing companies:

   ```text
   Search the web for companies already using any of these names in software or healthcare.
   ```

   — Web Search answers with numbered sources.

5. Put it together:

   ```text
   Put the shortlist in a one-page Page: name, domain status, monthly searches, conflicts found, and your recommendation with one reason.
   ```

   — A Page preview appears in the chat. **Open** shows it full size; download it or publish it with **Share file**.



**Swap in:**
- **Another market:** name the country and language in step 3, such as "UK search volume in English". Search data defaults to the United States and English.
- **Search terms for brainstorming:** start with `/seo-keywords` and your product description to see what people search for, and use those words as seeds in step 1.
- **Council of Experts for the final call** (Pro): send `/council` with the five names and your criteria in the question.
- **Email for the Page:** ask "Email me the Page." Send Email attaches the HTML file.

**If it stalls:** An "unknown" status is inconclusive and isn't retried automatically; ask again later. A domain check isn't trademark clearance, so check names with a lawyer before you commit.

## Prepare for a meeting with someone you haven't met

You find the right people at a set of companies, get their work emails, build a cited brief on the person you're meeting, and draft an opener.

**Why it works:** Find people returns a ranked table in the reply, so the next message can say "the top three" and Find work emails knows who you mean. People Search builds a brief on one person after you confirm the right candidate on a card. The brief stays in the chat, so the last step can write an email from it.
**You need:** **Analyst** plan or higher. Any platform. Organization controls: **People Search**, **People Search contact routes** (for emails and the **Reach out** section), and **Email notifications** (for the last step). Finding people, looking up emails, and building the brief each ask first; approve to continue.



1. Find the people:

   ```text
   /find-people Heads of platform engineering at Series B AI infrastructure companies in New York.
   ```

   — Bearly asks "Search for people?" before it spends anything. Approve it, and a ranked list comes back as a table.

2. Get their emails:

   ```text
   /work-emails Get work emails for the top three.
   ```

   — Bearly asks before the batch. Each email comes back verified, likely, or not found, and only found emails are charged.

3. Build the brief:

   ```text
   Build a brief on the second person. I'm meeting her Thursday to talk about our observability product.
   ```

   — Bearly shows a candidate card. If it lists more than one person, select the right one; Bearly never chooses for you. Approve **Build the brief?**, and a brief card appears with tabs and numbered citations to its sources.

4. Draft the opener:

   ```text
   Draft a short intro email to her that mentions one specific thing from the brief, and email the draft to me.
   ```

   — Send Email sends the draft to your own address so you can edit it and send it from your mail app.



**Swap in:**
- **Lead enrichment for Find people:** `/lead-enrichment` with up to 10 known profile links, emails, or name-and-employer pairs returns structured profile data for each.
- **Your own list for Find people:** attach a spreadsheet and run step 2 on it. See the next recipe.
- **A text for the email:** ask Bearly to text you the three things to remember from the brief before the meeting. Needs a verified phone in **Settings** → **Phone & SMS**.

**If it stalls:** With only a name, Bearly asks for one detail that tells people apart, such as employer, city, or role. If emails and the **Reach out** section are missing, your admin turned off **People Search contact routes**. Bearly doesn't retry a paid step on its own.

## Turn an event attendee list into a contact spreadsheet

Your attendee spreadsheet comes back as a CSV with each person's work email and how confident the match is.

**Why it works:** Find work emails needs a first name, last name, and company domain for each person, which Bearly reads from the file you attach. The results come back as a table in the reply, and Bearly can write that table to a CSV file in the chat, which you can edit and email to yourself.
**You need:** **Analyst** plan or higher. Any platform. Organization controls: **People Search**, **People Search contact routes**, **File uploads**, and **Email notifications**.



1. Attach the spreadsheet and send:

   ```text
   /work-emails Find work emails for the first 25 people in the attached list. Use the first name, last name, and company website columns.
   ```

   — Bearly asks before the batch. Approve it, and each person comes back verified, likely, or not found.

2. Write the CSV:

   ```text
   Write a CSV with my original columns plus email and status, in the same row order as my file.
   ```

   — The CSV appears as a grid showing its rows and columns. **Open** it to edit cells, add a row or column, and **Save** a new version.

3. Send it to yourself:

   ```text
   Email me the CSV.
   ```

   — Send Email attaches the file.



**Swap in:**
- **The next batch:** repeat step 1 with "the next 25 people". Each request takes up to 25.
- **Lead enrichment for emails:** `/lead-enrichment` adds role and company details for up to 10 people per request.
- **A chart for the list:** ask "Chart how many verified emails we found per company" to see where coverage is thin. Needs **Run Python** and **Charts**.

**If it stalls:** People without a company domain can't be looked up; ask Bearly to list them so you can fill the gaps. Only found emails are charged.

## Review a contract clause with a panel of models, then redline it

Two to five models review a clause against your contract and public sources, Bearly synthesizes their view, and you get a Word file with tracked changes and a comment on each change.

**Why it works:** Council of Experts doesn't pass the chat's files to its experts unless the question names them. Naming the contract with an `@` reference lets each expert search and read it, and each can search the web. The synthesis stays in the chat, so the Document editor can apply the edits most experts agreed on as tracked changes, and Send Email attaches the result.
**You need:** **Pro** plan or higher. Any platform. Organization controls: **Council**, **Web**, **File uploads**, **Run Python** (the Document editor runs on it), and **Email notifications**. The chat's **Web** setting on **Auto** (the default), so experts can search the web. A chat that isn't end-to-end encrypted, so experts can search the file.

> **Caution — Not legal advice**
> Bearly's review is a starting point, not legal advice. Have a lawyer check anything you sign.




1. Attach the contract as a Word file (.docx), so step 2 can add tracked changes. Type `/council`, then type `@` and pick the contract under **Assets** so it appears as a chip, and finish the question:

   ```text
   /council Using @contract.docx and current public guidance and case law, should we accept clause 7 (limitation of liability) as written? Each expert: cite sources, rate the risk, and list the exact wording changes you'd ask for.
   ```

   — A **Council of Experts** panel shows each model's answer as it finishes, then Bearly's synthesis follows in the reply.

2. Apply the edits:

   ```text
   /document Redline clause 7 in contract.docx with tracked changes that make the edits most experts agreed on. Add a comment on each change explaining why. Keep everything else unchanged.
   ```

   — A **Run Python** step edits the document and saves a new Word file in the chat. The preview shows it as a PDF; download the **Original** to see the tracked changes in Word.

3. Send it:

   ```text
   Email me the redlined contract with a three-line summary of the changes.
   ```

   — Send Email attaches the Word file.



**Swap in:**
- **Rerun for Council of Experts** (any plan): ask the clause question once, then use the **Rerun** menu to get second and third opinions from other models.
- **A form for a redline:** `/document` can also fill a PDF form, fillable or not, from details you give it.
- **A summary deck:** ask the Document editor for a three-slide PowerPoint that explains the changes for your board.
- **A restricted Page for the email:** publish the synthesis as a Page with the **People and teams** audience so only your legal team can open it.

**If it stalls:** If an expert says it can't see the contract, the question didn't name it; type `@` and pick the file. A panel needs at least two experts to answer. If the Document editor fails, expand **Run Python** and read **Error**, then ask again one change at a time.

## Turn a sales spreadsheet into a dashboard anyone can open

Your spreadsheet becomes a chart, an interactive dashboard Page with filters, and a link you can send to investors or the board.

**Why it works:** Run Python reads the attached file and writes a summary CSV. **Create chart** draws from that CSV. Bearly builds the summary into the Page itself, so the dashboard works on its own without the spreadsheet. Publishing gives the Page a stable link.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization controls: **File uploads**, **Run Python**, **Charts**, **Sharing**, and **Public links** (or **Share with people and teams** for a restricted link).



1. Attach the file and send:

   ```text
   Analyze the attached sales.xlsx by region and quarter. Show totals and quarter-over-quarter growth.
   ```

   — A **Run Python** step reads the file and returns the summary.

2. Chart it:

   ```text
   Chart quarterly revenue by region as a grouped bar chart.
   ```

   — A **Chart** card appears. **View data** shows the rows; **Download PNG** saves the image.

3. Build the dashboard:

   ```text
   Build an interactive dashboard Page with filters for region and quarter, a KPI row for total revenue and growth, and the bar chart.
   ```

   — A live Page preview appears. **Open** it and try the filters.

4. In the open Page, select **Share file** → **Prepare share**. When it's ready, the prepared Page opens on its own, marked "Ready to share"; select **Publish** in its header, choose **Anyone with the link** under **Audience**, select **Publish** again, then **Copy link**. — The Page is live at a bearly.sh link.



**Swap in:**
- **People and teams for Anyone with the link:** choose **People and teams** in step 4 and publish, then add people or teams with **Manage viewers**, which appears once the Page is published to that audience. Viewers sign in to open it.
- **A KPI with a goal:** ask "Show this quarter's revenue as a KPI chart against a 2 million dollar goal." A KPI chart shows one value with progress toward the goal.
- **A folder for an attachment** (desktop app): select **Work locally** → **Choose folder…**, pick the folder, and ask Bearly to attach the spreadsheet from there (up to 50 MB). Step 1 then works on that copy.
- **Next quarter's numbers:** attach the new file in the same chat and ask "Update the dashboard with this file." The Page saves a new version. Open it and select **Publish** → **Publish update**, and the link stays the same. If the popover asks you to use Share first, select **Share file** → **Prepare share** again.

**If it stalls:** A Page you share must be under 9 MB. If your organization allows neither audience, the popover says "Your team policy does not allow a publishing audience." Charts read up to 10 columns, so ask for a narrower summary if the table is wider.

## Find the search terms competitors win and you don't

You compare your site's search visibility with up to three competitors, size the gaps, see what kind of page ranks for each, and get a content plan as a CSV and a chart.

**Why it works:** SEO data tools return their numbers into the chat: site comparisons, keyword volumes for up to 100 terms, live results pages for up to five searches, and a one-page audit. Each step works from what the steps before it returned, and Run Python turns the final list into a CSV the chart draws from.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization controls: **SEO Data**, **Run Python**, and **Charts**.



1. Compare sites:

   ```text
   /seo-compare-sites Compare organic search visibility for oursite.com against rival-one.com and rival-two.com in the US.
   ```

   — Bearly returns visibility figures for each site.

2. Find the gaps:

   ```text
   Which keywords do they rank for that we don't? Measure monthly volume for the top 30.
   ```

   — Bearly lists the gaps with search volume.

3. See what wins:

   ```text
   /seo-serp Show the live results for the five biggest of those keywords and tell me what kind of page wins each one.
   ```

   — Bearly reads the results pages and describes the winning page types, such as comparison pages, guides, or tools.

4. Audit your closest page:

   ```text
   /seo-audit-page Audit oursite.com/pricing against what's winning for "scheduling software pricing".
   ```

   — Bearly audits that one page and lists what it's missing.

5. Make the plan:

   ```text
   Turn the gaps into a content plan CSV: keyword, volume, winning page type, our matching page or "none", and priority. Chart volume by priority as a bar chart.
   ```

   — Run Python writes the CSV and a **Chart** card draws it.



**Swap in:**
- **Deeper or quicker data:** ask for a quick look (up to 20 rows, or 10 results for a live results page) or a deep one (up to 100 rows).
- **Backlinks for visibility:** `/seo-backlinks` on a competitor shows who links to them.
- **Another market:** name the country and language; search data defaults to the United States and English.
- **A Page for the CSV:** ask for the plan as a Page and publish it for your team with the **People and teams** audience.

**If it stalls:** An audit reads one public address per request. If a step returns nothing for one site, ask again for that site alone.

## Related

- [Research](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/research.md) for Domain research, SEO data, People Search, and Council of Experts
- [Documents](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/documents.md) and [Data and code](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/data-and-code.md) for the Document editor, Run Python, and charts
- [Pages and Canvas](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md) for publishing and audiences
- [Email and text](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/email-and-text.md) for who you can email and attachment limits

title: "Recipes for automation and agents"
description: "Step-by-step recipes that combine Routines, Bearly Browser, Chrome control, Work locally, Computer Use, connectors, Deep Research, and email or text delivery, including what scheduled runs can and can't use and which steps need the desktop app."
canonical: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/automation-recipes/"
markdown: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/automation-recipes.md"
---

# Recipes for automation and agents

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/automation-recipes/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/automation-recipes/)

> Step-by-step recipes that combine Routines, Bearly Browser, Chrome control, Work locally, Computer Use, connectors, Deep Research, and email or text delivery, including what scheduled runs can and can't use and which steps need the desktop app.

These recipes use Bearly's agent tools: Routines that run on a schedule, Bearly Browser and your own Chrome, Work locally on a folder, Computer Use in desktop apps, connectors to your other apps, and email and text to reach you. Every recipe here needs the desktop app (Mac, Windows, or Linux) for at least one step. In a chat, Bearly Browser, Chrome, Work locally, Computer Use, and connectors ask before they act, unless you turn on Full access for that chat; a Routine gets its access when you save it in the editor. See [Permissions](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/permissions.md). For what each block takes in and gives back, see [Building blocks](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/building-blocks.md).

> **Note — What a scheduled run can use**
> A Routine run can search the web, read public pages, read X, search papers, read files in its chat, write files and Pages, email or text you, and use the connectors and skills you pick for it. When you turn them on in the routine's editor, it can also use Bearly Browser on sites you list (**Browser access**), run local commands (**Unattended local access**), and start one Deep Research run (the **Deep Research** plugin). It can't run Python, draw charts, make images, video, or audio, transcribe, watch YouTube, or use Best Pick, People Search, Maps, Weather, SEO data, Domain research, Council of Experts, Chrome, or Computer Use.


## Get a weekday digest from sites you sign in to

Every weekday morning a Routine reads the sites you choose in Bearly Browser, writes a summary into its own chat, and emails you only when your condition is met.

**Why it works:** A Routine is a saved prompt that runs in the desktop app on a schedule, and every run lands in the routine's chat. **Browser access** lets runs use Bearly Browser on the exact sites you list, with the sign-ins Bearly Browser already has. **Build on previous runs** lets each run see earlier runs, and the **Notification filter** checks each result against your condition and stays quiet when nothing meets it.
**You need:** **Pro** plan or higher. The desktop app, open on the device the routine is installed on when it's due to run; you can create and edit routines anywhere, but browser access can only be turned on from the desktop app. Organization controls: **Routines**, **Browser in Routines**, **Browser**, and **Email notifications**. On the device: **Settings** → **Desktop** → **Browser** → **Enable Browser** and **Allow chats to use Browser**.



1. In the desktop app, select the globe (**Open Browser**) in the title bar and sign in to each site. Or use **Settings** → **Desktop** → **Browser** → **Import browser cookies** to bring your current sign-ins over from Chrome or Edge. — Routine runs use these sign-ins. Import copies cookies only, never passwords, history, bookmarks, or autofill.

2. Open **Routines** in the sidebar and type in the **Create a routine** box:

   ```text
   Every weekday at 7:30 AM, open status.example.com and community.example.com and summarize what changed since the last run in five bullets with links. Only tell me if there's a new incident or a staff post.
   ```

   Select **Draft routine**. — The editor opens with a drafted name, instructions, and schedule (here **Weekdays** at 7:30 AM), and your "only tell me if" condition in **Notification filter**. Check each one before you save.

3. Under **Tools** → **Browser access**, turn on **Bearly Browser** and add each site under **Sites**, or select the suggestions under **From the instructions:**. Leave **Click, type, and submit** off. — Runs can read pages and follow links on exactly those sites. A subdomain counts as a separate site.

4. Under **Results and delivery**, turn on **Email each result** and keep **Build on previous runs** on. Select **Create routine**, then **Allow browser access and save** in the confirmation. — The confirmation lists what the routine can do on those sites while you're away. A routine you create in the desktop app is installed on that device.

5. Open the routine from the list and select **Run now**. — The run uses at most three background tabs and writes its summary into the routine's chat. When the result meets your condition, it emails you and shows a **Routine ran** notice with **Open**. When it doesn't, **Recent runs** marks the run **quiet** with the reason, and no notice appears.



**Swap in:**
- **A text for the email:** turn on **Text each result** for a short summary on your verified US or Canadian phone. Routine texts are limited to 3 a day.
- **Public sites only:** drop **Browser access**. Runs can search the web and read public pages without it.
- **A Page in the email:** add "Build a one-page Page comparing today with last week and attach it to the email" to the instructions. Runs can write Pages and attach them to email, but can't publish a link.
- **Sites that need a search box:** turn on **Click, type, and submit**. Runs can then take the actions your instructions ask for on those sites, including submitting forms, so keep the site list short.

**If it stalls:**
- "Runs go without a browser because the instructions or these settings changed after you approved them" means the routine changed after you approved its browser access. Select **Save** to approve it again.
- "It stopped at … because that site isn't approved for this routine" means a link led to another site. Add it under **Sites** if you trust it.
- Runs happen only while the desktop app is open on the installed device. After the device was away, the latest missed slot runs once. Two failures in a row pause the routine; resume it from **Routines**.
- After six quiet runs in a row, the seventh delivers a short check-in, so you know the routine is still working.

## Schedule a weekly Deep Research digest

Every Monday a Routine runs a Deep Research report on your topic and emails you the PDF.

**Why it works:** The **Deep Research** plugin lets each run start one Deep Research run at the tier you choose, without asking. The report lands in the routine's chat and its PDF is emailed to you. With **Build on previous runs** on, each run can see what last week's said.
**You need:** **Pro** plan for Routines, and **Analyst** or higher for a Standard Deep Research run (**Research** for **Ultra**). The desktop app, open on the installed device at run time. Organization controls: **Routines** and **Deep Research**.



1. Open **Routines** and type in **Create a routine**:

   ```text
   Every Monday at 8 AM, research new US regulations and enforcement actions affecting telehealth from the past week. Lead with what's new since last week's report.
   ```

   Select **Draft routine**. — The editor opens with a **Weekly** Monday schedule.

2. Under **Tools** → **Tools and skills**, open **Plugin** and choose **Deep Research** (marked "Uses plan allowance"). To change the tier, select the **Deep Research** chip and pick **Standard** or **Ultra** under **Tier**. — Each run can start one Deep Research run.

3. Keep **Build on previous runs** on and select **Create routine**. Open the routine from the list and select **Run now**. — When the report finishes it appears in the routine's chat, and the PDF arrives in your email.



**Swap in:**
- **Scholar for Deep Research** (Pro is enough): drop the plugin and ask for "new papers this week on …". Scholar runs in routines without a plugin.
- **X for Deep Research:** "Every Friday, find posts from the last 7 days that mention our company and summarize the sentiment." X reading works in routines.
- **A text for the email:** turn on **Text each result** for a short summary on your phone.

**If it stalls:** Only one Deep Research run per person can be in progress at a time, so don't start one in a chat while the routine is due. Your plan is checked on every run; if it no longer includes the tier you chose, the run can't use Deep Research.

## Turn a chat that worked into a Routine

One message turns a chat that already produced the result you want into a scheduled Routine.

**Why it works:** A chat can create routines when you ask it to. It keeps the task, asks one short question if the schedule is unclear, and shows a card confirming the saved routine. It adds email, text, connectors, browser access, local access, or paid research only when you ask for them.
**You need:** **Pro** plan or higher. Create from any platform; runs need the desktop app. Organization controls: **Routines**, plus **X (Twitter)** and **Email notifications** for this example.



1. Do the task once in a chat:

   ```text
   /search-x Find posts from the last 7 days that mention Acme Analytics, group them by sentiment, and quote the three most-liked.
   ```

   — Check that the answer is what you want every week.

2. Schedule it:

   ```text
   Do this every Friday at 4 PM and email me the result.
   ```

   — Bearly shows a card for the saved routine with its schedule and **Open in Routines**. Asked in the desktop app, the card says the routine is installed on that device; asked anywhere else, it says to open the desktop app to choose the device that runs it.

3. In the desktop app, select **Open in Routines** on the card (or open **Routines** and the routine). Check its instructions and that **Email each result** is on. If **Run location** doesn't say "Runs on this device.", select **Install on this device**. Then select **Run now** to test it.



**Swap in:**
- **A condition for the email:** add "Only tell me if a post gets over 500 likes" in step 2; it becomes the **Notification filter**.
- **A text for the email:** say "text me" instead of "email me". Texts need a verified phone in **Settings** → **Phone & SMS**.
- **The Routines page for the chat:** select **New Routine** in the command menu (<kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>K</kbd>, or <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>K</kbd> on Windows and Linux). It opens **Routines**, where you can build the routine in the editor and paste the prompt from step 1 into **Instructions**.

**If it stalls:** If the chat used a tool routines can't run (Python, charts, images, YouTube, Maps, Weather, Best Pick, People Search, and others in the note at the top), rewrite the routine's instructions to do without it.

## Turn a folder of invoices into a spreadsheet with formulas

Bearly reads every invoice in a folder on your computer, builds an Excel workbook with a summary sheet, and saves it back into the folder.

**Why it works:** Work locally lets a chat list, read, and copy files in a folder you choose. It copies each invoice into the chat as a file, where the Document editor (which runs on Run Python) can read them and write an Excel workbook with formulas. Bearly then saves the workbook from the chat back to your folder.
**You need:** Any plan. Desktop app. Organization controls: **Work locally** and **Run Python**. In Standard permissions, commands, file changes, and saves ask first; attaching a file from inside the chosen folder doesn't. On an approval card, pick **This chat** instead of **Once** so the same kind of action doesn't ask again in this chat on this device.



1. In the composer, select **Work locally** → **Choose folder…** and pick the folder with the invoices. — The chip shows the folder's name.

2. Bring the invoices in:

   ```text
   List the PDF invoices in this folder and attach them all to the chat.
   ```

   — Approve the card for the command that lists the folder; it shows the command, and **Details** shows the folder it runs in. Each invoice then appears as a file in the chat (up to 50 MB each).

3. Build the workbook:

   ```text
   /document Build an Excel workbook from these invoices: one row per invoice with vendor, invoice number, invoice date, due date, subtotal, tax, and total; a second sheet with totals by vendor using formulas; and flag any invoice that's past due as of today.
   ```

   — A **Run Python** step builds the workbook and saves it as a file in the chat. The preview shows it as a PDF; download the **Original** to open it in Excel.

4. Save it to the folder:

   ```text
   Save the workbook to this folder as invoices-2026-09.xlsx.
   ```

   — Approve the save card if it asks. The file appears in your folder.



**Swap in:**
- **The web app for Work locally:** attach the invoices with **+** → **Attach files**, start at step 3, and download the workbook instead of saving it.
- **Scanned invoices:** say they're scans in step 3. The Document editor can read them with text recognition.
- **A chart:** add "Chart spend by vendor as a bar chart" after step 3.
- **An email for the folder:** ask "Email me the workbook." Send Email attaches files up to 20 MB in total.

**If it stalls:** Run Python saves up to five files per run, so ask for extra outputs in a separate step. If you deny a local action, Bearly doesn't retry the same request; ask again to get a new card. The working folder is context, not a sandbox: commands can reach anything your user account can.

## Read numbers from a desktop app and chart them

Bearly reads values from an app window on your screen, charts them, and points out the outliers.

**Why it works:** Computer Use runs a focused agent that looks at screenshots and accessibility text of the apps on screen, and clicks, scrolls, and types. Computer use runs on your device; the screenshots you approve go to the model you picked. The agent reports what it read back to the chat, where Run Python and **Create chart** take over; the Computer Use agent itself can't run Python.
**You need:** Any plan. Desktop app on Mac, Windows, or Linux. Organization controls: **Computer Use** (with a model policy that allows a model Computer Use supports), plus **Run Python** and **Charts** for the chart. On a Mac, Bearly needs Accessibility and Screen Recording permission (System Settings → Privacy & Security).



1. Open the app with the numbers, for example the Reports window of your accounting app, and leave it visible.

2. Ask Bearly to read it:

   ```text
   Read the twelve monthly totals from the Reports window in the accounting app that's open. Scroll if you need to.
   ```

   — Bearly asks **Use your computer**; approve it. A **Computer** chip in the composer shows what it's doing, with **Stop**. The answer lists the totals, and a **Computer Use** step records the actions it took.

3. Chart them:

   ```text
   Chart them as a line chart and tell me which months are outliers and by how much.
   ```

   — Run Python writes a CSV and a **Chart** card draws it.



**Swap in:**
- **Screen sharing for Computer Use** (works in the web app too): select **+** → **Share your screen** and pick the window. Each message you send carries a fresh capture, so Bearly can read the numbers, but it can't click or scroll.
- **Work locally for the chart:** add Work locally and ask Bearly to save the CSV into a folder.
- **Typing for reading:** ask Computer Use to enter the totals into a spreadsheet app. Before anything consequential, such as sending or deleting, it asks **Confirm this computer action**.

**If it stalls:** Computer Use can't work in Bearly's own windows and runs one task per chat at a time. If it says it needs Accessibility and Screen Recording access, turn both on for Bearly and try again. On Windows, finish the optional helper setup if Bearly offers it.

## Find and fix a bug in a local web app

Bearly opens your app in Bearly Browser, reproduces the problem, fixes the code in your project folder, and reloads to confirm.

**Why it works:** Bearly Browser can open `localhost` addresses and read and click pages. Work locally gives the same chat access to your project folder, so what Bearly sees on the page leads straight to the file it changes.
**You need:** Any plan. Desktop app. Organization controls: **Browser** and **Work locally**. **Settings** → **Desktop** → **Browser** → **Enable Browser** and **Allow chats to use Browser**.



1. Start your app's development server yourself, in your own terminal. — Bearly's local commands can't keep a server running.

2. In the composer, select **Work locally** → **Choose folder…** and pick the project folder.

3. Reproduce the bug:

   ```text
   Open http://localhost:3000/settings in Bearly Browser and tell me why the Save button does nothing.
   ```

   — Bearly asks "Let this chat use this site?"; approve it. It reads the page and clicks in a background tab. Select **Open Browser** to watch, or **Take over this tab** to drive it yourself.

4. Fix it:

   ```text
   Find the cause in this folder, fix it, then reload the page and confirm Save works.
   ```

   — Approve the read and edit cards. Bearly changes the file and reloads the page to check.



**Swap in:**
- **Chrome for Bearly Browser:** with Chrome control connected (**Settings** → **Desktop** → **Chrome Extension**), ask for console errors and failed network requests. Reading the console asks **Read your Chrome tabs**; inspecting network requests asks **Use Chrome DevTools**.
- **Point at the problem:** in Bearly Browser, use **Capture and annotate page**, click the element, type what's wrong, and select **Attach**.
- **Full access for approvals:** in **Permissions**, choose **Full access** so this chat uses its enabled tools without asking. Read the dialog first; it applies to this chat on this device.

**If it stalls:** A local command times out after 2 minutes by default and 10 at most. Bearly Browser keeps up to eight tabs open across chats.

## Research competitors from a brief open in Chrome

Bearly reads a document open in your signed-in Chrome, runs Deep Research on competitors that match it, and compares them with your brief.

**Why it works:** Chrome control lets the desktop app read your regular Chrome tabs, with your sign-ins. Deep Research takes a text question, so the chat carries what it read from Chrome into the research query, and the finished report stays in the chat for the comparison.
**You need:** **Analyst** plan or higher. Desktop app, with Chrome and the Bearly extension connected. Organization controls: **Chrome extension browser** and **Deep Research**.



1. In the desktop app, open **Settings** → **Desktop** → **Chrome Extension**. Select **Install Chrome extension** if it's not installed, and keep Chrome and Bearly open until it shows **Connected to Chrome**.

2. Open the brief in Chrome, then ask:

   ```text
   Read the product brief open in my Chrome tab and list its target customer and three must-have features.
   ```

   — Bearly asks to **Read your Chrome tabs**; approve it.

3. Research the market:

   ```text
   /deep-research Find competitors that serve that customer with all three features. Include pricing and recent funding.
   ```

   — Check that the card's **Query** names the customer and the three features, and edit it if not. Select **Create Plan**, review the plan, then **Run Research**.

4. Compare:

   ```text
   Compare each competitor in the report against our brief in a table: feature by feature, price, and where we're ahead or behind.
   ```

   — Bearly works from the finished report and the brief.



**Swap in:**
- **Bearly Browser for Chrome:** sign in to the site in Bearly Browser and ask Bearly to read the page there.
- **Web Search for Deep Research** (any plan): ask for the competitor scan in a normal message instead of `/deep-research`.
- **A Page for the table:** ask for the comparison as a Page and publish it with the **People and teams** audience.

**If it stalls:** Chrome control works with Chrome itself, not other browsers. Chrome shows its debugging banner while Bearly is attached to a tab. Routines can't use Chrome.

## Email yourself a weekly report from a connected app

Every Friday a Routine pulls the week's closed issues from a connected app, writes them to a CSV, and emails you a summary with the file attached.

**Why it works:** Connectors add a service's tools to chats and Routines. A routine uses only the connectors you add to it, and **Read-only** limits it to that connector's read tools. Runs can write CSV files into the routine's chat and attach them to the result email.
**You need:** **Pro** plan or higher. The desktop app, open on the installed device, with the connector connected on that device. Organization controls: **Routines**, **MCP Connectors**, and **Email notifications**.



1. Open **Settings** → **Connectors**. Under **Built-in connectors**, select **Add** next to Linear, then **Connect** and sign in. — A green dot appears next to Linear, and its button changes to **Disconnect**.

2. Test it in a chat. Open the **Connectors** chip in the composer, select Linear so it shows **Active**, and send:

   ```text
   List issues closed this week by the Platform team, grouped by project.
   ```

   — Bearly asks **Allow Linear?**; approve it.

3. Open **Routines** and type in **Create a routine**:

   ```text
   Every Friday at 4 PM, list issues closed this week by the Platform team in Linear, grouped by project. Write them to a CSV and email me a short summary with the CSV attached.
   ```

   Select **Draft routine**.

4. Under **Tools** → **Tools and skills**, open **Plugin** and choose Linear under **Connectors**. Select the Linear chip and set **Tools** to **Read-only**. Turn on **Email each result** and select **Create routine**. Open the routine from the list and select **Run now**. — The run uses Linear without asking, writes the CSV into the routine's chat, and emails it to you.



**Swap in:**
- **Another connector for Linear:** Notion, GitHub, and most other built-in connectors work the same way, as does a server you add with **Add custom MCP server**. The **Plugin** menu lists only the connectors routines can use.
- **A skill for the formatting:** add a skill under **Plugin** → **Skills** and turn on its **Allow scripts** to run its scripts unattended.
- **A text for the email:** turn on **Text each result**.

**If it stalls:** A routine uses only the connectors added to it, and each must be connected on the device the routine runs on. In Standard mode, a run uses its selected connectors without you; any other action that needs approval, beyond what you allowed in the editor, is skipped and the run continues without it.

## Related

- [Routines](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/routines.md) for the editor, schedules, limits, and delivery
- [Browser and computer](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/browser-and-computer.md) for Bearly Browser, Chrome control, and Computer Use
- [Connectors and skills](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/connectors-and-skills.md) for adding services and skills
- [Permissions](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/permissions.md) for approval cards, Standard, and Full access
