# Recipes for everyday life

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/everyday-recipes/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/everyday-recipes/)

> Step-by-step recipes that chain Bearly's Maps, Weather, Best Pick, YouTube, Scholar, voice calls, text to speech, Run Python, Pages, and email for rides, trips, shopping, learning, reading health research, language practice, and splitting bills.

These recipes use Bearly for plans and decisions outside work: a ride, a day in a new city, a purchase, a lecture, a language, a question about your health. Maps, Weather, YouTube, Scholar, and the **Learning** style work on every plan; Best Pick needs Pro. A plan Bearly makes is a starting point, so check opening hours, prices, and conditions before you go. For what each block takes in and gives back, see [Building blocks](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/building-blocks.md).

## Plan a bike ride around the weather

You get a cycling route with a coffee stop along the way, the afternoon's rain, wind, and air quality, and a start time that avoids the worst of it.

**Why it works:** Maps finds routes by bike, on foot, by transit, or by car, and can search for places along a route. Weather reads the forecast and, when you ask, air quality. Both results stay in the chat, so the last step can weigh one against the other.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization controls: **Maps** and **Weather**. Name your start point, or allow location access when your browser or device asks.



1. Ask for the route:

   ```text
   Plan a Saturday bike ride from Golden Gate Park to Sausalito with a coffee stop along the way.
   ```

   — Bearly finds a cycling route, searches along it for coffee, and shows a map card with the route and the stops on the way.

2. Check the conditions:

   ```text
   Will it rain or be windy along that route on Saturday afternoon? Check air quality too.
   ```

   — A weather card shows the hourly forecast. Air quality is included because you asked for it.

3. Adjust the plan:

   ```text
   If the wind picks up after 2 PM, move the ride earlier. Give me a start time and the coffee stop's opening hours.
   ```

   — Bearly reads the place's details and gives you one plan.



**Swap in:**
- **Walking or transit for cycling:** say "walking" or "by transit"; transit routes take an arrival or departure time.
- **Pollen for air quality:** ask for the pollen forecast with `/pollen` if allergies matter more.
- **Several starting points:** "My friends start from Mission, Sunset, and Oakland. Who has the shortest ride to the café?" Bearly compares routes from each origin.
- **A text for the plan:** ask "Text me the plan." Needs a verified phone in **Settings** → **Phone & SMS**.

**If it stalls:** If Bearly uses your location, it says when it's estimated from your network rather than your device; name the start point to be exact. Maps and Weather don't run in Routines.

## Pick the right thing to buy, then send it to yourself

You get one product recommendation backed by expert and owner reviews, refine it with a tap, see when its price was checked, and email it to yourself.

**Why it works:** Best Pick researches reviews and store listings and answers with exactly one product and a plain store link. Its chips (**Cheaper**, **Worth spending more**, **Not this one**) each send an ordinary follow-up message, so the pick narrows while the earlier requirements stay. The final card stays in the chat, so the email step can use it.
**You need:** **Pro** plan or higher. Any platform. Organization controls: **Best Pick**, **Web**, **Chat web search**, and, for the last step, **Email notifications**.



1. Describe what you need, including where it ships:

   ```text
   What's the best cordless stick vacuum under 400 dollars for a home with two cats? Ship to Vancouver.
   ```

   — A Best Pick card shows the product, its price in the local currency, **View at** the store, and why it won. Naming Vancouver makes Bearly shop Canadian stores.

2. Refine it: select **Cheaper** on the card, or type a requirement:

   ```text
   Quieter, and it has to have a washable filter.
   ```

   — A new card shows the pick that fits, and the reply says whether it changed and why.

3. Read the line next to **View at**: "Price checked" with the time, and "Store's checkout price applies". — If you come back to the card more than a day later, the price shows as "Price on" a date, and the card can offer **Check current price** to look it up again.

4. Send it to yourself:

   ```text
   Email me this pick with its price, the store, and the spend-less option from the card.
   ```

   — Send Email sends it to your own address. Bearly doesn't receive the store's web address, so the email names the store; open it from **View at** on the card.



**Swap in:**
- **Worth spending more for Cheaper:** see what the extra money buys.
- **Not this one:** rules the current pick out and finds the next best.
- **A text for the email:** ask "Text me the pick."
- **A different country:** "Show me stores in the UK instead." Bearly searches again in that country's currency, so say your budget again in it.

**If it stalls:** A pick stops after about 90 seconds; select **Try again**. "Limited store coverage" means Bearly has less store data for that country, so it checks fewer stores there. Best Pick doesn't cover medications, supplements, infant car seats, firearms, services, travel, cars, homes, or used items.

## Plan a day in a new city as a Page you can carry

You get a timed walking plan with places chosen from recent reviews, rearranged around the weather, as a Page on your phone.

**Why it works:** Maps searches places, reads their reviews, and times routes between them; Weather gives the hourly forecast for the day. Bearly writes the plan as a Page, which is an HTML file in the chat, and Send Email can attach that file, which opens in a browser on your phone.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization controls: **Maps**, **Weather**, and **Email notifications**.



1. Find the places:

   ```text
   I have one day in Lisbon next Saturday. Find six must-see places around Alfama and Baixa with strong recent reviews, plus a lunch spot locals like.
   ```

   — Bearly searches places and reads their reviews, then lists them with what people praise and complain about.

2. Put them in order:

   ```text
   Order them into a walking route starting at 9 AM from Rua Augusta 100. Use transit for any leg over 25 minutes on foot, and give me times for each stop.
   ```

   — Bearly times each leg and gives you a time for each stop.

3. Plan around the weather:

   ```text
   Check Saturday's hourly forecast and move indoor stops into any rainy hours.
   ```

   — A weather card shows the hours; the plan changes if rain is forecast.

4. Make it a Page:

   ```text
   Make this a one-page Page that works on a phone: a timeline, a map link for each stop, opening hours, and a checkbox for each stop.
   ```

   — A Page preview appears; **Open** it to try it.

5. Send it to your phone:

   ```text
   Email me the Page.
   ```

   — Send Email attaches the HTML file. Open the attachment on your phone.



**Swap in:**
- **A published link for the email:** in the open Page, select **Share file** → **Prepare share**. When it's ready, the prepared Page opens on its own, marked "Ready to share"; select **Publish** in its header, choose the **Audience**, select **Publish** again, then **Copy link**. Open the link on your phone or send it to the people you're traveling with.
- **Air quality or pollen for the forecast:** ask for them in step 3.
- **Best Pick for a purchase on the trip** (Pro): "What's the best compact travel umbrella I can buy before Saturday?"

**If it stalls:** A plan built on a forecast changes with it, so ask Bearly to check the weather again the day before. Check marks on the Page may not be kept after you close it.

## Learn a lecture's ideas, then quiz yourself

You get a step-by-step explanation of a YouTube lecture, a quiz Page with instant feedback, and a cut of the parts you got wrong.

**Why it works:** The **Learning** style tells Bearly to teach step by step, with analogies, common misconceptions, and a takeaway. A YouTube link gives Bearly the lecture's transcript with timestamps, so the quiz can point to the moment each answer is explained, and a cut can replay just those moments. A Page can be an interactive quiz.
**You need:** Any plan. Web app, phone browser, or desktop app. Organization controls: **YouTube**, **Web**, and **File uploads** (the transcript is saved as a file in the chat). The lecture needs readable captions for timestamps.



1. Open the sliders menu in the composer and set **Style** to **Learning**.

2. Paste the lecture:

   ```text
   https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VIDEO_ID Teach me the core idea of this lecture step by step. Assume I know high-school math.
   ```

   — The video card opens, and Bearly explains in the **Learning** style.

3. Make the quiz:

   ```text
   Using the transcript, make a 10-question quiz Page with instant feedback and, for each answer, the timestamp where the lecture explains it.
   ```

   — A Page preview appears; **Open** it and take the quiz.

4. Rewatch what you missed:

   ```text
   I got questions 3 and 7 wrong. Make a cut of the parts of the lecture that explain them.
   ```

   — A cut card plays just those parts.



**Swap in:**
- **Flashcards for a quiz:** ask for a flashcard Page you can flip through.
- **Scholar for depth:** ask `/scholar` for the papers behind the lecture's main claim.
- **An audio review:** ask for a two-minute spoken summary with `/say` and listen on a walk.
- **Your own notes for a video:** attach lecture slides or notes instead of a link; Bearly reads the file and makes the quiz from it, without timestamps or a cut.

**If it stalls:** Without captions, Bearly watches the video instead, so timestamps are approximate. Quiz progress on a Page may not be kept after you close it.

## Read the research on a health question

You get what systematic reviews and trials say about a health question, in plain language with citations, and a list of questions to take to your doctor.

**Why it works:** Scholar searches biomedical and preprint literature, prefers systematic reviews for medical questions, flags retractions and conflicting results, and says when the evidence is thin. The **Papers** card under each search stays in the chat, so you can open the papers behind the plain-language summary.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization controls: **Scholar** and **Web**.

> **Caution — Information, not medical advice**
> Bearly summarizes published research. It isn't medical advice and doesn't know your history. Talk to a doctor or another qualified clinician before you start, stop, or change any treatment.




1. Ask Scholar:

   ```text
   /scholar What do systematic reviews and randomized trials say about cognitive behavioral therapy for insomnia compared with sleep medication, over six months or longer?
   ```

   — Bearly answers with superscript citations and a **Papers** card listing the sources.

2. Go deeper on the strongest evidence:

   ```text
   Read the two strongest reviews and tell me the effect sizes, how many people were studied, and how certain the authors are.
   ```

   — Bearly reads passages from those papers. Some show "abstract only" when the full text isn't available.

3. Make it usable:

   ```text
   Explain this in plain language, say where the evidence is weak, and list five questions I could bring to my doctor.
   ```

   — Bearly rewrites the findings from the papers it already read. Open each paper from the **Papers** cards above.



**Swap in:**
- **Learning for the default style:** set **Style** to **Learning** in the sliders menu first for a slower, step-by-step explanation.
- **A video for a question:** paste a health video's YouTube link, select **Check claims** on the card, then ask what the research says about the disputed figures.
- **Deep Research for Scholar** (Analyst): for a wider cited report drawn from web sources.
- **Email for later:** "Email me this summary with the title of each paper."

**If it stalls:** If Scholar finds little, it says the literature is thin rather than stretching it. Try broader terms or a wider date range.

## Practice a language out loud, then get corrections

You hold a spoken conversation in another language, then get a list of your mistakes with corrections and hear the corrected sentences read slowly.

**Why it works:** A voice call gets the chat's recent messages as background, so it can pick up a role you set up in text. Only the call's transcript is saved, never audio, and later typed messages can read it. `/say` turns the corrected sentences into an audio file you can replay.
**You need:** Any plan. Choosing the voice model (**Settings** → **Chats** → **Voice** → **Model**) needs Pro; Free uses a fixed model. A microphone and a browser or app that supports calls (Firefox doesn't). Organization controls: **Voice** and **Text to speech**.



1. Set the scene:

   ```text
   Let's practice Spanish. When I start a voice call, play a waiter at a café in Madrid. Speak only Spanish, keep your sentences short, and don't correct me during the call.
   ```

   — Bearly confirms the role.

2. Select **Start voice call** (the sound-wave button in the composer, near send), order a coffee and a pastry, and ask for the bill. Select **End call** when you're done. — A call card appears in the chat with the duration and **Show transcript**.

3. Get the corrections:

   ```text
   From the call transcript, list each mistake I made with the correction and a one-line explanation.
   ```

   — Bearly works from the saved transcript.

4. Hear it right:

   ```text
   /say Read the corrected sentences slowly, with a pause between each one.
   ```

   — Bearly records the audio as a file in the chat. In the **Voice** studio, try "Clear and measured" for the delivery and select **Generate** for another take.



**Swap in:**
- **Another language or setting:** a job interview in French, a pharmacy visit in Japanese.
- **A flashcard Page for the list:** ask for a Page that quizzes you on the corrected sentences.
- **Typed practice for a call:** set **Settings** → **Chats** → **This Chat** → **Language** so Bearly writes its replies in that language.

**If it stalls:** A call ends after 30 seconds of silence. In Firefox the call button says voice is unavailable; use Chrome or the desktop app. If the button is missing, check that your organization allows **Voice**.

## Split a restaurant bill from a photo

You photograph a receipt and get each person's exact share with tip, then a Page where you can reassign items and change the tip.

**Why it works:** Bearly reads the photo, and Run Python does the arithmetic, so the shares add up to the total. A Page can hold that receipt as an interactive calculator.
**You need:** Any plan. **Take photo** is on phones and tablets; anywhere else, attach a photo of the receipt. Organization controls: **File uploads** and **Run Python**.



1. Tap **Take photo**, photograph the receipt, and send:

   ```text
   Split this bill. Ana had the pasta and a glass of wine, Ben had the steak, and we shared the calamari. Add an 18% tip on the pre-tax total. Use Python for the math.
   ```

   — Bearly lists the items it read, and a **Run Python** step computes each share.

2. Make it adjustable:

   ```text
   Make a Page where I can tick who had each item and change the tip with a slider.
   ```

   — A Page preview appears; **Open** it and move the slider.



**Swap in:**
- **Currency conversion:** "Also show each share in euros at today's rate." Bearly looks up the rate with Web Search.
- **A trip's worth of receipts:** attach several photos and ask for a CSV of every item with who paid, then a settle-up table.
- **A text for the Page:** ask "Text me each person's total."

**If it stalls:** If an item or price is read wrong, say which one and the right amount; Bearly recalculates. Retake blurry photos in good light.

## Related

- [Research](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/research.md) for Maps, Weather, Best Pick, and Scholar
- [Videos and posts](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/videos-and-posts.md) for YouTube cards and cuts
- [Voice](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/voice.md) for voice calls and dictation

---
title: "Recipes for teams and projects"
description: "Step-by-step recipes that combine projects, Always Search Documents, meeting transcripts, project sharing and locked settings, organization policies, Memories and chat management, Prompt Templates, email to teammates, and restricted Pages for shared knowledge bases, meeting recaps, and team rollouts."
canonical: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/team-recipes/"
markdown: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/team-recipes.md"
---

# Recipes for teams and projects

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/team-recipes/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/team-recipes/)

> Step-by-step recipes that combine projects, Always Search Documents, meeting transcripts, project sharing and locked settings, organization policies, Memories and chat management, Prompt Templates, email to teammates, and restricted Pages for shared knowledge bases, meeting recaps, and team rollouts.

These recipes use projects, meetings, sharing, and organization policies to make Bearly work the same way for a whole team. A project's files, instructions, and settings apply to every chat in it, so what one person adds, everyone in the project can use. Sharing a project needs an organization; emailing teammates and organization policies need a managed organization. For what each block takes in and gives back, see [Building blocks](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/building-blocks.md).

## Build a searchable library of customer calls

Every call you record goes into one project, and any chat in that project answers from the transcripts first, with quotes.

**Why it works:** **Add to project** uploads a meeting's transcript to a project's files as a document. **Always Search Documents** in the project's chat settings makes every chat in the project search the project's files before it answers, so a question gets quotes from the calls without anyone naming a file.
**You need:** Any plan. Recording works on every platform with a microphone; recording an online meeting's audio needs the desktop app on Windows or macOS 13 or later. Organization controls: **Meetings**, **File uploads**, **Project documents and data**, and **Create projects**. Writer access to the project. Chats that aren't end-to-end encrypted.



1. In the sidebar, select **New project** in the **Projects** header, enter "Customer calls" as the **Project Name**, and select **Create Project**. — The project's settings page opens.

2. Turn on **Project Chat Settings**, then turn on **Always Search Documents**. In **Instructions**, enter:

   ```text
   Answer from the call transcripts first. Quote customers with the call title and timestamp. Say when the transcripts don't cover a question.
   ```

   — Every chat in the project now follows these instructions and searches its files first.

3. Open **Meetings and Recordings** in the sidebar. Choose **Online meeting** (desktop app) or **In person or voice note**, then **Start recording**, and **Stop recording** when the call ends. — After the upload, Bearly transcribes the whole recording again with better speaker labels. Name each speaker once and every matching turn updates.

4. Select **Add to project** and choose Customer calls. — "Transcript added" confirms it. Repeat steps 3 and 4 after each call.

5. Start a chat in the project and ask:

   ```text
   What did customers say about onboarding this month? Group it by theme and quote them with the call and timestamp.
   ```

   — Bearly searches the transcripts and answers with citations you can click to open the transcript they came from.



**Swap in:**
- **A synced folder for recordings** (desktop app): in the project's settings, use **Local Directory Sync** → **Add Directory** to keep a folder of notes or exported transcripts synced into the project.
- **Uploads for recordings:** open **Docs & Data** and select **Upload** for call notes, PDFs, or contracts.
- **Your team for just you:** share the project (see the next recipe) as **Writer**, so teammates can start their own chats in it.
- **Past chats as well as files:** set **Memories** to **Project Only** in the sliders menu, and ask with `/recall` to search earlier chats in the project.

**If it stalls:** **Always Search Documents** does nothing in end-to-end encrypted chats, which can't search documents. **Add to project** needs write access. A recording stops at 1 hour 15 minutes and keeps everything up to then. If the live transcript pauses at your account limit, the audio keeps recording.

## Set up a shared research desk with locked settings

Your team works in one project where every chat uses the same model, style, instructions, and source files, whoever starts it.

**Why it works:** A project's chat settings apply to every chat in it, and each field can be locked so members can't change it. Sharing the project with a group gives everyone in the group the same project, files, and settings. Members' own settings can change only the fields you leave unlocked.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. You own the project and belong to an organization that has a group. Organization controls: **Create projects**, **Project collaboration**, **Project documents and data**, and **File uploads**.



1. Create a project named "Market desk". Open **Docs & Data** and select **Upload** for your style guide and source list.

2. Open the project's **Settings** and turn on **Project Chat Settings**. Choose the **Model**, set **Response Style** to **Formal**, turn on **Always Search Documents**, and enter **Instructions**:

   ```text
   Write for the strategy team. Lead with the answer, cite every number, prefer primary sources, and follow the style guide in this project.
   ```

   Use the lock next to each field so members can't change it. — Locked fields apply to every chat in the project.

3. In the **Collaboration** row, select **Share**. Under **Groups**, set your strategy group's role to **Writer**, then select **Done**. — The **Collaboration** row shows **Shared**, and the settings card becomes **Shared Project Settings**.

4. A teammate starts a chat in the project and asks:

   ```text
   Summarize this week's pricing moves by our top three competitors.
   ```

   — The chat uses the locked model, style, and instructions, and searches the project's files before it answers.



**Swap in:**
- **Reader for Writer:** readers can view chats and files and take part in existing chats, but can't start new chats or change files.
- **The whole organization for a group:** under **Organizations** in the share panel, share with everyone in your organization.
- **Skills for instructions:** add a skill under the project's **Skills** section so every chat in the project can use it.
- **Personal settings for locks:** leave a field unlocked, and each member can set their own value under **Personal Chat Settings**.

**If it stalls:** The **Collaboration** row appears only for the project's owner, in an organization that allows **Project collaboration**. Only writers and owners can change shared project settings. In **Personal Chat Settings**, a field the project has locked shows "Disabled by policy." and can't be changed.

## Send a meeting recap and a decisions Page only your team can open

You turn a recorded meeting into decisions and action items, email the follow-up to the people involved, and publish the decisions as a Page only your team can open.

**Why it works:** **Add to chat** attaches the meeting's transcript to a new draft as a file and sends nothing. The chat reads the transcript for every later step. Send Email finds teammates in your organization's contacts, and a Page published to **People and teams** opens only for the people and teams you add.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform; recording an online meeting's audio needs the desktop app. Organization controls: **Meetings**, **Email notifications**, **Sharing**, and **Share with people and teams**. Emailing teammates needs a managed organization.



1. After you stop a recording in **Meetings and Recordings**, select **Add to chat** → **New chat**. — The transcript is attached to a new draft. Nothing is sent until you send it.

2. Type in that draft and send:

   ```text
   List the decisions and action items from this meeting. Give an owner and a due date only where someone said one. Then draft a short follow-up email.
   ```

   — Bearly reads the transcript and answers.

3. Send the follow-up:

   ```text
   Email the follow-up to Priya Shah and Sam Lee.
   ```

   — Bearly finds them in your organization's contacts and sends it. You're copied and set as the reply-to address.

4. Make the Page:

   ```text
   Turn the decisions into a one-page Page with the date, who attended, each decision, and its reason.
   ```

   — A Page preview appears.

5. In the open Page, select **Share file** → **Prepare share**. When it's ready, the prepared Page opens on its own, marked "Ready to share"; select **Publish** in its header. Set **Audience** to **People and teams** and select **Publish**. Then select **Manage viewers**, add the teammates or the organization that should see it, and select **Copy link**. — Only the people and organizations you added can open the link, after signing in.



**Swap in:**
- **Add to project for Add to chat:** keep every recap in one project with **Always Search Documents** on. See [Build a searchable library of customer calls](#build-a-searchable-library-of-customer-calls).
- **The meeting's summary for your own list:** select **Create summary** on the recording for a quick summary with decisions and action items before step 1.
- **Anyone with the link for People and teams:** for a recap you can share outside the organization.
- **A Prompt Template for the format:** save your recap format as a Prompt Template and run it in step 2. See [Turn a team form into a published Page](#turn-a-team-form-into-a-published-page).

**If it stalls:** If Bearly can't find a teammate, use their full name as it appears in your organization, or their work email. If your organization allows no audience, the publish popover says "Your team policy does not allow a publishing audience."

## Limit a team to zero-retention chat models with locked defaults

As an organization admin, you give one group a policy that allows only chat models labeled ZDR, sets a locked default model and instructions, and turns off public links, then email yourself a plain-language summary of it.

**Why it works:** A policy's model allow-list controls which chat models members can pick, and the model filter can search for ZDR models. Chat defaults set the model and instructions for members' chats outside projects, and locking them stops members from changing them. Assigning the policy to a group applies it to everyone in the group. **Summarize the Policy** describes the policy in plain language before you rely on it.
**You need:** Organization admin in a managed organization. Any plan. Any platform.



1. Open the account menu → **Organizations** → your organization → **Policies**, and select **Add policy**. Enter a **Policy name**.

2. Under **Models** → **Model access**, choose **Allow-list**. Type "ZDR" in **Filter available models**, and add the models you want to **Allowed models**. — Members on this policy can pick only these chat models.

3. Under **Chat defaults**, turn on **Enable chat defaults**. Choose the **Model**, enter **Instructions**, and set both to **Locked**. — Members can't change them in chats outside projects.

4. In the feature list, turn off **Public links**. — Members can still share Pages with named people and teams, if **Share with people and teams** stays on.

5. Select **Save**, which returns you to the policy list. Open the new policy again, select **Summarize the Policy**, read the **AI policy summary**, and select **Email me a copy**. — **Summarize the Policy** appears only on a policy that has been saved.

6. Open **Groups**, choose the group, and under **Policy and settings** set **Policy** to the new policy, then select **Save**. — Everyone in the group gets it, unless they have their own individual policy or belong to a group with a higher **Priority**.

7. Ask a member of the group to open the model picker. — It shows "Managed account · Some models hidden" and lists only the allowed models.



**Swap in:**
- **Deny-list for Allow-list:** block specific models or providers and allow the rest.
- **A spend limit:** set **Monthly spend limit** on the policy; at the limit, members can keep chatting only on Bearly Fast, and only if the policy allows it.
- **Team connectors:** under **Team MCP servers**, add a server that appears for everyone on the policy; each member provides their own authentication values.
- **Everyone for one group:** select **Make Default** so every new member gets this policy.

**If it stalls:**
- An empty allow-list can't be saved: "Add at least one model, provider, or model family to the allow-list, or switch model access off."
- The allow-list covers chat models only; image, video, voice, transcription, and text-to-speech models aren't governed by it.
- Policies apply to members whose email is on your managed domain. If a member is in two groups with the same top **Priority**, their policy can't be resolved; give one group a higher priority.

## Find an old decision and file the chats where they belong

You find what was decided in an earlier chat, then have Bearly rename and move the related chats into the right project.

**Why it works:** **Memories** lets Bearly search past chats with Recall. With **Chat management**, the same chat can read another chat in full, list your chats, and rename or move them, after you approve reading and listing.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization controls: **Memory Recall** and **Chat management**. A chat that isn't end-to-end encrypted, because Recall is off in those.



1. In the sliders menu, set **Memories** to **All Chats** (or **Project Only** to search just this project's chats).

2. Find the decision:

   ```text
   /recall Find what we decided about annual pricing, with the date and the reason. Then rename this chat to "Pricing decision" and move it to the Finance project.
   ```

   — Bearly searches past chats. To read an earlier chat in full it asks first, with a card that names that chat; select **Allow**. Then it renames and moves this chat, which doesn't need an approval.

3. File the rest:

   ```text
   List my other chats about pricing from the last three months and move them into Finance too.
   ```

   — Bearly asks to list "your chats and projects"; select **Allow**. It moves the matching chats and lists what it moved.



**Swap in:**
- **Search for Recall:** press <kbd>⌘</kbd> <kbd>F</kbd> (<kbd>Ctrl</kbd> <kbd>F</kbd> on Windows and Linux) to search chats and files yourself.
- **Archive for move:** ask Bearly to list the chats, then select them in the sidebar with **Select** and **Archive** them.
- **An export for the record:** open the command menu and choose **Download as Markdown** for a copy of the decision chat.

**If it stalls:** Recall doesn't work in end-to-end encrypted chats, and a regular chat can't list, read, rename, or move encrypted ones. Full access never approves listing or reading other chats for you; Bearly asks unless you saved an approval for that.

## Turn a team form into a published Page

A saved Prompt Template turns a few filled-in fields into a full write-up, such as an incident postmortem, which you publish for your team.

**Why it works:** A Prompt Template turns each `{{field}}` in its text into a form field, and the filled form runs as a message in any chat. The **Share** button turns the substance of the chat into a Page you review before publishing, with an audience you choose.
**You need:** Any plan. Any platform. Organization controls: **Sharing** and **Share with people and teams**. Adding viewers needs teammates in an organization.



1. Select **Prompt Templates** in the sidebar, then **Create Prompt Template** (or **New Prompt** if you already have one). Describe the form in the **AI Prompt Builder** and select **Create Prompt**. Under **Template Design**, make the text:

   ```text
   Write an incident postmortem for {{service}}. Impact: {{impact}}. Timeline: {{timeline}}. Root cause: {{root cause}}. Use these sections: Summary, Impact, Timeline, Root cause, What went well, Follow-ups with owners.
   ```

   — Each name in double braces becomes a field in the form.

2. In any chat, type `/`, pick the template under **Skills**, fill in the fields, and select **Submit** (or press <kbd>Shift</kbd> <kbd>Enter</kbd>). — The postmortem appears. The filled form stays in the chat; change a value and **Submit** again for a new version. Blank fields are sent as "N/A".

3. Select **Share** in the chat header → **Prepare share**. When it's ready, the prepared Page opens on its own, marked "Ready to share"; select **Publish** in its header. Set **Audience** to **People and teams** and select **Publish**. Then select **Manage viewers**, add your teammates or your organization, and select **Copy link**. — The Page holds the postmortem itself, not the whole chat.



**Swap in:**
- **Files in a field:** turn on **Allow File Uploads** for a field under **Field Configuration**, so the form can take a log file or screenshots, which Bearly reads with the form.
- **Canvas before publishing:** select **Edit in Canvas** under the answer to edit the write-up before you share it.
- **Email for a Page:** ask Bearly to email the postmortem to the people involved (needs a managed organization).

**If it stalls:** Prompt Templates are saved to your account and sync across your devices, but not to teammates. Share the template text so each person can save their own copy.

## Related

- [Projects](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects.md) for project settings, roles, sharing, and Docs & Data
- [Organizations](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/organizations.md) for members, groups, and policies
- [Meetings](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/meetings.md) for recording and transcripts
- [Pages and Canvas](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/pages-and-canvas.md) for publishing audiences

---
title: "Projects: shared context, files, and settings"
description: "How Bearly projects work, including creating one, sidebar actions, roles and sharing, every Project Settings control, Docs & Data files and desktop folder sync, how project chats inherit settings and memory, password protection, archiving, and tested setups."
canonical: "https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects/"

---

# Projects: shared context, files, and settings

HTML: [https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects/](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/projects/)

> How Bearly projects work, including creating one, sidebar actions, roles and sharing, every Project Settings control, Docs & Data files and desktop folder sync, how project chats inherit settings and memory, password protection, archiving, and tested setups.

A project keeps related chats, files, instructions, and chat defaults in one place. Every chat you start inside it can use the project's files and follows the project's settings, and you can share the whole project with people in your organization.

## Create a project

A project is the right container when you'll come back to the same material across many chats: a client engagement, a research topic, a course, or a product launch.

**You need:** your organization's **Create projects** policy on, if you belong to one. If **Require project passwords** is on, the password switch is already on and can't be turned off.



1. In the sidebar, find **Projects** and select **New project** (the folder-plus icon) — this opens **Create New Project**.
2. Enter a **Project Name** — Bearly suggests an icon from the name, and you can pick another from the icon picker.
3. Optional: turn on **Enable password encryption?**, then enter a **Password** of at least 8 characters and **Confirm Password** — see [Password protection](#password-protection) before you do.
4. Select **Create Project** — Bearly opens the new project's **Settings** page so you can set it up.



You can also start a chat in a project from the new-chat screen. Select the project chip under the composer ("Start this chat in a project"), then search with **Find or create a project...**, pick from **Recent** or **All projects**, or choose **New project**. **No project** starts an ordinary chat. The chip can't be changed once the chat starts; to move the chat later, use **Move to project...**.

## Find your way around a project

Select a project in the sidebar to expand it. You'll see:

- **Settings** — Project Settings, covered [below](#project-settings).
- **Docs & Data** — the project's files.
- **Start chat** — a new chat inside the project.
- The project's chats, pinned chats first. Chats you archived individually don't appear here.

A people icon marks a shared project. The **Projects** header also has **Collapse all projects**.

The project's **…** menu offers **Rename** (owners), **Archive** or **Unarchive**, and **Delete** (owners). An archived project shows **Delete · unarchive first**.

### Move chats into or out of a project

Open a chat's **…** menu and select **Move to project...**. Search with **Search projects...**, or pick from **Pinned**, **Recent**, or **All projects**. **Remove from project** moves the chat back to your main chat list without deleting it. You can't move a chat into an archived project. See [Organize chats](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/organize.md).

## Roles

| Role | What the role picker says | What they can do |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **No Access** | — | Nothing through this grant |
| **Reader** | "Can view chats and files within the project." | View the project and take part in its existing chats |
| **Writer** | "Can view, participate, and create new chats within the project." | Everything a Reader can, plus start chats, add and delete project files, edit shared project settings, and add or remove project Skills when their policy allows adding Skills |
| **Owner** | "Full access including managing project settings, permissions, and deletion." | Everything a Writer can, plus rename, delete, password protection, sharing, and the project icon |

Projects have no public link. Only people you share with can open them.

## Project Settings

Open a project and select **Settings**. The page header shows the project icon, which an owner can change, and an owner badge. Below it are the sections in this order.

### Collaboration

**You need:** to be the project's owner, to belong to at least one organization, and your organization's **Project collaboration** policy on.

The row shows **Private** or **Shared**, with **Share** or **Manage**. The share panel reads "Share with a person, group, or whole organization." and has three parts:

- **People** — search with "Search teammates by name or email" (type at least 2 characters). Only members of your organizations appear, and each organization's **Member directory** setting decides whether its members can be found. **People with access** lists everyone already shared.
- **Groups** — your organization's groups. An inactive group shows **Unavailable**.
- **Organizations** — share with everyone in an organization at once.

Give each row a role: **No Access**, **Reader**, **Writer**, or **Owner**. Select **Done** to close. You can share with individuals only inside your own organizations. Organization admins can see and remove organization and group access from the [Projects tab](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/organizations.md).

### Encryption

Owners manage project passwords here. Others see "Only owners can manage encryption." The status reads **Protected**, **Device Encrypted**, or **Not Protected**, with **Protect**, **Lock**, **Change**, or **Remove** as they apply. See [Password protection](#password-protection).

### Project Chat Settings

This card sets the defaults for chats in the project ("Default settings for chats in this project."). In a shared project it's called **Shared Project Settings** ("Default settings for everyone in this shared project."). Turn the card on with its switch. The badge reads **Enabled** or **Disabled**, and changes save automatically.

| Field | Options | What it does | Lockable |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Model** | Models available to your account | The model project chats use | Yes |
| **Language** | **Default** or a reply language | The language replies are written in | Yes |
| **Response Style** | **Default** or a style such as **Concise** or **Formal** | Tone and length of replies | Yes |
| **Temperature** | **Factual** (0.5), **Default** (1), **Creative** (1.5), or a slider from 0 to 2 | How focused or varied replies are | Yes |
| **Instructions** | Free text ("Custom instructions for this project...") | Added to every chat in the project | Yes |
| **Disable Personalization** | Switch: "Ignore user personalization settings in this project" | Your name, occupation, and About you aren't sent in project chats | No |
| **Always Search Documents** | Switch: "Always search project documents before responding" | Every message searches the project's files before the reply | Yes |

The lock beside a field decides whether people can change it in their own chats. Its tooltip reads "Users can change..." or "Users cannot change...". If your organization's policy controls a field, it shows "Disabled by policy." In a shared project only Writers and Owners can edit this card ("Only project writers and owners can modify shared project settings.").

> **Tip — When to turn on Always Search Documents**
> Turn it on when nearly every question in the project depends on its files, such as a contract set or a research library. Leave it off when most chats are general and only some need the files. Chats can still search the files when you ask. It has no effect in end-to-end encrypted chats, which can't search documents.


### Skills

"Skills available to chats in this project." Writers and Owners can add or remove project Skills when their policy allows adding Skills. Otherwise the section says "Your policy lets you use shared Skills, but not add or remove them." or "Only project writers and owners can change project Skills." See [Connectors and Skills](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/connectors-and-skills.md).

### Personal Chat Settings

In a shared project, each person also gets **Personal Chat Settings**: "Your personal settings that override shared project settings." It has the same fields and switch as the shared card. Your choices apply only to you and only to fields the shared settings leave unlocked.

### Local Directory Sync

**You need:** the [desktop app](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/get-bearly.md), Writer access ("You need writer permissions to create directory syncs."), and file uploads allowed by your organization.



1. Select **Add Directory**, then **Browse** to choose a folder — Bearly copies its files into the project.
2. Decide on **Auto-remove** — when on, a file deleted from the folder is removed from the project too.
3. Set **Max file size** (1 to 1000 MB, default 100 MB) and, if you like, **File types** as comma-separated extensions — only matching files are synced.
4. Select **Create Sync**.



Each sync has **Sync now**, **Configure sync**, and delete ("Delete this sync? Files already synced will remain in the project."). Syncs set up on another device are listed with that device's name and can only be deleted from here.

## Docs & Data

**Docs & Data** holds the project's files. Every chat in the project can use them without attaching them again.

- **Upload** with the button, or drag files onto the page ("Drop files here").
- Each upload shows **Waiting...**, a percentage, then **Complete**. A failed upload can be dismissed. After upload, Bearly analyzes the file so chats can search it.
- **Search files...** filters by name. Switch between grid and list view.
- Select mode lets you pick several files and **Delete** them together.
- Select a file to open **File Details**: rename it, read its **Document Information** (Description, Topics, People, Extracted Text), check **Analysis Engines**, use **Rerun Analysis** or **Get help** if something failed, see its **Directory Sync** source, **Download File**, or **Delete File**.

A file that needs attention shows "Analysis failed or incomplete". Deleting a project file is permanent.

**Limits:** each file can be up to 300 MB, and a project holds up to 2 GB of files on every plan. Writers and Owners add files; Readers see "You need writer access to add files to this Project." Your organization can turn uploads off ("File uploads are disabled by your organization.") or turn off project files ("Project files are disabled by your organization."). See [Documents](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/capabilities/documents.md) for what chats can do with files.

## How project chats inherit settings

A project chat builds its settings in layers. A later layer overrides an earlier one, unless an earlier layer locks the field:

1. Bearly's defaults.
2. Your organization's explicit chat rules and feature controls.
3. The project's shared settings (**Project Chat Settings** or **Shared Project Settings**).
4. Your **Personal Chat Settings** in a shared project.
5. **This Chat** in Settings, or the composer controls for that one chat.

Two layers that apply to ordinary chats are skipped in projects: your **New Chats** defaults and your organization's **Chat defaults**. In a project chat, **Settings** → **New Chats** reads "Default settings are not available in project chats. Use Project Settings to configure." Organization feature controls and locks still apply. See [Chat settings](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/settings.md).

Project **Instructions** are added to every chat in the project, alongside any instructions on the chat itself.

### Memories in a project

In a project chat, the composer's sliders menu offers **Memories**: **Off**, **Project Only**, or **All Chats**. Project chats start on **Project Only**, which recalls context only from other chats in the same project. **All Chats** searches all your chats. Your organization's **Memory Recall** policy can turn Memories off, and end-to-end encrypted chats don't use Memories.

## Password protection

A project password adds a lock to the project's files and settings. While the project is locked, its chats are read-only and **Settings** and **Docs & Data** ask for the password. Use it for material that should need a second step to open on each device.

**You need:** to be the project's owner.



1. Open the project's **Settings** and, under **Encryption**, select **Protect** — "Set a password to encrypt this project. We cannot recover your data if you forget it."
2. Enter a password of at least 8 characters and confirm it, then select **Protect** — the status changes to **Protected**.
3. Select **Lock** when you're done — chats become read-only until the password is entered again.
4. To unlock, open **Settings** or **Docs & Data**, enter the password, and select **Unlock** — turn on **Unlock all rooms that use this password?** to unlock other items that share it.



- **Change** asks for the **Current password** and a new one, then **Change Password**.
- **Remove** warns "Protection will be permanently removed. Anyone with account access can view this project." Select **Remove Protection** to confirm. **Remove** is hidden when your organization requires project passwords.
- **Settings** → **Account** → **Lock all data** relocks every password-protected project on this device.

**Limits:**

- Bearly can't recover a forgotten project password, and there's no reset. Store it somewhere safe.
- You enter the password again on each new device and after you log out.
- A project password is a separate lock on stored project data. It isn't [end-to-end encryption](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/privacy-and-security.md), and messages you send still go to the model as usual.
- **Device Encrypted** means the project is locked with a key kept on one device, as happens when an organization requires device-only encryption. It opens only on that device, and there's no password to change.

## Archive or delete a project

**Archive** hides a project from the sidebar without deleting anything. Archived items are listed under **Archives** at the bottom of the sidebar, where you can open the project's chats or select **Unarchive**.

**Delete** (owners only) asks you to confirm **Delete Project**: "This action cannot be undone. The project and its settings will be permanently deleted." The **Also delete all chats in this project** switch decides what happens to the chats:

- Off: "Chats will be moved back to your main chat list".
- On: "All chats will be permanently deleted".

Unarchive a project before deleting it.

## Setups that work well

### A knowledge base that always cites its files

Every answer in the project checks the project's files first and points to the passage it used.

**Why it works:** **Always Search Documents** makes every message search Docs & Data before the reply, and project **Instructions** apply to every chat. Locking both keeps individual chats from turning them off.
**You need:** Owner or Writer access; files in **Docs & Data** that have finished analysis.



1. Upload your sources in **Docs & Data** and wait until each shows **Complete** — files still being analyzed can't be searched yet.
2. In **Settings**, turn on **Project Chat Settings**, then turn on **Always Search Documents** and lock it — every chat now searches the files first.
3. In **Instructions**, add a citation rule and lock it:

   ```text
   Answer only from the project files. After each claim, name the file and quote the passage you used. If the files don't cover the question, say so instead of guessing.
   ```

4. Select **Start chat** and ask a question the files answer — check that the reply names the file and quotes it.



**Swap in:** use **Local Directory Sync** on the desktop app so new files in a folder join the knowledge base automatically. Set **Response Style** to **Concise** for short answers.
**If it stalls:** a file showing "Analysis failed or incomplete" can't be searched. Open **File Details** and select **Rerun Analysis**. If replies ignore the files, confirm the card reads **Enabled**.

### A shared project where each person picks their own model

A team works from the same files and instructions, but each collaborator uses the model they prefer.

**Why it works:** shared settings apply to everyone, and **Personal Chat Settings** override any field the shared settings leave unlocked. Lock what must stay the same and leave **Model** open.
**You need:** Owner access, an organization with **Project collaboration** on, and teammates in that organization.



1. In **Settings**, turn on **Shared Project Settings**, write the team's **Instructions**, and lock them — everyone works from the same brief.
2. Leave the lock on **Model** open — each person can choose their own.
3. Select **Share**, add teammates or a group, and give them **Writer** — they can start chats and add files.
4. Each collaborator opens **Settings** in the project, turns on **Personal Chat Settings**, and picks a **Model** — their chats use that model, while the instructions stay shared.



**Swap in:** lock **Model** too when results must be comparable across the team. Give **Reader** to people who should only follow along.
**If it stalls:** a teammate who can't change a field is looking at a locked one or a field set by organization policy ("Disabled by policy."). If **Share** is missing, check that you belong to an organization and that **Project collaboration** is on.

### Keep a client's context inside its project

Recall stays within one client's chats, and nothing about you rides along.

**Why it works:** **Memories** on **Project Only** recalls only chats in this project, and **Disable Personalization** stops your profile from being added to project chats.
**You need:** Owner or Writer access to change project settings.



1. In the project's **Settings**, turn on **Project Chat Settings** and turn on **Disable Personalization** — your name and profile aren't added to project chats.
2. Start a chat in the project and open the composer's sliders menu — confirm **Memories** shows **Project Only**.
3. Ask about earlier work:

   ```text
   What did we decide about the launch timeline in earlier chats in this project?
   ```

   — Bearly recalls only from chats in this project.



**Swap in:** set **Memories** to **Off** for a chat that shouldn't draw on anything earlier. Add a project password for a client whose files need a second step to open.
**If it stalls:** if Memories is missing, your organization may have turned off **Memory Recall**.

## Related

- [Run Bearly for an organization](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/organizations.md) — groups, the Projects tab, and project policies
- [Privacy and security controls](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/privacy-and-security.md) — project passwords next to the other privacy controls
- [Chat settings](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/settings.md) and [Organize chats](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/chat/organize.md)
- [Team recipes](https://bearly.ai/docs/manual/combine/team-recipes.md)
